adopts WCAG 2.0 AA by reference, plus the 2.1/2.2 criteria a dense internal tool most often fails.
**Every finding names its criterion**, so it can be cited in a review rather than argued about.

It measures **what the browser actually painted, on a populated board, in every theme, on both
builds**. Both halves of that matter. `colour-check.mjs` holds three *token* pairs above AA — `ink`,
`mut`, `dim` against `panel` — which is necessary and nowhere near sufficient: it cannot see a chip
whose colour is set inline, a value on a lifted surface rather than the panel, or any of the
hundreds of pairs that only exist once requirements are loaded. And an empty board has no rows, no
panels and no chart, so a conformance run against one is a run against something nobody opens.

**Findings are grouped by the PROBLEM, not the instance.** A colour pair, a handler, a control
shape. 115 rows produce thousands of control instances; one finding per instance is a baseline
nobody reads and a diff nobody reviews. The count rides along so the scale stays visible.

### The baseline is debt, gated — never a pass

This board was not built to AA. Asserting it outright would produce a wall nobody acts on, which is
the failure mode the colour baseline and the money-coverage gaps already avoid. So every finding is
recorded in `tools/a11y-baseline.json` with a date, and the run **fails two ways**:

| fails when | because |
|---|---|
| a finding appears that is not recorded | conformance got worse, and nothing else would have said so |
| a recorded finding stops occurring | a list of known holes that has stopped being true is worse than no list |

Regenerate with `--emit` after a fix and say in the commit **which criterion closed**. `--full`
prints every finding rather than the summary. `--board FILE` measures another board (a proof on an
older one).

**2.1.1 Keyboard (since v1.91):** a visible element with a click handler that is not a native control
(or is an `a` with no address) and takes no focus. v1.91 recorded 12 older findings as debt; **v1.92
closed all of them and the baseline is empty** (see the v1.92 section). The run now measures with every
Funding detail panel open and with every toolbar menu, the row ⋯ menu and the Fund tools open in turn, which
is where most of the debt had been hiding; a new click-only element fails it, and `npm run check` holds the
same rule against the markup before anything is drawn.

### Never print a band's hue as text — derive a readable one

A decision band's colour identifies a **lane**. It was never chosen to be read as text, and on the
light themes it cannot be: `#d6a24a` on `--panelA` measured **1.56:1** against WCAG 1.4.3's 4.5:1
floor, `#ff6b81` measured 1.85:1. Section 508 adopts that floor by reference, so it was a
conformance failure rather than a preference.

`readableOn(hue, bg, need)` keeps the hue and walks its lightness toward whichever end the ground
allows until it clears the floor, stopping at the first step that does; `bandInk(c)` applied it
against `--panelA`, which is opaque in all five themes and so can be read straight off the token. (Since v1.90 the
row draws the band as a swatch and its name in ordinary ink; nothing called `bandInk` after that, and v2.03 removed it.
`readableOn` stays: the swatch still uses it.)
**The variant is derived, never hand-picked.** A second colour per band per theme would be five
values to keep in step — and an office that adds its own band, which ⚖ Bands exists to let them do,
would get none of it. The band still reads as itself; it stops being invisible.

Fixed at the same time: the rank `▲`/`▼` arrows were 20×13px against WCAG 2.5.8's 24×24, on both
buttons of all 115 rows — the most repeated finding on the board, and the control you use while
dragging the priority order, so the standard and the job agreed. Rows are 83px and the stack is
50px, so nothing grew. Measure before assuming a bigger control costs height.

### The name: ORBIT, formerly SPMT (v1.81)

Renamed on 22 Sep 2026 because the tool plans more than sustainment: **ORBIT — Out-year
Requirements & Budget Integration Tool**, tagline *the five-year spend plan across every
appropriation*. The rename is the DISPLAY name only — the eyebrow, the panel headings, the export
file names and sheet titles, the pre-flight and reconciliation headings, the status line of a
live push, and the tools' banners. Three things keep the old prefix on purpose and must not be
"tidied": the browser-storage keys `spmtTheme` and `spmtSeen` (renaming them would reset every
reader's theme and re-show every journal entry as unseen) and the package signature `spmtPkg`
(every submittal and appropriation package already issued carries it, and `pmoTake` checks it).
The file names `prioritization-board*.html` keep their names. The three Routines were renamed to
ORBIT and their prompts name `orbit-board`, rules untouched; their ATTACHED repository is still the old
URL, which only the Routine editor on claude.ai can change, and it clones through GitHub's redirect
(tested 22 Sep: a shallow clone of the old URL lands on the current main). The npm package
became `orbit-board` (66da2dc) and on 22 Sep 2026 the repository was renamed to
`bradludington-ui/orbit-board`, by hand in GitHub Settings: the proxy refuses repository-settings
writes from a session. GitHub redirects the old `spmt-prioritization-board` address, so an old clone
or link still works, but a session's repository access is granted by NAME: after a rename the old
name is refused and the new one must be attached (add_repo) before the API or a push will take it.
Older exports on the share still say SPMT in their file names; nothing reads a file by that prefix.

### v1.83: BAE budget names by rule, both lanes in the Spend Plan Source, every requirement on the Two Spend Plans

The first version built from `NEXT-VERSION.md` (items 2 and 3). Every rule below is the owner's,
decided item by item on 23 Sep 2026; the photos he sent were examples, and the program holds NO
list of budget names -- it reads the structure of whatever BAE file is imported.

**The BAE list is one list for the whole board** (`BUDDB`, key `prio27buddb`, saved as `budDb`
beside `execDb`/`aceDb`/`rpDb`/`cycDb`). It used to be `MS.budgets` inside the open appropriation's
store; switching appropriation reloads the board with a different store, so a list imported on
O&M was absent on Full Fund, Spares and OPN and the export fell back to made-up names. A board saved
before v1.83 keeps its list inside one appropriation's store; `budSrc()` reads the newest such list
from `PUBS` (every appropriation the file carried -- NOT `PUB`, which is narrowed to the open one at
boot) and localStorage until the next import writes `BUDDB`. `budReg()` re-parses every stored name
by today's rules, so an old import is judged by the new rule without a re-import (TrngDev names,
which the old import discarded, do need one re-import).

**The rule** (`budParseName`, `budPickList`, `budCandidates`, `budRuleOf`), every name read as
*service + appropriation + funding line + [lane] + PMO*. **Since v1.92 `budResolve()` is the one authority on
a line's budget** -- whether a stored choice can be used, and if not, every reason why; nothing else judges a
name (see the v1.92 section):
1. No PMO in the name = a LEGACY budget: never offered, never filled in; a stored choice pointing at
   one is unusable -- shown as such, never printed, and a pre-flight block (`budbad`).
2. CONG, BOS, OCO, Withhold and Spares TrngDev (`BUD_HAND`) are offered by hand, never automatic.
   Modifications, RDT&E, Flyaway are out (`BUD_EXCLUDE`). TrngDev is part of Spares (`BUD_APPN`).
3. Service and PMO match; the lane matches EXACTLY; where an office's names carry no lane its one
   lane-less name serves both. `USAF O&M NON AFRC NON ANG` is the active Air Force (first in `BUD_SVC`).
4. The funding line: on O&M the hand rule leaves CAM; on Full Fund the COST ELEMENT decides, one to
   one, through `BUD_CE_LINE` (Depot Standup Software -> Depot Software; both other Depot Standup
   cost elements -> Depot Standup, the PMO choosing AV/PP/PSM; Production Engineering Support -> PES;
   JRE -> whichever line names JRE once one exists).
5. Still more than one: the budget CCaR files the record under in the last Spend Plan Source pull
   (`budCcarBuds`, memoised per `EXDB.ts` and year) decides if exactly one candidate is on it.
Nothing is guessed. Measured on every example name the owner sent: 16 of 20 cases resolve on 1-3,
all 56 Full Fund service x PMO x cost element combinations on 4.

**A budget is per service AND lane.** `S.budgetAsg[id]['svc|disc']` / `['svc|mand']`; a choice stored
before v1.83 under the bare service still counts for any lane its own name fits (`budAsgOf`).

**The Spend Plan Source now carries mandatory dollars** (`SRC_BASIS='funded'`): one line per service
per lane, discretionary first, each with its own verbatim BAE name. This REVERSES the pre-v1.83 rule
("this office sends discretionary") at the owner's explicit decision; the registered defect it grew
out of -- the allocation basis, unfunded money exported -- is still guarded (`src-basis-allocation`,
now aimed at `srcLaneOf`), and `src-drops-mandatory` guards the new basis. The trace matrix states
the file as FUNDED, not discretionary drawn. With no BAE list loaded the Budget cell is BLANK (it
used to print a made-up `USAF O&M`, which CCaR always rejects), and `exportSrc` asks before writing
any file that has a blank Budget, naming the requirements; declined, no file is built. The CCaR
reconciliation compares on the same funded basis, so until a file with the mandatory lines reaches
CCaR, requirements carrying mandatory money will show as differences there.

**Two Spend Plans lists every requirement**, in rank order. `planRows` dropped any requirement
neither plan funded, and the TOTAL line then said those "ask for nothing" -- the owner found a $50M
requirement missing that way. Each tab adds FY Requirement and Unfunded (requirement less what that
plan funds, per service, floored) and an UNFUNDED summary line: on a board with no funding-source
overrides Plan A's ties to UNFUNDED vs DISC and Plan B's to TOTAL unfunded. `planStLbl` says NOT
FUNDED -- EITHER PLAN (or NO REQUIREMENT for a zero ask); the panel has a "Not funded by either plan"
filter.

**Cost elements:** Production Engineering Support is #8, before Fixed JPO Support. Saved by name, so
no stored choice moved; only a number typed into the cost element prompt follows the new order.

Tests: audit-other gains *budget names -- the owner's rule* (every line's budget, amounts distinct
so each line is identified, the CCaR tiebreak, legacy and CONG choices, the parser, the pick list,
the warning, Full Fund by cost element) and *the two spend plans -- every requirement listed*;
audit-import proves the list travels in the saved file, not inside an appropriation, and that an old
layout's list is found from another appropriation. 29 new or replaced mutations, 5 re-aimed: 368
in all, 11 equivalent.

### v1.84: the PMO copy does the legwork; one appropriation comes back from a consolidated file

Built from `NEXT-VERSION.md` items 4-7 on the owner's "go" (24 Sep 2026).

**Every field but the allocation travels** (item 6; Brad: offices "edit all available items within
the requirement except for the discretionary and mandatory allocations ... do most of the legwork").
`PMO_FIELDS` gained the plan event (`titles`), PMO (`grp`), band, Mission Critical, prime/OGC,
obligation category, WBS, RCN/EEIC (and by service), Navy SI code, destination, cost element, BAE
budget assignment, award date, CR half-month and minimum useful increment. The band and the star
travel by the owner's decision. What stays: `alloc`, `fsrc`, the CLIN drill, the shortfall absorber,
the ACE mapping and the realignment's UPL marks (keyed by funding pool, not requirement -- the log
listed UPL as sent; it is not a requirement field).
- **A copy says which field set it carries** (`PMODE.pv`, `PMO_PV=2`). A copy published before v1.84
  has a baseline of the first eight fields only; comparing the rest against it would offer every
  band, WBS and award date already on the board as a proposal nobody wrote. `pmoKeysOf()` sends the
  old eight for an old copy. `pmoVal()` reads absent, empty (`{}`, `[]`, `''`) and `false` as one
  value for the same reason.
- **Checked at the door, field by field** (`pmoNorm`, in `pmoTake`): a band this board lacks, an SI
  code it does not know, a WBS vector of the wrong width is LEFT OUT AND NAMED, never shaped to fit.
  WBS vectors are positional and are remapped with the asks when the services arrive reordered.
- **Scope by the office at publication** (`pmoHomeOf`): a copy that hands a requirement to another
  office must send the hand-over; reading the office *now* dropped it as "another office's".
- **The office's own ranking travels** (`PMODE.ord` at publish, `pmoRankBuild`, `pkg.rank`), compared
  on the requirements both lists hold. It is ONE review row, applied whole by `pmoApplyRank()` to
  `S.pmoOrder[office]` only -- the master list never moves (owner: "store it only"). `pmoOrder` is
  outside the journal's captured walk, so the apply adds its own entry; it must NOT call
  `jrnlSync()` first, which would fold every field the same batch already applied into the shadow
  and lose their entries.
- **The allocation is locked in a copy** (`allocLocked()` at the top of every writer a person can
  reach: `setAlloc`, `resetAlloc`, `setFsrc`, `setFsrcSvc`, `setSvc`, `zeroPMO`, `restorePMO`,
  `zeroSpread`, `realignAll`, `convertAllToSplit`, `clinToRow`, and rank-entry's restore). The copy
  also injects a style that dims or hides those controls, found by the writer their handler calls --
  the grid draws its inspector AFTER the shared `render()`, so a pass at the end of render would miss
  it. The old "⇪ Apply PMO Ranks" hint promised a control that never existed; it now says what is true.

**A copy points to Submit input, never Save** (item 4): the header button becomes ✉ Submit input,
the save indicator says the work is kept in this browser, File ▸ Save says it is off, and the opening
line no longer says "Save to pass it on". **A refused appropriation switch puts the box back** (item 5).

**One appropriation back from a consolidated file** (item 7). Importing a consolidated `.html` used to
seed all four slots, so a file sent to the O&M lead came back and replaced Full Fund, Spares and OPN
with what they had been when it left (measured). Now `consTakePick()` asks which to take (numbers or
ALL; nothing by default) and `consTakeApply()` takes each one the way a returned package is taken:
the services guard, a conflict check, a preview, locked baselines and the package ledger kept. The
board-wide stores come across only when the whole file is taken. The shell still takes everything.
A lead working in a consolidated copy can instead **⬆ Send back** one appropriation (`pkgSendBack`):
a return package with no signature, carrying the ledger number the file had.
- **"What did I change since the file left?" is read from the change journal** (`pkgMineSince`): the
  newest entry both journals share marks when the file was saved; every later entry this board has
  and the file does not is a change the take would discard. Not a signature: two copies of one store
  that went through different boots are not byte-identical, and a conflict that fires on every
  import is one people click through. `pkgApply()` uses it for any return without a signature.
- **The preview names every container** (`pkgDiff`): it was written for the order and the money, and
  previewed a package that changed only notes as "nothing changed". Seed flags (`*Seeded`) are skipped.

Tests: audit-import gains four v1.84 blocks -- every field set in a real published copy, carried,
reviewed (conflict included) and applied, with the allocation and the master list proved unmoved and
every allocation door refused; an old copy sends the old eight; malformed fields named; the copy's
header, status line, File menu, appropriation box and office hint; a proposal carrying every field;
and the consolidated take (nothing named, a bad number, O&M only with Full Fund and the stores
untouched, a conflict declined, send back merged and its conflict named). 36 new mutations, 2
re-aimed: 404 in all.

### v1.85: the five-year spend plan by PMO, every appropriation, each read by its own board

Built from `NEXT-VERSION.md` item 8 on the owner's "go" (24 Sep 2026).

**A tab per PMO after the three sheets** (`fydpPmoSheets`). By Requirement, By PMO and By Service do
not change -- a test proves they are byte-for-byte what an O&M-only board prints. Each PMO tab holds
one block per appropriation the office has requirements in, in `APPN_SLUGS` order: the office's CCaR
records (grouped case- and space-insensitively, so " jsf5594 " is JSF5594), the requirements under
each, "No CCaR record" last, then the block total. Requirement and Allocation (the Allocated figure)
per year, FYDP totals at the right. Every line comes from `fydpRows`/`fydpSub`, the functions the By
Requirement and By PMO sheets use, so the tie is structural. The span is the report's span (base + 4,
plus the year on screen when it sits outside the window), the same as the other three sheets. No
total across appropriations. The tab names the source; each block names its own, and says so when an
appropriation's working plan is tagged under a different cycle than this board's.

**The other appropriations are read, never recomputed.** The engine runs on one appropriation per
page (DATA, SVC, BASE_ITEMS are constants of the drill), so a Full Fund figure worked out on the O&M
page would be a second engine. Instead `fydpGather` opens each as its own board: SRC0 in a
`sandbox="allow-scripts"` srcdoc frame (`fydpFrame`), with a shim that runs before any board line and
replaces `localStorage` with an in-memory COPY (`fydpFrameMem`: this browser's keys less the live-view
ones, `prio27appn` set to the target), the file's slot for that appropriation and its packed cycles in
pubState, and `fydpProbeReply()` answering by postMessage once the board has opened. The frame's
origin is opaque, so the browser itself keeps it out of this page, this browser's storage, IndexedDB,
the file, the folder and downloads (proved in Chromium by `tools/fydp-frames.mjs`, in verify:grid).
- **Which copy:** the one that appropriation's board would open -- browser storage, the file's when
  newer (`fydpStoreLocal`), the shared folder's when the team works out of it and it is newer
  (`fydpStoreFolder`; the frame cannot reach the folder, so that copy is handed in).
- **Checked before a figure is used** (`fydpTake`): the appropriation asked for, this BUILD, a clean
  opening (`_bootLost`), the very saved state chosen (its `ts`), and a D whose held years all carry a
  projection. Any failure REFUSES the block and every tab says so ("⚠ FULL FUND IS NOT ON THIS TAB --
  why. Its figures are missing here, not zero."). No lock under the chosen cycle is refused, never
  replaced by the working plan. An appropriation with nothing is said, not hidden.
- **Synchronous when nothing has to be opened**, asynchronous otherwise: `fydpGather` counts answers
  and calls back once (`fydpWhen` takes a value or a promise). `_fydpBusy` turns away a second export
  while frames are open. A PMO copy opens nothing and prints its own appropriation.
- **The harness stands a second board in for the frame** (`boot.mjs` sets `w.fydpFrame`): booted from
  the exact HTML the page built, shim included, answering at once -- so every tool that captures a
  workbook synchronously still does, and `__fydpFrames` records what each frame did. `boot({appn})`
  opens an appropriation by setting storage before the page parses; the older trick of injecting a
  script into the HTML leaks into SRC0 and every frame then opens that appropriation (the check
  caught it: "the frame opened O&M instead").
- **The frame's pub object is built key by key** (`pub.execDb=EXDB` ...) so the short find strings of
  the older `handoff-copy-drops-*` mutations still match once.

**Data-loss fix found on the way: a save kept only the cycle stores this browser held.** `cycByAp()`
read other appropriations' cycles from browser storage alone, so a consolidated file opened on O&M in
a browser that had never opened Full Fund, then saved, lost Full Fund's locked cycles (measured: cycDb
went from om+ff to om). `CYC_FILE` keeps the file's packed stores from boot and `cycStoreOf()` picks
the newer of the two by the boot rule.

**A PMO copy's five-year workbook is stamped on every sheet.** Its file name carried the
`[PMO COPY date]` stamp, but the sheets did not carry the "◨ PMO COPY (snapshot of …, NOT the current
plan)" banner every other copy export has (the guide said they all did). The three sheets and the PMO
tabs now do; the portfolio board's are unchanged.

Tests: audit-import gains six v1.85 blocks on a three-appropriation file built board by board (Full
Fund and O&M lock PB27 then change; Spares locks nothing; OPN empty; a second year with a different
trim): every block total against its own board's By PMO, every requirement line against its own By
Requirement, records and totals footed, em dashes, each requirement once, the first three sheets
unchanged, storage/PUBS/MS/CYCDB byte-identical; the PB27 report tying to each own PB27 report with
Spares refused on every tab; wrong state, wrong appropriation, failed frame, bad shape, other build,
unreadable copy all refused; newest copy wins both ways; the cycle-tag warning; the late path (status,
a second export turned away, the same workbook, the board freed); the cycle-loss fix both ways; a PMO
copy, with every sheet stamped. 25 new mutations: 429 in all.

### How changes ship: queued, then one version on "go" (from 23 Sep 2026)

Brad asked for fewer one-off updates. Every change he asks for is logged in `NEXT-VERSION.md` —
what he sees, what testing showed, what is still needed, and the proposed fix — and NOTHING in the
board files changes until he says **go**. On go: build every open item, run `npm run validate:math`
once, bump `BUILD` once, push once, and move the items to *Shipped* under the version. Logging an
item commits `NEXT-VERSION.md` alone, so the list survives a container reset; that is not a release.
Investigating an item before go (reading code, running a throwaway test outside the tracked tree) is
fine and makes the entry better — changing the board is not.

### The logo: the O is the orbit (v1.82)

Chosen on 22 Sep 2026 from three options rendered side by side (a separate five-bead mark, this one, and
a monoline wordmark). The wordmark is ORBIT in outlines of Liberation Sans Bold — the metric twin of the
Arial Bold the board already falls back to, so no file depends on an installed font — with the O drawn
as an orbit: a ring the weight of the letter stems, a body at its centre, and a satellite on the ring
at two o'clock with a gap masked out of the ring so it reads as a separate body. The O alone is the
mark and the favicon. `assets/logo/` holds the set and `gen.py` regenerates it (fontTools; the header
of the script says which generated file became which committed one).

How it is carried, and why each way:
- **Inline in the header of both builds** (`.brand .logo`, 30 px tall, colour from `--ink`, the
  satellite from `--gold` through `--orbit-accent`). Inline because the board loads nothing from
  anywhere: a `<link>` or `<img>` to an SVG beside the file would break the one-file rule, and a saved
  board is `documentElement.outerHTML`, so an inline SVG travels with it. The mask id is `logoO`;
  nothing else in the document may take it.
- **The favicon is a `data:` URI** `<link rel="icon">` after the `<title>`, colours baked (ring
  `#0f5a91`, satellite `#d4a24e`) because a favicon has no host colour to inherit. The SVG is
  percent-encoded — `&`, `#`, `<`, `>` and the quotes. An `&amp;` left raw inside the attribute is
  decoded by the HTML parser into a bare `&`, which is not XML, and the icon silently fails to load.
- **One colour everywhere else.** `orbit-wordmark.svg` and `orbit-mark.svg` paint with `currentColor`
  and take `var(--orbit-accent, currentColor)` for the satellite, so the same file prints in black,
  sits on the navy theme in white, and shows the theme's accent only where the host page sets it.
- **The lockup is for large uses only.** Its subtitle is about 9% of the wordmark height, so below
  roughly 60 px tall it is illegible; the wordmark or the mark goes alone there. `orbit-lockup.png`
  (navy ink) and `orbit-lockup-white.png` are 1600 px renders for slides that will not take an SVG.

Two things moved with it. The eyebrow lost its "— ORBIT" suffix, since the wordmark beside it now
says so; its hover text names the old name correctly as "Formerly SPMT" — v1.81's blanket rename had
turned that one into "Formerly ORBIT", the one place the rename over-reached. And the header's brand
block became a flex row (wordmark, then eyebrow over title), which kept the header at 47 px; the
toolbar-fit, editors-reachable, panels, menus, colour and standards checks all hold with it.

### A check that greps the serialised world fails on the clock

The v1.80 gate's *suites* stage failed one check in the board suite — *the journal carries
nothing from the view* — while the same suite passed before and after, and the sweeps passed
341/341. The check searched `JSON.stringify(MS.jrnl)` for `'999'`, the value typed on the locked
view, and found it in every entry's **timestamp**: the clock read `1789 99x xxx xxx` from 11:26Z
to 14:13Z on 21 Sep 2026, and the stage ran at 12:35Z. Nothing was wrong with the board. The check
now snapshots the journal's JSON before the view and requires it byte for byte after, and looks
for the typed value only in an entry's before/after fields. Same family as the hand-kept menu list
and the vacuous criterion: **a check must test the thing, not a substring of everything.**

### The cost estimate beside the five-year plan (v1.80)

Asked for from the real board on 21 Sep 2026: the annual cost estimate as a column per year on the
five-year plan, by appropriation, PMO and service, with the estimate chosen the way the cycle is.
`fydpAce` is the picker's choice (the latest set the first time it is asked, or *none*); the
figures are that set's lines for **this appropriation** (`fydpAceLines`), read live from `ACEDB`
— a lock never copies an estimate, so a locked cycle's report reads the estimate as it stands. By
PMO it is the estimate PMO the board PMO draws from (`acePmoFor`, the same alignment the tie-out
uses), and two board PMOs that share one estimate PMO print the figure once and name it on the
other (`fydpAceShare`) rather than splitting it by a rule nobody agreed. By service it is the
line's own service. By requirement it is only what the requirement **confirmed** in the tie-out —
its claimed WBS lines read from that year's own state (`fydpAceReq`), blank when unconfirmed and
blank for a year the plan holds no state for — and never a child line swept in by a prefix; a
subtotal of requirement rows would double a shared line, so subtotals and TOTAL read the
estimate's own PMO and appropriation figures, as the tie-out does. A year the estimate carries no
figure for prints an em dash, never 0; a year the plan does not hold still prints the estimate;
the FYDP estimate adds every window year the estimate carries. The panel gains the picker and a
fourth column per year (`.fydpt.c4` moves the year rule to every fourth column). Guarded by *the
cost estimate beside the plan* (audit-recon: six seeded lines including a child line, another
appropriation's line, an alignment, a shared PMO, a suggested-only mapping and a year the plan
does not hold; the three sheets, the panel, *none*, and a locked cycle) and ten `fydp-ace-*`
mutations.

### The PMO menu never opened, and the menu check never looked (v1.78)

Reported from the real board on 21 Sep 2026: clicking 🏢 PMO did nothing. `toggleMenu('pmo')`
set the flag, but `syncBtns()` painted the `open` class from a hand-kept list of eight menu ids
that predated the PMO menu (added in v1.67), so the ninth never opened — on every board, in both
builds, for three days. Neither browser check noticed, because `panels-render.mjs` and
`menu-paths.mjs` each kept a hand-kept list of their own with the same eight entries. The same
failure as the vacuous 1.4.11 criterion and the stale mutation search string: **a check with a
hand-kept list cannot fail on what the list forgot.** `syncBtns()` now reads the toolbar off the
page (`.menu[id^="m_"]`), the PMO menu is in both check maps, and `panels-render.mjs` asserts that
every menu in the file is in its map, so the next menu added to the toolbar either opens or fails
the check by name.

### 1.4.11 and 2.5.8 closed: every control has a discernible edge and a 24×24 target

The last two open criteria, recorded on 16 Sep as *design decisions to be seen before they ship*,
were closed on 21 Sep 2026 (v1.77) on the standing instruction to keep pressing. The baseline now
records **0 findings** over all nine criteria, on a populated board, every theme, both builds.

**1.4.11 Non-text Contrast** — a new token pair per theme, derived the way `--dim` was: each
theme's `--line` walked along its own lightness, hue and saturation kept, to the first step that
clears 3:1 against *every* surface of that theme (`--edge`: Midnight `#626f8b`, Navy `#657ca6`,
Parchment `#948055`, Blueprint `#6483a0`, Harbor `#627185`; worst pair 3.06–3.10:1) and 4.5:1 for
the hover (`--edge2`). One rule at the end of the stylesheet moves every control that drew its
edge in `--line` or `--line2` to `--edge`: buttons, quick buttons, chips, fields, selects, text
areas, the lane and fit chips, the grid's record-panel fields, date inputs. States keep their own
colours (gold, blue, red, green). Dividers, panels and rows keep `--line`: they are not controls,
and 1.4.11 is about controls.

**2.5.8 Target Size** — every pointer target is now at least 24×24 CSS px, and the layout did
not move for the largest group. An inline text target (the title, the CCaR and PID spans) takes
vertical padding, which an inline box *paints without moving its line*, so the hit area grows and
nothing else does; the record-name line (a block) and the pencil (inline-block) do the same with
negative margins that hand the space back. Chips (`.rchip`, `.lanechip`) grow to 24px tall, small
fields and selects get `min-height:24px`, checkboxes are 24×24, quick buttons at least 24×24.
Measured after: the card row is 87px, was 83 — the chip line grew 4px; the grid row is 77px. Note
that `[onclick]` weighs as a class, so `.tt span[onclick]` outranked `.recname.edit` until the
specific rules named the attribute too — the first pass left the record name at 22px for exactly
that reason.

Proved by `npm run standards` (0 findings, baseline regenerated with `--emit`) and `verify:grid`
(every panel and menu opens, no field clips its value).

### 1.4.3 closed: the dim ink clears 4.5:1 on every surface it is painted on

All thirty *Contrast (Minimum)* findings in the 16 Sep baseline were one token: `--dim`, the
quietest ink, measured on the surfaces it is actually painted on — the sink, a lifted row, the
selected row — rather than on `--panel`, which is the one pair `colour-check.mjs` held. It read
3.82:1 on Blueprint's sink and 4.03:1 on Midnight's selected row against the 4.5:1 floor. Each
theme's `--dim` was walked along its own lightness, hue and saturation kept, to the first step
that clears 4.5:1 on the *worst* surface the standards tool had seen it on, so every other surface
clears by more: Blueprint `#5d7488`→`#536779` (4.60:1 on `#dde5ec`), Parchment `#787058`→`#6e6650`
(4.59:1 on `#ece6d8`), Navy `#8b98ac`→`#919db0` (4.57:1 on `#26344b`), Midnight
`#8a92a4`→`#959dad` (4.62:1 on `#243257`); Harbor's `#46505e` already cleared everywhere (5.03:1)
and is unchanged. The baseline was regenerated with `--emit` and holds the two criteria still
open: *1.4.11 Non-text Contrast* (control borders at 1.2–2.3:1 against a 3:1 floor, which is a
new `--edge` token per theme and a visible change to every button and input) and *2.5.8 Target
Size* (31 controls under 24×24, most of them inline edit spans and 21px inputs in the grid
inspector). Both are design decisions to be seen before they ship, not token arithmetic.

As recorded on 16 Sep 2026, after those two fixes — **95 findings, 5,155 instances**, over four
criteria (1.4.11 is new: it was declared but never emitted, and reported a vacuous pass until it
was implemented):

| criterion | state |
|---|---|
| 1.4.3 Contrast (Minimum) | 32 distinct, 223 instances — was 38 / 304 |
| 1.4.11 Non-text Contrast | 30 distinct, 2,950 instances — low-contrast border tokens |
| 2.5.8 Target Size (Minimum) | 31 distinct, 1,980 instances — was 33 / 2,700 |
| 1.4.10 Reflow | 2 — both builds scroll sideways at 320px |
| 4.1.2 Name, Role, Value | clean — every control has an accessible name |
| 2.4.7 Focus Visible | clean |
| 1.3.1 Info and Relationships | clean — every data table has headers |
| 3.1.1 / 2.4.2 Language, Title | clean |

The clean ones are the expensive ones to retrofit, and they are clean: every control has an
accessible name, focus is visible on every control shape, every data table has real headers. What
remains is colour and geometry, which are local fixes.

> **A criterion that cannot fire reports a vacuous pass.** 1.4.11 was declared in the criteria table
> and never emitted, so the first run printed "Non-text Contrast — no findings" while checking
> nothing. Implementing it surfaced 2,950 instances. This is the same failure as a stale mutation
> search string, wearing different clothes: **the tool that catches checks which cannot fail must
> not contain one.** Keyed by colour pair, not by control — a low-contrast border token is one
> decision applied to hundreds of controls, and keying by handler reported it as 240 findings
> nobody could review.

### v1.86: one destination list, the editors on top, mandatory in the months, what does not draw, full imports, and the guide inside the file

Built from `NEXT-VERSION.md` items 9–14 on the owner's "go" (26 Sep 2026).

**Destinations are one list for the board** (item 11). `DESTDB` is its own store (`prio27destdb`,
`destDb` in the file; a PMO copy's key carries its publish stamp). A requirement names a destination by
KEY, and a key means something only beside the list it was written against, so **every plan still
carries that list** (`MS.flow.ents`, the board's own from v1.86, marked `flow.dv`) wherever it travels --
file, shared folder, package, PMO copy -- and `destMigrate()` reads a plan through it when it opens:
- A v1.86 list: keys are the eight seeded ones or made unique (`destNewKey`), so a key the board holds
  IS that destination, renamed or not; anything else is folded by NAME (case- and space-insensitive).
- A list from before v1.86 numbered its own (x1, x2...): only the name is trusted. What each old key
  became is kept against that exact list (`DESTDB.lmap` by `destSig`, `lsig[ap]`), so every later
  reading -- the same plan reopened unsaved, its locked cycles, a submittal from a copy published before
  the move (`destPkgIn`) -- lands the same way, whatever was renamed or deleted since.
- **Locked cycles are re-pointed ONCE and marked** (`CYCDB.dv`, kept at once): an old key can be another
  destination's new one (O&M's renamed `pw` and its re-added Pratt & Whitney), and a second pass would
  move it again. The test builds exactly that chain and reopens unsaved.
- `flowEntsAZ()` for the panel and every picker (a placeholder reads first in the panel, focused);
  `flowEnts()` keeps insertion order for the funds flow. Delete and reset are refused while another
  appropriation or a locked cycle points at the destination (`flowEntUseMap`, one parse per store);
  deleted keys are tombstoned (`gone`) so a stale list cannot bring them back.
- The migration calls `flowCfg()`, not a bare object: the fuzz caught a plan saved without its funds-flow
  settings reading them back as defaults.

**The RECORD panel's written editors are at the top** (item 12, grid). Under the title: one quiet line
(★ MC · PMO · cost element · ▲ not drawn), then three buttons that say their state -- **⚠ Impact
statement**, **📄 Description & note**, **📋 Requirement edit** (N of M not set). The money, the ⚙ lane-walk
chip, CCaR record + PID + Decision band, By service, Planning and the award schedule follow; "Open on the
row" is gone. Description and note share one pop-up (`FLD_DEF.dn`, parts `FLD_PARTS`), description first,
one Save that writes **only the part whose text changed** (`fldSame`), so the note is never re-stamped or
blanked. Both builds' `toggleDesc`/`toggleNote` open it; the card's in-place boxes are gone. One name
everywhere: pop-up titles, the card's row chip and menu, `PMO_FIELDS`, the proposal gate, the messages
("📋 Requirement edit"). `editors-reachable.mjs` now proves the three buttons are on screen, unclipped, at
1366×768 and 1440×900, with and without an award schedule.

**Mandatory lands in the months, by one rule** (item 10). `landSvc(aw, i, dsv, msv, literal)` is the
single placement, per service, used by `monSpreadOf`, `forecast`, `crBuckets` (CR by service and by PMO,
`crData` is its by-service view) and `spendPlanByMonth`: an increment is discretionary dollars placed as
typed (scaled back only if over-scheduled); undated discretionary is UNSCHEDULED; **mandatory rides the
requirement's own dates** -- its own increments' proportions, or, in a service with none, the requirement's
overall proportions (measured before: a mandatory-only service on a dated requirement went wholly to
UNSCHEDULED). The CR views stopped spreading the whole funded amount by weights (which inflated undated
discretionary into the windows) and By PMO's TOTAL IN WINDOW now counts only the windows. The 1A9A adopts
the rule: on the populated fixture its in-window total moved $7,171,260 → $7,151,178 (−$20,082) and one
more requirement appears. The award block shows *Balance to schedule — discretionary* and a *Mandatory —
rides these dates* row; `toggleAwdInc` seeds discretionary only (it seeded d+m against a d balance). The
◨ switch now moves the CR panel By PMO, the PMO tabs' spend-plan block (marked in its title) and the fit
chart with its table (`svcSummary`); the pot views (Two Spend Plans, Plan A, the fit strip, MC) do not
move -- `audit.mjs` cuts each PMO tab at its spend-plan block to prove both halves. The deck gains **Spend
Plan by Month** (slide 3): shapes, not a chart part (nothing to repair), a column per month plus
UNSCHEDULED, disc with mand stacked, exact dollars as each bar's alt text, a table of every figure
beneath; it ties to `forecast()` to the dollar. **Superseded in v1.93 (item 40):** an award is now both lanes,
discretionary fills the awards forward from the first and mandatory backward from the last, a service with no
award of its own is UNSCHEDULED, and the award block schedules discretionary + mandatory -- see that section.

**What does not draw is found, flagged and fixed** (item 13). `ndData()` (cached per render, against the
FUNDED plan whatever the ◨ switch says) lists every split line where the typed Disc or Mand is more than
drew -- `drewGap` against `basisSvcOf`, the test the "drew" lines make -- with why (a lane ran out above
this rank, or the requirement value stops at $X) and `short`, the shortfall setting that line moves.
**A**: ▲ not drawn N · $X in the fit strip filters the list (`laneFilter='nd'`, composes with the rest;
also 🔍 Filter ▾); 🔧 fix list beside it. **B**: ▲ $… not drawn on the row (both builds) and in the RECORD
panel's line, hover for each line. **C**: `ndBody` lists them in priority order; set to drawn
(`ndSetDrawn` → `setFsrcSvc`) and set all to drawn (confirm names lines, requirements, total and the exact
shortfall it moves). Proved: no funded dollar and neither unfunded reading moves; the shortfall falls by
exactly what each line says; a copy lists them and offers no fix.

**Full imports, one appropriation out and in, the PMO copy under File** (item 14). 📁 File ▾ is grouped
Save · Import · Export · Team. `fullImportText()` (⬅ Import a full board (new version)) replaces what the
board holds with everything the file carries -- all four plans, every appropriation's locked cycles, BAE
list, execution data, cost estimates, reprogramming log, destination list -- after asking per
appropriation about work it would lose (`pkgMineSince`) and one confirmation listing it all; refuses files
before v1.80 (`fullBuildOf`); keeps and reports what the file does not carry; the report survives the
reload (`prio27impRep`, said in the opening-message chain, guarded from the "No saved state" line). Tested
from files WRITTEN BY v1.80, v1.83 and v1.85 themselves (`git show`, the old program saving the fixture's
state): every plan, cycle and store arrives unchanged and every workbook figure equals the file opened
directly. `oneAppnHtml(k)`/`exportOneAppn` write a working board holding one appropriation (its plan,
cycles, execution data and BAE rows; destinations, cost estimates and the log whole; `only` in pubState,
and `APPN` opens on it unless this browser holds work for the stored choice; other packs blanked in
`APPNS`). **Two data-loss paths closed on the way:** `importStores` replaced the whole execution store
with a file's (one appropriation's file wiped the other three) -- it now merges per appropriation; and an
appropriation taken back arrived without the cycles locked on it -- `cycTake` takes them for every
appropriation taken. 📤 Publish PMO copy, ✉ Review submittals (always there; its panel loads submittal
files) and a copy's ✉ Submit input moved from 🎯 Budget year ▾.

**The guide is inside the file** (item 9). 📖 Help in the header (both builds) and 📖 How this copy works in
a copy's bar open `#helpPanel` on a contents list of two parts, *For programme offices* and *For the
portfolio team* (`<template id="helpDoc">`, ~4,000 words, no network, the board's tokens, BUILD as its
version, Esc closes). It never opens by itself and stores nothing. Paragraphs that differ by build carry
`data-b` and only this build's show. Every menu path it names is `.mp[data-m]` and every button `.bt[data-q]`;
`menu-paths.mjs` checks each against the rendered menus and bars of both builds -- the team part on the
board, the office part in a published copy. The web guide and the PDF are made from this text.

Tests: audit-import gains the v1.86 blocks (destinations ×3, spend plan ×4, not drawn ×2, full import and
one appropriation ×4, help ×1); audit-board the combined pop-up and the names; audit.mjs the PMO tabs
under the switch; editors-reachable the fold; the export-one block also proves the exported board prints every figure
the whole board prints for that appropriation (`v14Figures`). `boot({ls})` reopens a board in "the same
browser". 56 new mutations: 485 in all, every one applying exactly once on both builds.

**Checks that changed with it** -- each one a finding, so none of it is re-learned:
- `check.mjs`'s "an external script or stylesheet is linked" reads ONE tag (`[^>]{0,400}`). Its old
  `[^>]+` ran from a `<script` inside v1.85's frame code to a `src=` 9,000 characters later, so
  `npm run check` has failed on a clean board since v1.85. The gate runs `parity`, not `check`, which is
  how it went unseen: run `npm run check` before a commit, as the section above says. It still catches a
  real `<script src>` and `<link href="http…">` (tried both, and one split across lines).
- `audit-recon.mjs` (9 the deck) finds each deck table by a header or a row only it carries (`TB`), not
  by position: the spend plan by month table moved every index up one, and every check read "not found".
  It also checks the five are found and are five different tables. The v1.86 presweep did not depend on
  it (every catch came from audit-import or audit-board).
- `panels-render.mjs` opens `ndPanel` (with a line in it) and `helpPanel`.
- `row-controls.mjs` pairs handlers with single-quoted ids normalised, so the grid's 📄 and 📋 buttons keep
  `toggleDesc('id')`/`toggleRec('id')` -- the card menu's spelling. The impact button keeps `&quot;`, the
  card row chip's spelling, so one mutation covers that route in both builds.
- `money-coverage.mjs` registers `exportOneAppn` as carrying no arithmetic: it copies state.
  `one-appn-trim-shifted` proves the figures check sees a changed allocation. A first attempt that
  shifted `discSvc` in the exported plan SURVIVED: a board rebuilds `discSvc` from its Budget Management
  base when it opens, so that field is a cache and never a source. Aim a money mutation at a source.
- Found by the browser checks and fixed: `.insp-act` edges use `--edge` (they were `--line2`, 1.7–2.3:1 in
  every theme); below 420px the header's `#helpBtn` shows 📖 alone and `.hsp` tightens to 4px (the new button
  made both builds scroll 19px at 320px); the fix list is `.fctab.ndtab`, sized to its content (the shared
  `.fctab` is fixed-layout and ran long names into the Service column -- no check saw that; a screenshot did).
- The presweep found three tests too weak to see their own feature break, each now stronger: the ◨
  check on what does not draw compared a COUNT of lines (a line's drawn figure could change unseen --
  now the whole list); the guide check confirmed this build's paragraphs were there but not that the
  other build's were gone (now every section); and the one-appropriation round trip never ran the
  execution merge, because `importStores` takes execution data only when the file's is NEWER and the
  lead's never was (now the lead imports a newer pull: Full Fund's comes back, O&M's is kept). A test
  of a guarded path has to meet the guard.
- **The first v1.86 gate crawled and was stopped** (26 Sep, 6½ hours in, ~95 of 485 mutations a
  build): four sweep suites at once no longer fitted in 16 GB. audit-import peaked at 5.3 GB (v1.85's:
  4.1 GB) because NO board it opened was ever freed -- every one of ~105 was alive at the end. The
  cause, measured: a board created in a top-level `{ }` block of a module is kept for the life of the
  module; the same code inside a function lets it go. So each block now runs as
  `await (async () => { ... })();` (same text and indentation, so the self-check still hears every
  `const name`), closes its boards at the end (`bootMark`/`bootRelease`, keeping V185W, V186L and V14,
  which later blocks read) and lets the loop turn (`settleBoards`). Peak 5.3 GB → 1.2–1.8 GB. The sweep
  also caps each suite at 2.5 GB of heap (`mutate.mjs` runSuite), so a run that needs more dies loudly
  and is reported CRASHED. Other suites keep the bare-block pattern; each boots few boards.
- **The gate that then ran** (26–27 Sep, 9.7 h) failed on one old mutation, `fld-stale-stays-open`:
  audit-board's stale-editor check opened kind `'note'`, which v1.86 retired, and an unknown kind closes
  the editor by another line. It now takes the kinds from `FLD_DEF` and checks each.
- **Why 9.7 hours, and what cut it** (Brad, 27 Sep: "let's figure out how we can shorten the
  processing time"). Timed on the clean card build: import 150 s, board 36 s, the other four 35 s
  together; a mutation pays for every suite before the one that catches it, so ~2.4 min each, 485 of
  them, twice, on 4 cores. Two changes, neither touching what a catch means:
  1. **Boot sets the program in rather than parsing it** (`boot.mjs`, `splitProgram`). 634 of every
     687 ms of a boot was jsdom's parser reading the 3.1 MB inline program a character at a time.
     The page is parsed with a placeholder and the program set in as one script element; what
     follows it is held out while it runs, so the source it records (`SRC0`) is the parser's to the
     character. `tools/export-audit/boot-same.mjs` proves page, SRC0, saved file (clock held) and
     errors identical both ways, both builds, shipped / seeded / reopened / PMO copy -- and
     `audit:suites` runs it first. Suites: 220 s → 94 s (import 150 → 67, board 36 → 13).
     `BOOT_PARSE=1` forces the old parse.
  2. **The sweep tries the suite that caught each mutation last time first** (`mutate.mjs`,
     `orderFor`; `.math-reports/catchers-<build>.json`, merged, never committed). Every suite is
     still tried until one catches; only the order changes. Measured on four: 198 s → 149 s.
  **The gate that passed v1.86** (27 Sep, 13:05–19:02 UTC): every stage clean, 474/474 caught on
  both builds (11 equivalent), 356.7 min -- the sweeps 332 and 351 min against 562 and 581 the run
  before, with no catch record yet to order by. The next sweep has one.

### v1.87: the math gate, shortened (tests only)

Built from `NEXT-VERSION.md` items 15 and 16 on Brad's "go" (27 Sep 2026). The board changes only its
BUILD; everything else is the test harness.

**Item 16 -- the import suite, 65 s → 38 s, and it was a bug.** Profiled with `--cpu-prof`: 26 of its 65
seconds, and most of its garbage collection, were `keepBoards()` -- added in v1.86 to keep the boards
later blocks reuse -- spreading `Object.values()` over each kept variable. `V186L` is a whole saved
board FILE, a 3.2 MB string, and spreading a string gives one entry per character: 3.2 million, after
every block. It now spreads objects only. Peak memory 1.8 GB → 0.9 GB. The logged plan (share boards
between blocks, with a guard) was not needed, so not built. Proof: `SUITE_CHECKS=<file>` makes
audit-import record every check -- scenario, place, claim, verdict, what it read -- and on both clean
builds all 1,030 came out in the same order with the same verdicts and the same values once clock stamps
and random destination keys are masked (15 values vary run to run for that reason alone).

**Item 15 -- the sweep stops a suite at its first failed check.** `stopAtFirstFailure(failures)` in
`boot.mjs`, called once by each of the six sweep suites on its `failures` list; it acts only when
`MUT_FIRST_FAIL=1`, which only `mutate.mjs` sets for the suites it runs. The first push writes the line
the sweep reads ("N FAILING CHECK(S)") with a synchronous `writeSync(1, …)` -- a `console.log` into a
pipe can be lost to `process.exit` -- and exits 1. Safe because no suite ever removes or reassigns a
recorded failure (checked: every `failures` is only ever pushed to). One difference in kind, accepted: a
mutated board that fails a check and would LATER crash the suite now reads as caught rather than
CRASHED; a caught check is a catch. Proof: twelve mutations, two from each catching suite of the v1.86
gate, each caught by the same suite, 49 s for all twelve (the v1.86 gate averaged ~86 s a mutation);
then the whole gate's catch record against v1.86's, mutation by mutation (below).

With the catch record (v1.86) the sweep now usually costs one suite, run to its first failure.

**The gate that passed v1.87** (27 Sep, 21:47–23:25 UTC): every stage clean, 474/474 caught on both builds
(11 equivalent), **98.1 min** -- sweeps 89 and 94 min (v1.86: 332 and 351; the run before: 562 and 581).
Its catch record matched v1.86's mutation by mutation on both builds: none missing, none new, none
caught by a different suite. (The same suite is partly by construction -- the record orders the
suites; what the comparison proves is that nothing slipped and nothing crashed.)

### v1.88: one board file, and a file from someone else is data -- never code, never a false figure

Built from `NEXT-VERSION.md` items 17 and 21 on Brad's "go" (29 Sep 2026).

**Item 17 -- one build.** See the top of this file. The card build is gone; every tool, script and the
gate run once, on the grid. `parity.mjs` is retired; `row-controls.mjs --check` holds the grid against
`tools/row-controls.json` (a control that vanishes, or appears unrecorded, fails; `--record` for a
deliberate change). The full-import test imports files written by the v1.87 card build and the v1.87
grid, built from the history, beside v1.80, v1.83 and v1.85.

**Item 21 -- the door.** `inboundCheck(txt)` (beside `fullImportText`) reads a WHOLE file before any of it
is used and returns '' or one sentence saying what is wrong and where. `inRefuse(fname, why)` is the one
message ("“x” was not imported: … Nothing on this board was changed."). It is called at every way a file
comes in: `importFullBoard`/`fullImportText` (a full board), `importState` (a board, one appropriation
from a consolidated file, a JSON state, a package, a submittal -- all routed there), `importBoards`
(several at once: ONE refused file refuses the batch, nothing is taken), `bandImport` (a band ladder),
and every read of the shared folder through `driveParse()` (the store, peers, locks, execution data,
cost estimates, the reprogramming log, a restored copy, the five-year plan's folder copy) and the legacy
sidecar state file. `inTooBig(f)` refuses on the file's size before a byte is read. It only READS: a file
it passes goes on exactly as before, which is why the v1.80-v1.87 full-import tests pass unchanged
through it.

#### What a file may carry (the saved format, as the door enforces it)
- At most `IN_MAX_BYTES` (64 MB) of text; nested at most `IN_MAX_DEPTH` (64) deep. A populated board
  measured 3.3 MB and 8 deep.
- No field named `__proto__`, `constructor` or `prototype` anywhere: no board writes one, and copying an
  object that carries one (`Object.assign`) re-parents it.
- Every number finite and within ±`IN_MAX_NUM` (1e15; timestamps are ~1.8e12). Every AMOUNT -- a
  requirement's `ask`/`fy26`, a data pack's `budget`, a plan's `discSvc`/`mandSvc`/`discBA`/`mandBA`/
  `absorbFloor`, `bm.disc`/`bm.mand`/`bm.adds[].vec`, `asks`, `minInc`, `alloc` (a number, or `.v` a
  number or a vector), `fsrc` (`dv`, `mv`, `d`, `mnd`), a submittal's `asks` and proposed `ask` -- is a
  number within ±`IN_MAX_AMT` (1e13), or text the board has always read as one ("1,234,567"). Words, an
  object, a boolean in an amount's place are refused: `money()` and friends would have read them as 0.
- No text longer than `IN_MAX_TEXT` (200,000 characters; the longest measured was 74).
- A requirement id -- in a data pack's `items`, a plan's `order`, `removed` and `custom`, the keys of
  the per-requirement maps (`IN_ID_MAPS`), a submittal's `items`/`base` keys and proposed `nid` -- is text
  of 1-80 characters with no quote mark, `<`, `>`, backquote, backslash, `|` or control character. Ids
  must be TEXT: `id.startsWith` runs on every one, and a number there throws.
- A service name (`services`, `svc`) and an office name (`pmos`, an item's `grp`, a plan's `grp`
  reassignments, a proposed requirement's `grp`) has at most 200 characters and no double quote, `<`,
  `>`, backquote, backslash or control character (an apostrophe and `&` are fine: "O&M", "Brad's").
- A band's `c` is a `#hex` colour; its `id` follows the name rule.
- These shapes are recognised wherever they sit -- a board, a cycle, a snapshot, a package, a submittal.
- **Fields the door does not recognise are LEFT AS THEY ARE**, neither refused nor dropped. The log said
  "dropped"; that was changed while building it, and said so in the ship notes: nothing reads an unknown
  field, so it is inert, and dropping it would lose a NEWER version's data when the file went back to it
  -- the data-loss can't-fail. The three prototype names are the exception, and they are refused.

**Item 21 -- the drawing.** A board saved before v1.88 was never checked, so the board also has to draw
hostile text as text. `esc()` now covers the single quote. `jsa(v)` -- `esc(JSON.stringify(String(v)))`
-- is the ONLY way a value goes into inline handler code: ~190 hand-written `\''+id+'\'`,
`&quot;'+id+'&quot;`, `'${id}'` and `q(id)` sites were converted, and `check.mjs` fails on a value
concatenated into handler code any other way ("handler values"). The hostile fixture found a further ~40
places that wrote a service name, an office name, a requirement id (the row's own `id=` attribute), a
cost-estimate name or a band colour into HTML unescaped; each is escaped, and `readableOn()` turns a
band colour that is not a hex colour into the neutral grey instead of writing it into a `style`.
The log said handlers would read the id from a data attribute instead; the encoder was built instead,
because it closes the same hole for every value in a handler (keys, service and office names, cycle
keys), not just requirement ids, with a one-line change per site and a static check behind it.

**The proof.** `audit-hostile.mjs` (jsdom, the eighth suite, last in the sweep's order): every hostile
shape at every door its file type uses -- refused with the message, and browser storage and the whole
store byte-identical afterwards; every file the board writes (its own saved file, a PMO copy, a
submittal from it, an appropriation package, one appropriation exported, a band ladder) passes; and the hostile board
(`hostile.mjs`) swept screen by screen -- every requirement, row menu, pop-up, record and WBS panel, the
CLIN drill, 41 panels and 9 menus -- with every inline handler read as TOKENS: one that calls `__pw`
outside a string literal is a planted value that became code. `tools/hostile-render.mjs` runs the same
sweep in Chromium (in `verify:grid`), where a planted `<img onerror>` would also fire; on the v1.87 board
it finds 65 places. Both also require each planted field to be SEEN on screen, so a field that is never
drawn cannot pass by not being looked at. The data-loss fuzz gained a trip: every randomly filled board it
saves, and a PMO copy of it, must pass the door. 42 mutations (`door-*`, `esc-quote`, `jsa-*`, `draw-*`).

Two things the first sweep of those mutations taught, both now built in:
- **`IN_KEY_BAD` is a `Set`.** It was an object literal, and `door-proto` and `door-constructor` survived:
  `obj['__proto__']` and `obj['constructor']` answer from `Object.prototype` whether listed or not, so
  the check worked by accident -- and would have refused a genuine key named `toString` or `valueOf`. A
  test now passes a file carrying those keys.
- **jsdom drops markup inside a `<select>`.** `draw-rec-office` (office names written raw into the
  record panel's `<option>`s) survived: Chromium builds the injected `<img>` and `hostile-render.mjs` saw
  it, but jsdom's parser discards tags inside a select and keeps only the cut-off `value="grp'"`. The
  sweep therefore also looks for that stub -- an attribute value ending in a planted quote -- which both
  engines leave behind.

**The gate that passed v1.88** (29 Sep, 20:11–21:03 UTC): every stage clean, **52.4 min**, one sweep of
516 mutations (474 from v1.87 plus the 42 above), 516/516 caught, 11 equivalent; the sweep took 49.0 min
at four suites at a time. (v1.87's gate, two builds, took 98.1.) The run before it failed on one thing
only: two older mutations (`impact-band-gated`, `clin-writes-the-money`) still searched for the
hand-quoted handlers the encoding had rewritten, so they matched nothing -- NOT APPLIED, which fails the
gate by design. Retargeted to the `jsa()` spelling, both caught; the board did not change between the runs.
**When a change rewrites handler text, search `mutate.mjs` for the old spelling before the gate.**

**Not covered by the door:** the workbook imports (`importSrc`, `importAce`, `importBud`,
`importCcarRecs`). They are `.xlsx` files read cell by cell, not board state, and their amounts already go
through `parseAmt`; their text is drawn through the same escaping, and the hostile fixture plants values in
the stores they fill (execution rows, cost estimates, the BAE list).

### v1.89: whole dollars on every add, work that is never overwritten unasked, ids that never repeat

Built from `NEXT-VERSION.md` items 22-26 on Brad's "go" (30 Sep 2026). They came from a technical
review Brad shared; every finding was reproduced on the v1.88 board before it was logged, and every
fix below has a check that was shown failing on v1.88 (or on a planted mistake) first.

**Item 22 -- a requirement added by its total.** `addReq` spread a typed total with `tot/SVC.length`.
The workbook printed 999,999.9999999999 and an unfunded row of $1.000000000000007 until the next
reopen, which rounded each service by itself: $1,000,000 across six came back $1,000,002. It is
`apportion(tot, equal weights)` now (the leftover dollars go to the first services -- Brad), and a
requirement whose values are not whole dollars is refused. Boards saved before keep opening as they
did (each service rounded). `audit-board.mjs` "whole dollars — a requirement added by its total" adds
by total on 1-, 3- and 6-service boards through the form and, new, sweeps **every figure of all nine
exports**: a cell formatted to show whole dollars must hold one. The only fractions any workbook may
carry are in cells formatted to show them (the built-in two-decimal styles 10 and 11, the forecast's
$M, or an export's own format with a point or a %). On v1.88 it found over two hundred such cells from one
add; on a clean board, none.

**Items 24 and 25 -- `REQ_MAPS`, and the two kinds of Remove.** `REQ_MAPS` (beside `yearCopy`) is the
one list of what a year keeps per requirement. `yearCopy` normalises from it, `reqPurge(T, id)` purges
by it (every container, the UPL mark keyed `'r|<id>'`, the what-if levels), and `newReqSeq(T)` counts
by it. The next added requirement's number is past every number the year has ever held -- the added
requirements, every `NEW-` key in any container, the order, the removed list, the office rankings --
and past `T.newSeq`, the highest ever handed out, saved with the board: a purge removes the rest.
It was one past the highest REMAINING, so deleting the newest reused its number, and delete cleared
nine containers: the new requirement inherited twenty fields (band, award dates, destination, PID,
impact statement, WBS, the UPL mark) on screen, through a reopen and on four workbook sheets. Moving an
added requirement to another year now purges it here too (it left everything, allocation and star
included); a locked-cycle compare now reads a deleted one as removed and the new one as added. Boards
saved before v1.89 keep their leftovers, which the new numbering can no longer reach.
Removing a requirement from the board's DATA deleted its allocation, MC star, note, renamed title, CLIN
breakdown and cost element while its confirmation promised "Restore any time". It keeps them now,
and they count nowhere while it is removed: `audit-board.mjs` proves every figure of eight workbooks
identical to a board that never had it. Only an ADDED requirement's delete purges.
The round-trip fuzz gained TRIP 5: everything the populated board holds under any requirement is
copied onto an added one, which is deleted and replaced; a container that held it and is not on
`REQ_MAPS` fails by name (proved on a board with `band` taken off the list). The fuzz and the
year-copy check in `audit-import.mjs` read `REQ_MAPS` out of the board.

**Item 23 -- saving never silently overwrites somebody's work.** Three facts about opening and saving
that the fixes rest on:
- **Which saved copy the work on screen came from** is kept in this browser beside the board
  (`baseGet()`/`baseSet()`, key `<appropriation key>.base`: `file`, `folder` per appropriation and year,
  `dirty`), never in it -- like the save file's handle, it means nothing on another machine, and it must
  not travel in a saved file, a PMO copy or a package (which would also have broken every save → reopen
  comparison). `LASTKNOWN` is its `file`; `_driveBase` its `folder`.
- **Save compares by IDENTITY, not order.** It asks whenever the file (or the folder's copy) is any
  save other than the one the work came from, earlier or later. A "later than" test let a colleague
  whose clock is an hour slow be written over; the test for it is "a clock an hour slow".
- **Unsaved work is a flag this browser keeps** (`dirty`: set by `save()`, cleared by a write to the
  file or folder or by taking a saved copy), never a comparison of two computers' times. A browser
  copy opened before v1.89 recorded nothing, so the journal decides (`ownUnsaved` → `pkgMineSince`).
On opening, the newer copy still opens. If the file is newer and this browser holds work no saved copy
has, that work is KEPT ASIDE first (`keepAside`, key `<appropriation key>.kept`, one copy, removed only
by putting it back or discarding it) and the board says so; the header and 📁 File offer
`keptPutBack()` for as long as it is kept, and putting it back restores its checkpoint, so the next
Save asks before writing over the newer copy. No room to keep it: the file is NOT opened, and the
board says why (`bootTakeFile()` opens it deliberately). If this browser's copy is newer, it opens, and
the board says when the file has been saved by somebody since (`_bootMoved`); "No saved state in this
copy" is no longer said of a file that has one (`bootOwnMsg`).
`saveInPlaceFile` refuses to write over a file it cannot read, or one that is not a board (and forgets
that handle, so the next Save asks which file); an empty file is a new one and is written. A download
(`publishState`, and the fallback after a failed in-place save) no longer clears "unsaved": the header
says "downloaded — not on the shared file yet" (`_dlOnly`).
**The shared folder had the same flaw, found while building this.** Reopened, `driveStorePull` took the
folder's CURRENT copy as the one the work came from, so the next Save wrote over a colleague's save;
the refusal it did give said "Reload to take their copy", and a reload then wrote over them. Now the
folder checkpoint persists, a colleague's save after it is said on opening, and Save stops with a
choice (`driveClashSay`/`driveResolve`): **Load theirs** (this browser's work kept aside) or **Save yours
over theirs** (theirs kept in the history first, forced, or nothing is written). `driveStoreRead`
returns null only for a copy that is not there (`NotFoundError`) and throws otherwise -- it treated a
copy mid-sync, damaged or refused as "none", and a Save wrote over it with no history. A history copy
that fails (`snapKeep` now returns `'kept' | 'recent' | 'none' | 'failed'`) is said. Putting an old copy
back keeps the standing one in the history whatever the ten-minute spacing, and sets the checkpoint to
it -- Save after a restore was refused every time before. **History names never repeat**: two copies
kept in the same second shared a name and the second replaced the first (the new test found it).
`audit-import.mjs` "v1.89 — saving never silently overwrites somebody’s work" drives all of it
against a stand-in file on disk and a stand-in shared folder (an in-memory directory handle with
`getFileHandle`, `getDirectoryHandle`, `values`, `removeEntry`), in two browsers sharing one folder.

**Item 26 -- the journal says what it could not record.** `jrnlWalk` counts past `JRNL_PER_SAVE`
(`out.more`) instead of stopping blind, and `jrnlCapture` writes one line saying how many more
(`p:['(more)']`), and one line when the walk fails (`['(unrecorded)']`; it dropped the whole save's
entries). Neither is undoable (`jrnlUndoable`: any path starting with `(`). PMO Apply's question says
where the journal stops when more than 200 fields are applied, and its result reads the journal back.
A RECORD panel that fails to redraw is emptied and says so.

**Mutations:** 33 new (`add-*` 4; `reqseq-*`, `reqmaps-*`, `delete-*`, `purge-*`, `move-leaves-data`,
`remove-erases-fields` 8; `guard-*`, `boot-no-keep-aside`, `unsaved-flag-*`, `download-*`, `folder-*`,
`snap-name-reused`, `restore-base-old` 14; `jrnl-*`, `apply-*`, `record-panel-stale` 7), all caught in
their pre-sweep; `full-report-lost` retargeted to the opening message's new spelling. 560 in all.

**The gate that passed v1.89** (30 Sep, 01:19–02:25 UTC, its first run): every stage clean, **66.2 min**;
one sweep of 560 mutations (527 from v1.88 plus the 33 above), 549/549 caught, 11 equivalent, the sweep
62.5 min at four suites at a time; fuzz seed 844746176.

### v1.90 (item 27): the layout -- one sticky top, workspace modes, a row for triage

Approved by Brad on 30 Sep 2026 exactly as prototyped, after a Stage 1 review with rendered
before/after screenshots. The problem it solves was measured, not felt: at 1440×1000 the first
requirement sat **1,370px** down the page (1,467 at 1366×768) under the header, the toolbar, the status
line, four scorecards, the fit strip and two chart panels, so no requirement was on screen when the board
opened; rows ran 64-110px because they carried editors; and between ~1401 and ~1745px with the RECORD
panel docked the row's allocation and ⋯ column was clipped on every row.

**The top is one sticky element, `#topStack`**, and every control the old header, toolbar, status line
and search row held is in it **by the same id** (`#appnSel`, `#m_fy`, `#m_pmo`, `#q`, `#m_view`,
`#saveState`, `#saveBtn`, `#helpBtn`, `#m_share`, `#hardBtn`, `#dragNote`, `#rmChipWrap`, `#pmoActs`,
the hidden file inputs):
- `.hdr` -- the context bar: brand and build, appropriation, fiscal year, PMO (its button says whose
  requirements are showing: *All offices* or the office), search (title, event, CCaR **and PID** -- the
  box said PID, so the search reads it), Filter with the active filters as `.fchip`s that take
  themselves off, the save state, Save, Help, File.
- `.tbar` -- the task bar: the five workspace tabs, the chosen mode's tools (`#t_review` … `#t_brief`,
  always in the page, shown by `html[data-mode]`), then `#m_reports`, `#m_data`, `#m_setup`.
- `#posture` -- the funding posture, written by `scoreboard()`.
`syncSticky()` sets `--hdrH` to the stack's height and `--tbarH`/`--scsH` to 0; the grid header and the
docked RECORD panel pin beneath it. **`fitTop()` measures, it does not guess a breakpoint**: a bar that
would run past the window first drops its labels (`tight1`: Appropriation, Fiscal year, PMO, the build,
"Reports & exports" → "Reports"), then the task bar folds the mode's tools into `#m_modetools`
(`tight2`). It runs last in `syncBtns()`, after the filter chips are drawn -- measured before them, two
chips pushed Save off the screen.

**The menus are grouped by task, by the same calls.** Active year, Budget year, FYDP, Tools and Export
became *Reports & exports* (every view by horizon, every export), *Data* (everything that brings data in),
*Setup* (the shape of the board; Appearance with the themes and the densities) and the mode tools. The
items are built once into an object `I` in `syncBtns()` and placed; the Fund tools' spend-plan basis is
its own small menu, `#m_basis`. `tools/layout-fits.mjs` B carries the v1.89 list of calls and fails by
name if one is no longer on a menu or a mode's tools. Messages and the guide that named a path were
re-pointed (`Data › Import CCaR QPE`, `Setup › 🏢 PMOs`, `Review › Classify requirements` …) and
`menu-paths.mjs` knows the new menus and the mode tools (`t_*`).

**Modes and densities are this browser's, never the board's.** `uiMode` (review, rank, fund, compare,
brief), `uiDensity` (compact, comfortable, presentation) and `fundDetailOpen` live in `spmtMode`,
`spmtDensity`, `spmtDetail`; `uiApply()` stamps `data-mode` and `data-density` on `<html>`. Brief always
reads at presentation size. A mode changes which cells a row draws, never a figure (layout-fits G).

**The row is triage.** `render()` draws: rank (the ⠿ handle, the rank box; ▲▼ only in Rank), PMO (its
own order, or *master #N* when one office is showing, in Rank or when filtered), the title with ONE quiet
line under it (event, CCaR, PID, award, prime/OGC, cost element, record name, 📝 note) and its exceptions
pinned at the end where a long line cannot push them out (⚠ impact statement required, ▲ $X not drawn
with the per-service hover `ndRowTip()` shares with the RECORD panel's chip, ★ MC short, the cycle delta
chip), the decision band as a swatch and its name, requirement, cumulative, lane (word and meter), ★ (a
24px toggle with `aria-pressed`, the same handler), ⋯. Fund swaps band/cumulative/lane for
Discretionary, Mandatory, Unfunded and the allocation box (`.acts.alloc`, same input, same `setAlloc`);
Brief draws no control at all. `rowChipsHtml()` is gone with the chip row; the cost element, prime/OGC tag
and record name stopped being row editors (each is edited where it already was). **Drag starts only from
⠿**: `gripDown()` makes the row draggable while the handle is held, `dend` and a document `mouseup`
take it away. Selection is `--sel` (gold at 10% over the panel, one hex per theme -- 13% took Navy's dim
text to 4.25:1) and a 3px gold bar inside the lane edge; keyboard focus is its own 2px ring
(`.row:has(:focus-visible)`).

**The status line lives beside the save state** (`#statWrap`/`#shareStat`, still `role=status`). A message
shows there until the next or until ✕; its full text is the save state's tooltip and a click opens it
whole. (v1.91 replaced the click with the Message button -- see there.) The one ordinary start-up line ("Opened with the saved board state", a PMO copy's greeting) is set
with `_statQuiet` and waits in the tooltip; every warning at start-up still shows.

**Checks:** `tools/layout-fits.mjs` (in `verify:grid` as `npm run layout`): A the posture prints the
scorecards' figures in three states and its unfunded-by-service figures are the Funding by service
table's and add to TOTAL; B no v1.89 menu call lost; C no row control clipped at 1024-1920 in Review, Rank
and Fund; D the first requirement on screen at 1920, 1440, 1366 and 1024 wide; E Brief draws no control
and no RECORD panel; F Fund's columns are the RECORD panel's; G modes, densities and Funding detail change
no data and write nothing into the file; H selection and keyboard focus differ. Each was proved against a
board with the defect planted (8 boards, each failed on its own check). `toolbar-fits.mjs` now holds the
two bars to one line above 1100px and to the window at every width. `audit-board.mjs` "the funding posture
prints the scorecards' figures" is the jsdom half of A, with a fixture where the two unfunded readings
and the MC need/unfunded pair differ (both asserted), and **4 new mutations** (`posture-svc-lane-reading`,
`posture-total-lane-reading`, `posture-left-is-ba`, `posture-mc-need`), all caught in their pre-sweep;
`pmo-button-silent`, `delta-chip-hidden`, `copy-boot-line` and `nd-row-flag-missing` retargeted to the new
spelling, all caught. 564 in all. Changed on purpose, each saying so in the suite: the PMO button's
default text (*All offices*), Save's text (no 💾, it has an icon), the not-drawn flag's hover (the fix list
is under Needs attention), the row-allocation control check (runs in Fund), the File-menu check (the copy
is in no menu but File), and `row-controls.json`, which now records Review plus what Rank and Fund add and
counts ⠿ (`gripDown`) as the drag control.

**Not changed, and noticed:** the RECORD panel's by-service table clips its "lane short …" note in the
UFR column on a narrow panel. It did the same in v1.89; it is not this item's to fix. (Fixed in v1.91,
item 28.)

**The gate that passed v1.90** (30 Sep, 11:07–12:19 UTC, its first run on the commit that shipped): every
stage clean, **72.6 min**; one sweep of 564 mutations (560 from v1.89 plus the 4 above), 553/553 caught,
11 equivalent, the sweep 67.9 min; fuzz seed 568732149. A run on the branch before the PR (09:48–10:59
UTC) had passed the same way in 70.9 min, fuzz seed 793644620.

### v1.91 (items 28–35): the keyboard, 761–1023px, and the BAE budget in the RECORD panel

Built on Brad's "go" of 30 Sep 2026, from the post-v1.90 review and his own request (item 35). Each item
was approved as logged; `NEXT-VERSION.md` says where the build differs.

**28 -- "lane short" on two lines.** `laneShortNote(v)` in `inspectorBody()` draws *lane short* and the
figure on a line of its own at 9.5px, the whole sentence in its tooltip (`.insp-split .drew.ls`). The UFR
column stays 78px, the money boxes keep their widths, and $123,456,789 fits. On v1.90, 115 of 121 notes
were cut (*$150,000* read *$150,00*).

**29 -- the keyboard reaches the message and the RECORD panel's record fields.**
- `#statBtn` beside the save state (*Message*, or *More* / *Less* when a long one is showing;
  `aria-expanded`, `aria-controls="statWrap"`). `statSync()` shows it whenever there is a message;
  `statToggle()` opens it whole; Esc closes it and hands focus back -- only while focus is on the message,
  its button or ✕, since Esc anywhere else belongs to a box or the drawer; ✕ (`#statX`, moved out of `#statWrap`
  so Tab reaches it after the button) hides it and hands focus to the button. The save state is plain
  text; the kept-aside put-back is `button.keptbtn`.
- A hidden or quiet message is visually hidden (`clip-path`), never `display:none`: a `role=status` that is
  `display:none` leaves the accessibility tree and says nothing. `#shareStat`, `#saveState` and `#statWrap`
  keep their ids.
- The CCaR record and PID are `<button class="insp-f">`, the ✎ is `button.tedit`, and the lane-walk "pin
  these figures" chip is `button.rchip`. (`setCcarInline`/`setPidInline` are dead code and were left.)
- At 320px the new button pushed the header 29px sideways; `.hdr .hsp` wraps at ≤640px.
- `npm run standards` has 2.1.1 (see the 508 section). Its drive now shows a long message and opens
  Funding detail before it measures, which v1.90's folded panels had hidden from it, and loads a BAE list
  with one choice so the RECORD panel's budget section is measured with its pickers and buttons.

**30 -- the modes are buttons.** `.tbar .tabs` is `role="group" aria-label="Workspace"`; each mode is a
button with `aria-pressed`, set in `syncBtns()`, and the CSS keys off `aria-pressed`. No tab roles.
`spmtMode` and the fallback to Review are unchanged.

**31 -- 761 to 1023px.** At ≤940px the rows' gap is 6px and Rank, Fund and Brief have narrower
`--rowcols`; at ≤1100px the task bar wraps. **Below 760px the board is for reviewing and inspecting
only**: the money columns and the allocation box are hidden there (as before v1.91), and a phone reads a
requirement; it does not rank or fund.

**32 -- what the row may edit.** The rule above ("The row is triage"), and `row-controls` records surfaces
and opens a CLIN drill. Its fixture also loads a BAE list and one budget choice so the RECORD panel's
budget controls are inventoried: 56 controls recorded, the six new ones all on the RECORD surface, nothing
lost from v1.90. Proved: moving the minimum-increment editor from the RECORD panel to the row, the same
handler count, fails with `LOST record ▸ setMinInc` and `NOT RECORDED row ▸ setMinInc`; a handler-only
comparison passes it.

**33 -- Brief's label:** "Presentation view — requirements can’t be edited here", and the guide to match.

**34 -- one Chromium lookup (tests only).** `tools/chrome.mjs` exports `findChrome()` and `CHROME`:
`CHROME_BIN` (an error, not a fall-through, when it is set and is not a file), `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium`,
the newest `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium-<rev>/chrome-linux/chrome`, else an error naming everything tried.
The 14 browser tools import it (`ia-shots` and `ia-census` still honour their own `CHROME`). `npm run
chrome` proves the order on throwaway folders and is the first step of `verify:grid`.

**35 -- the BAE budget in the RECORD panel.** `budRecordHtml()`, under By service:
- `budLines(id)` -- a line per service per funded lane, and one line (`lane:''`, *not funded yet*) for a
  service with a requirement value and no money.
- A funded line's picker calls `budLineSet()`: a name goes to `setBudAsg(id, svc, name, lane)`, and the
