  empty choice ("— back to the rule: … —") is `budLineClear()`, so a lane-less choice answering the line
  is dealt with too. `budHow()` names how the line is settled -- chosen, automatic by rule, from CCaR, not
  set, legacy -- by `budForRow()`'s own steps (`budRuleOf()` is the rule's part), and the picker's title
  says what the Spend Plan Source prints. The rule's answer is shown in full.
- An unfunded line's picker calls `budUnfSet()`: the name is kept under the lane it carries (`svc|disc`,
  `svc|mand`), or under the bare service when it names none, which `budAsgOf()` reads for either lane.
  `budUnfChoice()` shows whichever is held.
- `#budfam` (*Budget for every line*) calls `setBudFam()`, unfunded lines included; its empty choice,
  "— none: every line back to the rule —" once a family is chosen, is `budClearAll()`.
- *clear* is `budLineClear()`: back to the rule. A bare choice that also answers the other funded lane is
  kept for that lane. *Clear all (n)* is `budClearAll()`: it deletes `S.budgetAsg[id]` whole, choices on
  lines no longer shown included, and says how many.
- With no BAE list the section says so, with a button that opens the import.
- The picker left 📋 Requirement edit (`recFields` has no budget field, so its count no longer counts it);
  `budRecFields`, `budSvcOpen`, `budToggleSvc` and `budFundedSvcs` are gone. The IDS message that sent
  people to Classify requirements points to the RECORD panel.
- **The budget rule did not change.** A name chosen for one lane is never used on the other: money in the
  other lane takes the rule's answer, and the line is *not set* only where the rule has no single answer
  (the Spend Plan Source then names it before it writes the file).

**Checks.**
- `tools/layout-fits.mjs` I: every "lane short" note fits its cell, every requirement on the example board
  plus a planted $123,456,789 shortfall (R019), 1920/1440/1024 × three densities. J: every width 761–1023,
  five modes, Harbor and Parchment -- no sideways scroll, no row control or money figure past or cut in its
  row, nothing in `#topStack` past the window; the drawer's editors on screen at 900 and 768; a 390px smoke
  check.
- `tools/keyboard-reach.mjs` (`npm run keys`, in `verify:grid`): the message (Esc pressed elsewhere
  leaves focus where it is), the three record fields and the pin chip, the kept-aside button and the
  modes, all with keys in Chromium; each section reports its own failure.
- `npm run standards`: 2.1.1 against the regenerated baseline (12 known findings).
- `audit-other.mjs` "v1.91 — the BAE budget in the RECORD panel" drives the rendered pickers and buttons
  (change events, clicks): assign, family, change a line, clear a line (its button, and its picker's
  "back to the rule"), clear all, an unfunded choice then funded in its lane and in the other, one
  lane-less choice on both lanes, CCaR, legacy, save and reopen, no BAE list. After every step it holds EVERY Spend Plan Source line against the budget the panel says
  it prints.
- `audit-import.mjs` "v1.91 — a BAE budget chosen in a copy’s RECORD panel travels": picked on an unfunded
  line in a PMO copy, submitted, applied at the board, used once that lane pays.
- Each new check was run on the v1.90 board and failed there: layout I and J (every mode), keyboard (14
  checks), standards (6 NEW findings), both audit blocks.
- **12 new mutations**, all caught in their pre-sweep: `bud-unfunded-line-missing`,
  `bud-panel-ccar-unseen`, `bud-panel-legacy-chosen`, `bud-unfunded-disc-only`,
  `bud-unfunded-choice-hidden`, `bud-line-clear-leaves-line`, `bud-line-clear-both-lanes`,
  `bud-clearall-funded-only`, `bud-fam-none-keeps`, `bud-fam-skips-unfunded`, `bud-fam-any-lane`,
  `bud-rule-option-ignored`. **576 in all.** Items 28–34 change nothing the jsdom suites compute, so their proofs are the browser
  checks above.

**The gate that passed v1.91** (30 Sep, 15:41–16:54 UTC, its first run on the commit that shipped): every
stage clean, **72.6 min**; one sweep of 576 mutations (564 from v1.90 plus the 12 above), 565/565 caught,
11 equivalent, the sweep 66.3 min; fuzz seed 874911235.

### v1.92 (items 18, 36, 37): the IDS export, one budget authority, the keyboard everywhere, Save diagnostics

Built on Brad's "go" of 30 Sep 2026 and **revised before merge on his decisions of 1 Oct 2026** (PR #3 was held
after review). `NEXT-VERSION.md` lists every decision; this is how the code carries them.

**One authority on a line's budget: `budResolve(id, svc, lane[, St])`.** Pure. It is the only code that decides
whether a stored BAE choice can be used, and everything reads its answer -- `budForRow` (now
`budResolve(...).effective` and nothing else), the RECORD panel (`budRecordHtml`), the Spend Plan Source
(`srcRowsOf`, and `exportSrc`'s refusal), the IDS export, the pre-flight, `ccarDiv`, `budAutoStats`, the
retired-budget count and clear (`budRetiredKeys`). Returns `{storedKey, stored, rule, effective, status,
exportable, bad, reasons}`, status one of chosen | rule | ccar | ambiguous | none | no-list | invalid.
- `budStoredFor(A, svc, lane)` names the stored key that answers a line: `svc|lane`, else a bare `svc` choice
  whose name fits the lane (lane-less names serve both) -- `budAsgOf`'s reading, as a key.
- `budCheckChoice(id, svc, lane, name)` gives EVERY reason a name cannot answer that line: `notbud`, `legacy`
  (no PMO), `missing` (not in the list; matched regardless of case, the list's spelling is printed), `retired`
  (`gone`), `appn`, `office` (the requirement's PMO), `service`, `lane`.
- **An unusable choice is never replaced by the rule.** It stays stored, the panel keeps it selected and says
  "chosen, and cannot be used: <why>", `effective` is '', the Spend Plan Source refuses (`budInvalidLines`)
  and the IDS export lists the line as not uploaded. Picking again or "back to the rule" clears the stored key.
- **A line with no money is resolved as the discretionary lane.** Its dollars are unfunded and unfunded goes on
  a discretionary or lane-less budget (Brad, A1). `budLines` gives such a line for a service with no money and,
  since 1 Oct, an *unfunded part* line (`part:true`) for a mandatory-only service that is still short. Its picker
  offers discretionary and lane-less names (`budPickList(id, svc, 'disc')`); `budUnfSet` refuses a mandatory
  name; `budPutUnf` (shared with `setBudFam`) replaces only what answers the discretionary line, so a
  `svc|mand` choice made while the line was funded in mandatory is KEPT -- shown as "Kept for mandatory money"
  -- and is the mandatory line's again when mandatory money returns.
- `budLineClear(id, svc, lane)` deletes the stored key `budStoredFor` names, never a value worked out from
  the rule; a bare lane-less choice that also answers the other funded lane moves to `svc|other`.
- Funding-line discovery (`budLines`, `budPairs`) never looks at budgets, so an unusable budget never hides a
  line that carries money.

**37 -- the IDS export.** `exportIdsReq(opts)` writes the IDS Batch Import requirements file:
- **Contributions** (`idsSources(T)`): one per requirement x service x lane, from `tiers()` and `unfSvcOf()`
  only. Mandatory: Amount = Approved = `m`. Discretionary (when `d>0` or unfunded `u>0`): Amount = `d+u`,
  Approved = `d`. A service with no money is the discretionary case with `d=0` (Amount = the requirement,
  Approved 0). A fully mandatory-funded service has no discretionary contribution.
- **The pipeline** (`idsBuild(T)`, pure): each contribution gets its budget from `budResolve` and its control
  number from `idsCtlOf` (all digits, no leading zero, at most 15 digits -- it is written as a number cell);
  one that fails either goes to `excluded` with EVERY reason and how to fix each; the rest are grouped by
  `idsKey(FY, budget, control)` (budget upper-cased), summed, sorted by budget then control, and numbered 1..N.
  `multi` (several requirements, or two spellings, on a row), `shared` (a control used by several
  requirements across budgets) and `zero` (Approved 0) are for review.
- **The check** (`idsCheck(P, T)`) works its expectations out again from `S.order`, the asks and `tiers()`:
  row Amount and Approved = their sources'; Amount ≥ Approved ≥ 0; uploaded + not uploaded = every requirement
  (Amount) and every draw (Approved); each contribution identity exactly once and none the board did not make;
  per requirement and service, Amount = the ask and Approved = d + m; whole dollars; unique keys; the printed
  totals are the rows'. Any failure refuses the export.
- **The refusals, in order, inside `exportIdsReq` and before anything is asked, built, downloaded or written:**
  `idsBoardRefusal()` (a locked cycle on screen; a PMO copy), the check, then `idsFormProblems(f)` (reason
  non-blank, storable, at most 32,767 characters; Add or Update; file ID `yyyyMMdd-NNNN` with a real date and
  confirmed unused; a numeric ORG confirmed for every row and service). Each refusal goes through `idsRefuse`
  (alert, status line, the box).
- **The prompt** is `idsForm`, in memory only, reset when the box opens (`idsFormReset`): reason '', action
  'Add', file ID `idsFidCandidate()` (yyyyMMdd-HHmm -- the time of day, never a sequence), ORG from
  `MS.ids.org`. Its inputs write `idsForm` and never save; a redraw keeps them.
- **The workbook** (`idsSheets`): *Requirement v1* exactly as the template lays it out (A1 the file ID; Ref No,
  ORG, FY, Control No, Amount, Approved as numbers; Action, Budget Name and the reason as text), and *Missing
  data and anomalies* (NO UPLOADABLE IDS ROWS first when there is no row; the Update warning on an Update file;
  the file's details; the reconciliation; every excluded contribution with every reason and fix; the
  consolidated rows with each contributor; shared controls; Approved-0 rows). `buildXlsx` now writes an
  integer verbatim (it rounded every number to the cent, which a 15-digit control number does not survive).
- **Nothing is recorded.** The only write is `MS.ids.org`, and only once the file exists. Whatever an older
  board holds under `MS.ids` (v1.91's `seq`, `action`, `cmt`; an unreleased build's `issued`, `sent`) is left
  untouched and never read. `buildSavedHtml` writes `PUBS` as v1.91 did.
- The menu line is fixed text; the preview is worked out when the box opens (and on a redraw while it is open).

**36 -- the keyboard reaches everything a mouse can.**
- Menu items are `<button type="button" class="mi">` (toolbar menus, a row's ⋯, the chip picker), in reading
  order, no ARIA menu roles. The eight Funding detail headers are `button.sc-h` / `button.gs-h` with
  `aria-expanded` (`hdrAria()`).
- **Every other clickable element is a native `<button>`** (revised before merge): `button.nb` -- a reset kept
  at zero specificity with `:where()`, so the element's own class (`.chip`, `.lanechip`, `.fitchip`, `.rchip`,
  `.cedit`, `.rtag`) still dresses it -- with `aria-pressed` / `aria-expanded` where it has a state;
  `button.nb.lnk` for the links in messages; a button INSIDE the cell for the execution and CR expanders, never
  `role="button"` on a `<td>`. The one exception is an SVG funds-flow node (`flowHit`): `<g class="flowhit"
  role="button" tabindex="0" aria-label aria-pressed>`, its name from `flowRows()`, Enter and Space through
  the `document` keydown delegate, and the gold focus ring (`svg g.flowhit:focus-visible`).
- **A redraw keeps the keyboard's place** (`focusMark` / `focusRestore` in the render wrapper; `_navStep`,
  `_byKey`), and a row field left with Tab moves on; after a mouse press nothing is moved.
- **Not built:** dialog focus (a box taking focus, containing Tab, closing on Esc). Deferred by agreement. Built in v1.93
  (item 39).

**18 -- Save diagnostics** (unchanged in the revision but for the IDS count): `DIAG`, `diagClean`,
`diagSecrets` (a walk of every store, `MS.ids` included), `diagVocab`, `diagReport`, `diagSave`; box = file;
Cancel and Esc write nothing; nothing is sent. **Never put a raw value into `DIAG` or the report.**

**CSV.** `csvCell(c)` writes every cell of both CSV exports (Proposed 1–N order, Executive Synopsis): a JS
number as is; a string starting with = + - @ (fullwidth too), or with a tab, CR or LF, or spaces before an
operator, gets a leading apostrophe unless it is a figure ORBIT formatted (`CSV_SAFE_NUM`: -$1,234, +3, 12%).

**Checks.**
- `audit-other.mjs`: "v1.92 — the IDS export: consolidated, reconciled, nothing recorded" (every row written
  from the scenario: A1, consolidation, provenance, reconciliation, B5, B7 with every reason, C1, the xlsx cell
  types read out of the real bytes, determinism, no history, B1/B2/B3, the ORG, every prompt refusal, zero
  rows, three forced check failures, a locked view, a PMO copy, the RECORD panel naming every row's budget);
  "… one budget and control across two services"; "CSV cells a spreadsheet would run are written as text";
  the BAE blocks revised (an unusable choice refuses the Spend Plan Source; every reason; the not-funded line);
  the diagnostics block with IDS-shaped state.
- `audit-import.mjs`: "an older board's IDS settings cross a full import untouched, and only the ORG is used";
  the PMO-copy budget block picks a discretionary name (a mandatory one is not offered).
- `npm run check` "keyboard reach (markup)": a click handler only on a native control or a labelled SVG
  `role="button"` group, and no `role="button"` on anything else.
- `money-coverage.mjs`: the IDS export via `idsSources`, `idsBuild`, `idsCheck`, `idsSheets`, `budResolve`.
- **Mutations**, revised before merge: the eight that guarded PR #3's record of batches were **retired as
  obsolete** -- the code they guarded was removed, so there is no line for them to break; they were not
  declared equivalent. The lane and funding ones, ten BAE ones and `dest-file-no-list` were **retargeted** to the new code, the
  same defect each. New: the IDS pipeline, check, prompt, refusals and sheets; the resolver's reasons, the
  not-funded line and the Spend Plan Source refusal; the pre-flight; `csvCell`; the diagnostics walk over
  `MS.ids`. Every one was presweept with `npm run mutate -- <ids>` and caught.

**The gate that passed the revision** (1 Oct, 14:20–15:54 UTC, on the final tree): every stage clean, **94.7 min**;
one sweep of 662 mutations (604 from the 30 Sep build, less the 8 retired, plus 66 new), 651/651 caught, 11
equivalent, the sweep 87.0 min; fuzz seed 672014855. The run before it (12:40–14:11 UTC, 91.1 min, also clean) was on
the same tree but for a CSS-only change. In the presweep two new mutations first SURVIVED -- `ids-export-records`
and `ids-org-saved-early` -- and showed two weak tests: the no-history check compared the store before and after an
export that followed others which had already written, and every refused prompt typed the ORG already stored. Both
now compare against the exact starting state (`{org:21}`) and a new ORG, and both mutations are caught.

### v1.93 (items 39–44): mandatory awards last, the forecast by view, Needs attention lists, menus, every box

Built on Brad's "go" of 1 Oct 2026. `NEXT-VERSION.md` has every decision; this is how the code carries them.

**40 -- one landing rule, both lanes on the awards.** `landSvc(aw, i, dsv, msv, literal, f)` is still the only
placement, used by `monSpreadOf`, `forecast`, `spendPlanByMonth` and `crBuckets`. It returns
`{at:[{d, j, dv, mv}], ud, um}`:
- an award (an increment) is the whole award, both lanes; awards are taken in DATE order (`awdKey`: month then
  day; ties keep the order typed). **Discretionary fills them forward from the first, mandatory backward from
  the last**, neither past an award's amount (`cap`); what neither covers is `ud` / `um`, UNSCHEDULED;
- a service's weights are its own `sv[i]`; a service with no award of its own is UNSCHEDULED (the plan said it
  would borrow the requirement's awards; the decision that uncovered dollars are UNSCHEDULED replaced that);
- `literal===false` (Two Spend Plans' modelled plan) scales the awards' shape to `dsv+msv` first;
- one date (`aw.d`, not incremental) takes both lanes; no date is UNSCHEDULED in both;
- **`f` is an office's share, applied AFTER the fill.** The fill is not linear in the amount (less money fills
  fewer awards), so `landSvc(..., d*f, m*f)` is wrong for a shared requirement; every caller passes the whole
  lane amounts and `f(i)`. Three mutations (`cr-share-placed-alone`, `oblig-share-placed-alone`,
  `fc-share-placed-alone`) hold that.
- The award block: `awdSplitOf(id, p)` (`{by:{j:{dv,mv}}, ud, um}`, from `landSvc`); `awardBlockHtml` (class
  `awdblk`) shows each award's split (`td.awdsplit`), *Balance to schedule — discretionary + mandatory*
  (`tr.awdbal`), and the undated note (`div.awdundated`); `fillLastAwd` puts d+m less the awards on the latest
  award BY DATE; `toggleAwdInc` seeds d+m. The *Mandatory — rides these dates* row is gone.
- `audit-board.mjs` "v1.93 — mandatory awards last" holds the rule from a stated table (requirements A–H).

**41 -- the forecast by view.** `forecast(fundOf, view)` takes `{pmo}` or `{svc}` (`vf(it)` gives the factor per
service: the office's share from `pmoSharesOf`, or 1 for the chosen service and 0 elsewhere) and skips a
requirement with nothing in the view. `fcViewOf()` reads `fcView` ('' | 'p:<PMO>' | 's:<i>'), set only through
`fcPick(v)` (an inline `fcView=this.value` does not reach a `let` in jsdom); `toggleFc` resets it. `fcViewData()`
is what the box draws AND what `exportFcView()` writes, so the two cannot differ; the export's TOTALs are sums of
the printed (cent-rounded) cells. `fcBody` lists offices with `pmoKeys`, not `GROUPS`. `money-coverage.mjs` registers
`exportFcView` as a money surface via `fcViewData`, `forecast` and `landSvc`.

**42 -- Needs attention lists.** `attLists(T)` returns `{imp, ccar, oblig, awshort}` (oblig: funded first, then
rank; awshort: incremental, funded, `awdSplitOf` `ud+um` ≥ $1). `ATT_KIND`, `attShow(k)`,
`attClose()`, `attBody()` (the `attPanel` box, in the render list), `attGo(k, id)` (imp → `openFld(id,'cons')`,
ccar → `editCcar(id)`, otherwise the first date input in `#inspector .awdblk`). The posture entries are buttons
(`ae(k, g, txt)`), each `min-height:24px` (2.5.8 -- `npm run standards` found them at 13px), in a two-column grid
that is one column below 640px (1.4.10 -- two columns overran 320px). `panels-render.mjs` opens the box with
`attShow('oblig')`.

**43 -- menus.** `fitMenus()` runs at the END of the render wrapper (after `gridAfterRender`, which redraws the
RECORD panel), at the end of `syncBtns`, and on resize and scroll (capture). A menu inside `.gridwrap, #list,
#inspector, .modal-bg, .posture` is made `position:fixed` -- its containing block measured, not assumed, since a
transformed drawer becomes the reference -- below its button when it fits, above when there is more room;
anything taller than its room gets `max-height` and scrolls. `npm run dropdowns` is the check.

**39 -- one controller for every box.** All of it sits just before the render wrapper:
- A box is `body > .modal-bg[id]` with a `.modal`, an `<h3>` and a close control (`h3 button.x`, else a button
  reading Close or Cancel). `npm run check` enforces that shape for every box in the markup; `fldPanel` and
  `uipPanel` draw their close control in code (`DLG_DRAWN_CLOSE`).
- **Open** is `dlgIsOpen(bg)`: the `open` class, or an inline `display` other than none -- five older boxes
  (`jrnlPanel`, `subPanel`, `livePanel`, `budRegPanel`, `idsPanel`) show themselves that way.
- `dlgSync()` reconciles `DLG.stack` with the page: a box newly open gets `role="dialog"`, `aria-modal`, an
  `aria-label` from its heading without the button (`data-dlg-label` marks it as ours), `tabindex=-1`, its opener
  (the last focus outside it, from `DLG.hist`) and a snapshot of its `data-held` fields; every other body child
  is made `inert`; the keyboard goes to `dlgFirst` (first field in view, else first control). A box that closed
  hands the keyboard to its opener found again by `dlgAgain` (the element, else its id, else the same onclick
  and text -- a redraw replaces RECORD-panel and posture buttons), else the menu button it came from, else the
  box below. With nothing opened or closed, a top box whose focused control was redrawn away gets the keyboard
  back (`dlgBack`).
- It runs at the end of the render wrapper, from a MutationObserver per box (class, style, and its subtree), and
  first thing in every key, click and focus handler (`dlgLive()` = sync, then top) -- so a box opened by its own
  code a moment ago is never a step behind. **jsdom's observers are async; call `dlgSync()` in tests.**
- `window` keydown (capture): Esc → `dlgClose(top)`: asks `DLG_ASK` if `dlgDirty`, then `dlgDiscard`, then
  clicks the box's own close control with `DLG.bypass` set. Tab / Shift+Tab wrap inside `dlgTabbables`.
  `focusin` (capture): focus outside the top box is sent back into it.
- **Dirty:** a `data-held` field (the written-field textareas, `idsReason`, `idsFid`, `idsOrg`) differs from its
  snapshot; or any other input has `__dlgPend` -- set on `input`, cleared on `change` (committed). A field with
  `oninput` applies as typed and is never pending. Add `data-held="1"` to any new field a box saves only with its
  own button.
- `mousedown` (capture) on the backdrop, or on a close control while dirty, is `preventDefault`ed: the field is
  not blurred, so nothing half-typed is committed by a change event on the way out, and the `click` that follows
  asks. A box's own Cancel is not a close control for the guard: it discards, as it says.
- The toolbar menus, a row's ⋯, the message and the narrow RECORD drawer keep their own Esc (they are not
  boxes). Help and Save diagnostics lost theirs.
- `npm run dialogs` (`tools/dialogs-keyboard.mjs`) is the browser check; it reads the page, not the controller,
  so it runs on any build (`BOARD=…`): 291 problems on v1.92, none on v1.93. `audit-other.mjs` "v1.93 — every
  box: named, inert behind, Esc closes, typing guarded" holds the rules in jsdom.

**44 -- IDS Approved by lane.** `idsBuild`'s `tot` adds `upAppD`, `upAppM`, `exAppD`, `exAppM` (summed from
contributions by lane, so a lane-less row splits correctly); `idsCheck` works `drawD` / `drawM` out again and
holds both lanes and the printed split; the summary's `span.idslane` and the reconciliation's two *of which*
columns (`table.ids-recon`) print them. The workbook is unchanged.

**Checks.** `npm run dropdowns` and `npm run dialogs` are in `verify:grid`; `npm run check` has the box rule.
Mutations: 54 new or retargeted, all presweept and caught (see `NEXT-VERSION.md` › v1.93 › Mutations); four
retired as obsolete (`fc-mand-only-unscheduled`, `fc-mand-rides-evenly`, `awd-seed-disc-mand`,
`help-esc-ignored`), each with a comment where it stood.

**The gate that passed v1.93** (1 Oct, 20:36–22:15 UTC, on the final tree): every stage clean, **99.3 min**; one
sweep of 704 mutations (v1.92's 662, less 4 retired, plus 46 new), 693/693 caught, 11 equivalent, the sweep 89.4
min; fuzz seed 996759275. Before it, `npm run standards` found the new Needs attention buttons 13px high and 19px of
sideways scroll at 320px (both fixed, baseline still empty), `npm run panels` could not open the new list box (it
had no opener), and `money-coverage` refused the unregistered `exportFcView`.

### v1.94 (items 45, 46): the CR half-month is the award's own date; awards on one date keep a defined order

Built on Brad's "go" of 2 Oct 2026, from the v1.93 review. Both findings were reproduced on v1.93 first.

**45 -- the award's date alone places CR cash.** Brad: "The date and amount reported is the date and amount reported
and should be the sole identifying information for where it resides in the CR view."
- `awdDay(x)`: the award's day, 1 to 31, or **1** for an award with a month and no day (the date its award block
  shows) or anything a file holds that is not a day. `awdKey` uses it, so a month-only award sorts as the 1st.
- `landSvc` placements carry `day` -- `{d, day, j, dv, mv}`, single and incremental alike -- and `crBuckets` takes
  the half from it alone: `half=(a.day>15)? 1 : 0`. That is the CR panel (By Service and By PMO), the workbook's
  CR Cash Needs sheet (`crData`) and the 1A9A (`crExportRows`), all through `crBuckets`.
- **`S.crHalf` decides nothing.** It was one mark per requirement per month, written by every date setter
  (`_crHalfSet`, gone) from whichever award was edited last and by a click on a CR panel cell (`crToggle`, gone):
  two awards in one month shared a half, and a moved or deleted award left its mark. The marks are kept in the file,
  still copied across years (`yearCopy`), still on `REQ_MAPS`, still in `PMO_FIELDS` (an older copy may send one; it
  is applied as before and the review says it is no longer used). Nothing writes one, and the only reader is:
- `crMovedList()`: each award an old SECOND-half mark had in 16-EOM that its own date puts in 1-15, funded plan, CR
  window only. Shown at the top of the CR box (`.crmoved`, with Go to it -- `crGo` reuses `attGo('oblig')`) and in
  Pre-flight's Worth knowing (`crmoved`; Pre-flight stays read-only). `crClearOldMarks()` removes exactly those
  marks, journalled with the save; no figure moves. The other way round -- a late award v1.93 showed early because a
  later edit had removed the mark -- is the defect itself and leaves nothing to point at.
- `toggleAwdInc` keeps the day both ways; `crObl` (the 1A9A OBL DATE) is the earliest award's own date, clamped to
  the month's last day. The one-time obligation-date seed sets the day and no mark.
- Checks: `audit-board.mjs` "v1.94 — CR half-months follow each award’s own date" (requirements A-K through the
  real setters: two dates in a month, the 15th and the 16th, a move, a delete, both lanes, one date, a month-only award with an old mark, a
  dated one with a disagreeing mark, one whose mark agrees; the panel, the workbook sheet and the 1A9A to the dollar;
  OBL DATE; no toggle; Incremental keeps the day; Pre-flight and the CR note list F and G only; clear; save → reopen;
  copy to the next year) and "… by PMO, a requirement shared between offices". The v1.93 block "CR cash needs — the
  half-month placement" now expects its month-only December award (B, with an old 16-EOM mark) in 1-15.

**46 -- awards on one date.** `awdCmp(a, b)` is the one award order: by `awdKey`, equal dates EQUAL, so the stable sort
keeps the stored order. `addAwd` pushes and sorts; `setAwdIncDate` splices the award out, pushes it, and sorts -- a
re-dated award joins the bottom of its new date's group -- so awards on one date are taken in the order they arrived
on it. `landSvc` sorts `awdCmp(a.x, b.x) || (a.j-b.j)`; `fillLastAwd`'s `>=` scan fills the last of the latest date;
`awdDateLbl` and `crObl` use `awdCmp`. `setAwdIncMonth` (no callers) is gone. **`npm run check` "award order"** refuses
a sort of anything named `inc`, or one comparing `awdKey`, that is not `awdCmp`: the comparators it replaced returned 1
for equal dates, which V8 sorts exactly as `awdCmp` does (2,760 random arrays), so no suite can see that defect.
`audit-board.mjs` "v1.94 — awards on one date keep a defined order".

**Mutations:** 24 new (`crgo-box-left-open` beside these; the CR half from the date: `cr-old-mark-decides`, `cr-half-reversed`, `cr-half-boundary`,
`land-day-dropped`, `land-single-day-dropped`, `land-index-dropped`, `awd-monthonly-late`, `awd-date-writes-mark`,
`toggle-drops-day`, `toggle-single-drops-day`, `crobl-*` 3, `crmoved-*` 2, `crclear-clears-all`, `pf-crmoved-dropped`,
`cr-note-dropped`; the order: `awd-tie-reversed`, `awd-cmp-ties-flipped`, `awd-edit-keeps-place`, `awd-add-front`,
`awd-putlast-first-equal`), and six retargeted to the line that now carries the same defect (`fc-mand-not-carried`,
`cr-half-ignored`, `awd-seed-disc-only`, `land-typed-order`, and `awd-dates-counted` / `awd-dates-unsorted` in
`awdDateLbl` -- template-literal finds, which a scan of plain-string finds does not read; the first gate run failed on
them as NOT APPLIED).

**The gate that passed v1.94** (3 Oct, 01:58–04:01 UTC, on the final tree): every stage clean, **122.3 min**; one
sweep of 728 mutations (v1.93's 704 plus 24 new; 6 retargeted), 717/717 caught, 11 equivalent, the sweep 111.4 min;
fuzz seed 870870077. Before it: the first run (2 Oct, 20:26 UTC) failed on the two NOT APPLIED above; two runs were lost
to container restarts; one was stopped at the session's two-hour cap on background jobs, 705 of 728 mutations in. On a
slow container the gate runs past two hours, so a background run is cut off before it ends: ask Brad before running it
outside the cap (this time it ran detached, with a watcher keeping the session awake).

### v1.95 (items 47–60): the pre-launch review — saves, imports, awards, the PMO by year, and a map of the money

Built on Brad's go of 3 Oct 2026 ("Proceed with the focused build after incorporating these points", Codex's scope
review), from Codex's pre-launch review (R01–R12 and the concurrent-writer race; recommended HOLD). Every finding was
reproduced on v1.94 first (`SP/v195r`). Brad's decisions: Q1, one writer per board file, enforced by folder permissions
plus ORBIT; Q2, "PMO's can change under reorganizations, leave the PMO assigned by year, as it's reported as that PMO in
that year"; R09, refuse at the import doors, repair and report on open, never zero; F2 (a), one Spend Plan Source line per
increment. Built in seven parallel workstreams on one base with a slot per workstream in each suite and in `mutate.mjs`
(`// ==== v1.95 Wn: … ====` / `// ==== end Wn ====`), then merged and integrated.

**47 -- a save acknowledges exactly what it wrote, one save at a time.**
- `_saveGen` counts every `save()`. A write captures the generation and the bytes before its first wait, and marks the
  board clean (`DIRTY`, `_baseDirty`, the folder base) only if the generation has not moved: an edit made while a save
  writes stays unsaved. `LASTKNOWN` is the time OF THE BYTES WRITTEN (`fileTsOf({appns:PUBS})`), not the time the write
  ended.
- `saveSerial(fn)`: one save at a time -- a promise chain in the page and the Web Locks lock `'orbit-save'` across tabs
  (where the browser has it). A second Save while one runs says it is queued.
- Each save captures its year (`fy`) when asked. A queued folder save refuses if the year on screen changed while it
  waited, and never writes one year's board into another year's copy. `driveBaseKey/Of/Set`, `driveStoreRead`,
  `driveHeartbeat` and `snapKeep` all take the year; `_driveStorePushRun(quiet, fyAsked)` re-checks it after the read.
- The wrappers (`saveInPlace`, `saveInPlaceFile`, `driveStorePush`, `driveResolve`) refuse a PMO copy or a locked view at
  once (synchronously) and queue everything else; the `_…Run` functions are where every write happens.

**50 -- work kept aside is never replaced without asking.** `keepAside` returns `true | 'occupied' | false`: a slot
already holding different kept work is `'occupied'`, and nothing is loaded over the new work (`keptOccupiedMsg`; at
boot, `_bootHeld.occupied`). Put back swaps: what is on screen is kept aside in its place. `keptSaveFile()` (📁 File ›
⬇ Save work kept aside as a file…) downloads a board file holding the kept work and never removes it.

**58 -- one writer per board file.** `WRITER` (`{by, fp, t}`, `fp = pkgSig('orbit-writer|'+key)`, the key in
`prio27writerKey`) travels in the file and in the folder copy. `writerMay(w, fresh, quiet, what)` → `'me' | 'take' |
'claim' | false`: another writer is refused outright (named), a copy with no writer is claimed once with a question,
an autosave never claims. `takePen()` (📁 File › ✍ Take the pen…) hands over after a confirm naming the writer it
replaces; the old writer's next Save is refused. A read-only file (NotAllowedError, NoModificationAllowedError,
SecurityError) gets a plain message and no download. **The guarantee is the folder permissions** (Codex): where two
people can both write the file, this is cooperative only, and the Help guide says so.

**48 -- a full import is all or nothing (W2).** `lsAll(W)` reads every key first, writes through `lsSet`, and at the
first refusal puts every key back, newest first (a key that held nothing is removed); `{ok, at, why, back, now, held}`.
`fullImportText` prepares the ten writes (both plans, their cycles, BAE, execution, estimates, reprogramming, the
destination list, `prio27impRep`), commits through `lsAll`, and only then touches memory and reloads. Put back:
`fullImportUndone` says nothing was imported. Not put back: it says which lists hold which, reopens nothing, and offers
two files (`fullImportRescue`): the board as it was (`fullImportPreHtml(held)`, built from memory and what storage held
before) and the file imported, byte for byte. `persistAce`/`persistRp` go through `lsSet`.
- **Integration: while that mix stands, nothing writes over the board file.** `_impRescue.mixed` and the storage mark
  `prio27impMixed` (best effort); `impMixedBlock(what, quiet)` in `_saveInPlaceFileRun`, `_driveStorePushRun` (the
  autosave silently) and `_driveResolveRun`; the opening message after a reload; `impMixedClear()` (allow saving again…)
  asks first. ⬇ Save a copy is never refused. Before this, the next 💾 Save wrote the mix over the file -- the one whole
  copy of the board as it was once the page reloads and the two files offered are gone.

**49 -- sharing a cost estimate (W2).** `driveAceFile(dir, name)` tells "no copy" (NotFoundError only) from
"unusable" (a read error, a parse failure, a door refusal, no whole estimate, `aceIntact` failing, a fingerprint that
disagrees). `driveAcePush` writes only where the folder has none; an identical copy writes nothing; a different or
unusable one is reported by name, nothing written, with "replace the folder's … with mine…". `driveAceReplace(k)` asks
by name, re-reads after the confirm, keeps the folder's copy first (`history/spmt-ace-<key>-<stamp>.json`), and never
replaces one it could not read. `driveAcePull` takes nothing unreadable, half-written or off its fingerprint, and names
each.

**One assembly for every board file (integration).** `savedHtmlOf(appns, cycDb)` writes the payload -- every
board-wide list and the writer -- for 💾 Save and the PMO copy (`buildSavedHtml`, with `pubsFor(own)` choosing the
appropriations), the kept-aside file (`keptSaveFile`) and the board as it was (`fullImportPreHtml`). W1's and W2's
copies had diverged already: the rescue file had no writer. A field added to the saved file is added there, once.

**54 -- the journal keeps fiscal years apart (W4).** `jrnlAdd` joins an entry to the last only for the same `fy`, an
unforced last entry and a later time; `(load)` lines are forced; a new year's lines are labelled.

**55 -- the PMO drill, the five-year plan and the comparison, by year (W4, Brad's Q2).** `allocVecFor` is gone.
`spOwnYear(fy)` reads a year through `aceWithYear(fy, cycProjOf)` → `spProjByPmo` -- the board's own allocation, the
year's own PMO, asks and added requirements, the figures a lock takes -- and `blPmoAlloc` groups the same way; the
PRIMES title roll-up (`spTitleGrp` via `spRecOf`) applies to both. Another appropriation is read from its own state
(`spOtherYear`); its Controls print "—" and are never added in as nothing. `fydpRows` and the comparison
(`cycCmpData`: `Y.filed`, `D.rows`/`D.order` keyed requirement + PMO, `D.n` the requirement count) file each year under
the PMO that year (each cycle) assigns: a requirement that moved is listed under each office for its years; totals
unchanged. Trace section 1g registers "Spruills (screen)" and "Spruills · PMO drill".

**56, 57 -- the checker and the Windows font (W5).** `npm run check` "award order" is rewritten with a self-test
(21 unsafe forms refused, 12 valid passed; it fails v1.93 on six sorts and the review's probes). `fitMenus()` clamps
the toolbar menus by measured width; `.insp-act .a1` wraps. `tools/font-standin.mjs` stands in for Segoe UI (+6%), and
`dropdowns-visible` and `editors-reachable` run a stand-in pass.

**59 -- the money map (W6).** `tools/money-map.mjs` + `money-map.json` → `money-map.md`: every surface in
`money-coverage.mjs` and every trace surface is a row; each of seven dimensions (appropriation, fiscal year, service,
requirement, month, CR half, funding lane) is a cell -- **proven** (checks cited by file, scenario and anchor, at the
grain printed, to the dollar), **n/a** (why), or **open** (named). It fails on a missing row or cell, a cited anchor
that is gone, an uncited check, a pending surface that has arrived (as W4's did: confirmed in integration), or a stale
report. **The gate runs it** (`validate:math`, stage "money map", after coverage). At v1.95: 329 cells, 114
proven, 206 n/a, 9 open -- each open cell one of Brad's deferrals by name (Two Spend Plans, the ATR/BTR log, Funds
Flow, the ACE tie-out). Integration added
`audit-board.mjs` "v1.95 — the Spruills by service, by year and for another appropriation" for the Spruills table's
cells (it passes on v1.94 too: coverage, not a fix; three mutations prove it bites).

**51 -- a submittal's awards follow the service names (W3).** `pkgSvcEach(pkg, fn)` walks every service-width
list in a package -- values, WBS lines, every award's dollars, in `items`, `base` and `added` -- and `pmoTake` uses
it to reorder by name (only a list of the right width; nothing is padded or cut), to refuse a package with figures
and no service list (naming each list), and to refuse a baseline of the wrong width. The review and the change
report show each award's date and dollars by service (`awdFmt` in `pmoFmtVal`); an inferred split or "needs
repair" is marked.

**52 -- a held submittal applies only to its own year (W3).** `pmoTake` reads `pkg.fy` (unreadable: refused; none:
asked; another year: the question names both) and keeps `held.forFy`. `pmoApply` applies nothing while any ticked
package is held for another year, and names every office's year; its confirm names the target year.

**53 -- one award check at every door and on every opening (W3).** `awdNorm(v, {door|open, n})`. At a door
(`inShapeWhy` → `inAwdWhy`, `pmoNorm('awards')`, the proposal loop): single or incremental, real months, days
1–31, whole dollars ≥ 0, one per service; text numbers and cents are converted (cents through `apportion`, the
total kept) and said; anything else refuses the file, naming the award. On opening (`applyState`): never refused,
never zeroed -- a wrong width keeps its total spread over the values (`fx:'split'`), an unreadable or negative
figure is nothing (that service UNSCHEDULED, `fx:'repair'`), a bad date or mode is set aside "needs repair";
`aw.fix = {orig, how, n, at, ok}` keeps the record as found. Reported on opening, in Needs attention ("🔧 awards
repaired on opening") and in the award block; "✓ Checked" (`awdFixOk`) clears the mark and keeps the original. The
setters carry `fix` (`awdKeepFix`, `awdDrop`); **integration:** so does accepting an office's award (`pmoApply`), and
the review compares awards without that bookkeeping (`awdBare`), so a repaired award is neither listed for nothing
nor left listed once accepted. `awdNorm` runs inside `applyState` at boot, before `IN_MAX_AMT`, `awdDay`, `ymLbl`
and `awdKey` exist: it reads `AWD_MAX` and `AWD_MN` and does not sort. `svcSaveList` refits awards by position when
the service list is edited (v1.94 zeroed them). `verify-file.mjs` counts a repaired award's set-aside parts as no
loss; the fuzz fills one award with `fix`/`fx`.

**60 -- the export defects the map found (W7; Brad: "F2 option (a)").**
- **F1:** `buildXlsx` stores every figure as it is (15 significant digits), not rounded to two decimals; a fraction in
  a General style gets a two-decimal twin of that style (`nf` is a built-in format id). The $M and $K files look the
  same and add up to the dollar.
- **F2:** an incremental award goes out on the Spend Plan Source a line per increment per service and lane, dated the
  day `landSvc` places it on (a day past the month's end is its last day), carrying its part of the lane; what the
  awards do not cover goes out on 31 August, as undated money always has. **Integration:** a single award date past
  its month's end is clamped the same way (it rolled into the next month).
- **F3:** the Executive Synopsis's first line and file name are `FYL()`.
- **F4:** an office's share is `pmoPartsOf(it, p)` / `pmoSplit` -- per service the ask apportioned, then
  discretionary, mandatory and unfunded each spread by what each office still has to take -- in the forecast and its
  views, the CR windows by PMO, `monSpreadOf` (an office part as `f`), the PMO tabs, the PMO Matrix, CE by PMO and
  the deck. Office totals add up both ways; an office's month can sit about a dollar from its exact share, the total
  exact. A part that is not whole dollars (a modelled plan) stays a fraction.
- **F5:** the CR Cash Needs footnote says each award's dollars sit in its own date's half.
- **F6 (integration, from W7's notes):** `clinSplit` apportions a whole-dollar amount, so a prime's CLIN lines on the
  Spend Plan Source and the RCN/EEIC monthly tab are whole dollars that add up (they were amount × share:
  $33.333…).

**Board imports that reorder the services (integration, from W3's notes).** `svcRemapState` now also reorders the
WBS lines (`S.wbs`, `S.clinWbs`; a line that takes its parent whole stores `[]` and keeps it) and the CLIN grid's
own figures (`S.clinAlloc`, keyed or the old array form; `clinMeta`/`clinNew` asks). They were left in the file's
order, on the wrong services. The "exhaustive remap" fixture now holds all five.

**Mutations.** New in v1.95: W1 21, W2 22, W3 28, W4 18, W7 19, integration 16 (`spgather-*` 3, `impmixed-*` 6, `clin-split-*`
2, `src-single-day-rolls`, `pmo-apply-drops-original`, `pmo-rows-compare-bookkeeping`, `remap-*` 2); W5 none in jsdom
(its browser mutants were run by hand; its slot says why), W6 none. 728 + 124 = 852. Retargeted in place: v1.89's
`boot-no-keep-aside`; W2's five `full-*-left`; W7's eleven share mutations (`mc-matrix-weight`, `pmo-matrix-disc`,
`ce-by-pmo-share`, `ppt-pmo-share`, `oblig-*` 2, `cr-back-to-weights`, `fcv-*` 2, `cr-/fc-share-placed-alone`);
`pmo-conflict-blind`; `excel-unf-vec` (after the first gate run, above); and **eight that W1's save changes had left matching nothing**
(`handoff-copy-drops-store/-exec/-ace`, `save-no-lt-escape`, `view-save-not-refused`, `cycle-file-drops-store`,
`bud-not-saved`, `guard-fails-open`) plus `dest-file-no-list` -- found before the gate by counting every target against
the merged board with a script that reads the array as text (never importing `mutate.mjs`). A workstream's presweep
of its own new ids does not see an old target it broke: count them all after every merge (AGENTS.md).

**Two `audit.mjs` checks** asserted the old fractional office share ($207.50 an increment); they now require a whole
dollar within $1 of the exact share, and their companion still asserts the exact sum (W7). Two `audit-import.mjs`
checks expected a listless package to be asked about; it is refused (W3).

**Found by an independent review before merge, fixed in v1.95** (Brad, 3 Oct: "Fix all four in v1.95"). A fresh agent reviewed the whole change for data loss and money; it found no wrong money (3,000 random office splits and 400 random incremental Spend Plan Source cases all added up) and four older doors round the new rules, each proved by a probe:
- **⬆ Publish now** (`livePublish`, drive mode) wrote the folder's copy through its own `drivePublish` -- no writer check, no clash check, no history, no `saveSerial`, no `impMixedBlock`, the writer record dropped. In drive mode it is now `driveStorePush(false)`; a watcher (store off) is refused with the reason and not offered the button; `drivePublish` is gone.
- **The ordinary ⬅ Import** (`consTakeApply`, every board-file import on a populated board) wrote each appropriation with `lsSet` and ignored the result. Now `_lsCapture` makes `lsSet` RECORD its writes -- the import's own, `cycTake`'s and `importStores`' -- and the import commits them through `lsAll`; a refusal restores memory (`impMemSnap`/`impMemRestore`, `CYC_FILE` included: `cycStoreOf` can hand back the file's own object) and goes to `fullImportUndone`.
- **↶ Put this back** (`driveRestore`) replaced unsaved work without `keepAside`; it now keeps it aside first, as `driveStorePull` does, and puts nothing back on `'occupied'` or no room.
- **The stopped-import hold** was never lifted by an import that later landed. The mark now names the keys left mixed (`keys`, added to any still marked); `impMixedLift(keys)` removes it once every one has been written again, all or nothing (a mark naming none is lifted by any import that lands).
Checks: `audit-import.mjs` "v1.95 — ⬆ Publish now in the shared folder is the folder save…", "…the ordinary ⬅ Import of a board file is all or nothing", "…↶ Put this back keeps unsaved work aside first", "…an import that lands whole lifts the hold…" (11 of their 16 checks fail on 2f19c9f; the rest are controls, or pass there because the old code never lifted the hold at all). Mutations: 8 new (`publish-unchecked`, `publish-watcher-silent`, `publish-offered-to-watchers`, `constake-writes-directly`, `constake-memory-kept`, `restore-no-keep-aside`, `impmixed-never-lifted`, `impmixed-lift-partial`) and 2 retargeted (`one-cycles-not-taken`, `impmixed-not-kept`).

**The gate that passed v1.95** (4 Oct, 00:06–02:24 UTC, on the final tree, review fixes included): every stage clean, **137.9 min**, the sweep 127.7 min; 860 mutations, 849/849 caught, 11 equivalent; fuzz seed 819798525; the new "money map" stage: 329 cells, 114 proven, 206 n/a, 9 open. Before it, two runs. The first (3 Oct, 13:33–16:49 UTC) failed on one survivor, `excel-unf-vec`: F4 had moved the PMO tabs to `pmoPartsOf`, so `PICK.unf`'s per-service vector -- the line it mutates -- was read by nothing. A consumer moving away from a line turns its mutation EQUIVALENT without a word; only the sweep sees it. The dead vector is gone and the mutation is aimed at `pmoPartsOf`'s split. The second (16:54–19:19 UTC, 145.1 min, 841/841) passed on 2f19c9f; the review above then found the four doors, and the third run is on the tree that closes them. The first run's sweep took 184.7 min: new mutations have no catcher history, so each tries the suites in order; with the history, 134.3 and then 127.7.

### v1.96 (items 64–70): the menus fold by the job; the 4 Oct review of v1.95 (NR01–NR05)

Built on Brad's go of 4 Oct 2026 for items 64 and 65; items 66–70 were added the same day from an independent review of
v1.95 (main 64f0319) that Brad pasted mid-build -- five ways work could still be lost, each reproduced by the review's own
probes (its evidence package stayed on Brad's machine; the regressions re-create each probe from the report).

**65 -- long menus fold.** `grp(m, k, title, items, now)` in `syncBtns` draws a heading button (`.gh`: `data-g`,
`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls="mg_<m>_<k>"`, a count `.gn`, and `.now` -- what is chosen, for a choice) and a `.gbody` that
is `hidden` until open. The items stay in the page, so `[onclick]` greps, menu-path checks, the census and the tests still
find them; `.mdd [hidden]{display:none!important}` because `.mi` sets display. `menuGrpOpen[m]` holds the one open group per
menu, in `localStorage.spmtMenuGroups` (never in the file); `menuGrp` toggles it, redraws, and puts focus back on the heading;
the heading's onclick stops propagation -- the redraw detaches it, and the page's click handler would read that as a click
outside. `menuGrpAuto` (from `toggleMenu('view')`) opens the Filter group that holds a filter that is on. Find:
`#mFind` (`menuFind`/`menuFindApply`) filters in place by item text or group title (so the box keeps focus), emptied when
Reports & exports closes (`if(openMenu!=='reports') menuFindQ=''`). `syncBtns` now ends with `fitMenus()`: the fit at its top
measured the menu before it was drawn, and a group opening made it taller and wider (761×960, Segoe stand-in). Menus of ten
or fewer (the year, Data) stay flat; the office picker never folds. Paths name their group (`Reports & exports ▾ › Spend plan
& cash › …`); `menu-paths.mjs` reads each menu's shape (`READ_SHAPE`) and fails a path to a folded item that names none.
Moved: Set base year and Reset (were also in Setup), Funding realignment → the year menu; WBS from execution data → Data.

**64 -- the browser checks reach the repaired-award controls.** `buildPopulated` seeds a SPLIT INFERRED (two figures on a
six-service board) and a NEEDS REPAIR award (date `2027-13`). Keyboard L uses the 🔧 entry, its list, Go to it, ✓ Checked;
the standards run measures the list and the award block and records a `coverage` finding if either is absent. It found the
`.modal .x` close button failing 1.4.11 and 2.5.8 on all 49 boxes -- now `--edge`, 24px minimum.

**66 (NR01) -- ↶ Put this back.** `driveRestore` pins `mySlug()`/`MS.cur`, reads and keeps the folder's current copy, then
-- after the last await, synchronously -- refuses if the board moved, decides `ownUnsaved`, `keepAside`s, and adopts. The
keep used to be decided before the awaits, so an edit typed during the history write was replaced unasked.

**67 (NR02) -- ⇊ Import several boards at once** commits through `lsAll` (`W`: `[key, text, what, say]`) before memory or
`PUBS` change; a refusal goes to `fullImportUndone` with the refused file's text for the rescue; `impMixedLift` on success.

**68 (NR03) -- `driveRpPush`** reads with only `NotFoundError` as "no log"; unreadable, refused or not whole (`entries` not a
list) stops the share; a readable log is kept with `snapKeep(dir,'rp','log',txt,true)` before the merged one replaces it.

**69 (NR05) -- the legacy shared state file.** The `loadShared` callback is `sharedArrived(st)`: `DIRTY` -- nothing replaced,
a link to `adoptShared()`; otherwise `sharedKeepMine(st)` keeps work no saved copy has aside first ('occupied' or no room:
nothing loaded). `adoptShared` keeps aside first too. Timestamps no longer decide whether local work is disposable.

**70 (NR04) -- a repaired award's original** is kept whole: `awdOrigKeep` (pieces of `AWD_ORIG_MAX`=100,000 characters, under
the door's `IN_MAX_TEXT`=200,000), read by `awdOrigText`; the door (`awdNorm` door) accepts a string or a list of strings.

**Checks.** `audit-board.mjs` "v1.96 — a long menu folds by the job…" (8 fail on v1.95, then it throws). `audit-import.mjs`
"v1.96 — …" NR01–NR05 (17 fail on v1.95 -- NR05's with only its callback named; 5 CONTROLs), and the v1.86 File test now
asserts the new groups (2 more). The W1H folder mock gained `failRead[name]` (a read refused once). Mutations: 30 new
(`menu-*` 17, `restore-*` 3, `batch-*` 2, `rp-*` 3, `sidecar-*`/`adopt-shared-no-keep` 3, `awd-orig-*` 2) and 3 retargeted
(`draw-filter-svc`, `copy-file-menu`, `w3-open-orig-dropped`). Browser-only behaviour proven by hand: without the trailing
`fitMenus()` the drop-down check fails; with the old `.modal .x` the standards run reports 6 new findings; a guide path that
drops its group fails `menu-paths`; on v1.95's fixture keyboard L fails 4 and the standards run its coverage.
