### v1.97 (items 62, 72–81): the 5 Oct engineering review of v1.96, and the money map's nine cells

Built on Brad's go of 5 Oct 2026 in six workstreams (W1–W6, each in its own worktree, each check failing on v1.96 unless
labelled a CONTROL) and integrated by hand. Brad's answers that shaped it: a folder save that cannot keep its history copy
writes nothing (79); the nine deferred money-map cells are built (62); the reprogramming log takes whole dollars only (81).

**72–75 -- typed money and the engine (W1).** `amtRead(s, o)` reads the WHOLE entry (optional `-` and `$`, digits with
commas only between groups of three, a decimal part, K/M/B, spaces only between parts), rounds to a whole dollar on the
digits (half away from zero, said), bounded by `IN_MAX_AMT`; below zero only with `{neg:true}`. `parseAmt` answers the
figure, `null` for a blank (a deliberate clear) or `NaN` for a refusal; `amtNo(v, val, what, o)` keeps the old figure and
says why. All 18 callers were audited. `amtReadM` (integration) is the same for $M fields -- a bare number is millions --
used by the Funds Flow's partner/FMS money and the realignment's need, cap, target and credits ("750K" there was
$750,000,000). `setBATot` and `clinSetReq` spread with `apportion`; a v1.96 fractional budget base reopens apportioned
to its rounded total. `svcAlloc` floors each service at 0 (`svcAllocAsSaved` is raw); a saved negative is kept and
listed under Needs attention (`allocNegOf`, `attLists().neg`, ➖). `scalarSrcVec(fo, basis)` (integration) places a
whole-requirement source -- forced Disc/Mand, or a legacy split with mandatory against what discretionary leaves of each
service -- and both `tiers()` and the Two Spend Plans (`fsDirVec`) read it.

**76 -- every list the file carries marks it unsaved (W2).** `lsSet()` is the one counting point: `fileKeyOf(key)` names the
open board's key `'board'` (moves `_saveGen`, sets `DIRTY`), every other key the saved file carries `'side'` (moves
`_sideGen`), settings nothing; it counts before the write, so a refused write still counts (`_lsRefused`). The file save
captures `_sideGen` with its bytes and `sideWrote()` acknowledges only that; the folder save never acknowledges the side
lists; a download acknowledges nothing. `saveStateShow()` / `unsavedNow()` drive the indicator. The side state resets at
the end of the boot script, as `DIRTY` does. Side lists stay out of `DIRTY` on purpose: it drives the folder autosave,
the newer-copy refusal, the shared-state arrival and the cycle-view stash.

**77–79 -- import, restore, folder (W3).** `importBoards` refuses a locked cycle first, sorts results into the order picked,
refuses an unreadable file, two files for one appropriation, and a service set that differs from that appropriation's
own list (other appropriations are now checked too); a remap is asked through `svcGuardImport` and the packs follow
(`svcRelabelPacks`, integration -- all three remapping imports used to relabel the names only); then one `lsAll`, then
`PUBS` and `applyStore`; a late refusal puts storage and `PUBS` back. `driveRestore` pins appropriation, year, `MS`,
`_fyMoves` (counted in `switchYear`) and the copy's name before its first await; `moved()` is checked before the history
keep and at the final synchronous boundary; the copy's `ap`/`fy`/`store.cur` must match its name. `_driveStorePushRun`
always keeps the exact bytes it read (`driveStoreRead(dir, ap, fy, raw)`); `snapKeep(..., out)` returns 'kept'/'none'/
'failed', a save under ten minutes after the newest spaced copy keeps its predecessor as `…-recent.json`, and
`snapPrune(dir, fy, ap, keep, recents)` thins only after the copy and the write have both landed (the 24 spaced copies
reach as far back as before). `snapList` takes only `NotFoundError` for "no history". `setStat` counts (`_statN`), and
`liveTick` says "Saved automatically" only if nothing was said while its save ran.

**80 -- a board's own service list (W4).** `svcSaveList(list, from)`: `from[k]` is where new service k sat before (-1 for
new); the import's `svcRemapState(st, from, n0)` carries every year (an award of another width is left to the repair);
`BASE_ASK`, `CLINS`, `DATA.budget` and any data pack naming the old list are rewritten; held submittals and this
appropriation's reprogramming rows follow (a named row keeps its name and its `svcPos` seal moves with its place; a
rename renames `svcName` -- integration). `svcRenameKeys(ren)` carries what is filed under a name. `svcListBlock` refuses
a change under a kept PPBE cycle (its services keep names and places; one can be added at the end), and removal of a
service with reprogramming rows or held-submittal figures on it. Removal says first what the service holds, by kind and
year (`svcHeldBy`). One non-undoable `(services)` journal line; `jrnlBySvcPlace` stops putting back older entries that
carry a place. On a data-backed board (`SVC_FROM_DATA`) the panel shows the list only. `svcRemapState` also carries
`S.buys` (base, adjustment, unit cost) -- integration; an import that reordered services left them in the file's order.

**81 -- the reprogramming log (W5).** A row carries `svcName`, `svcPos` (the seal: the name counts only while
`svcPos === Number(r.svc)`) and `svcBy` ('pick' or 'pos'). `rpSvcsOf(ap)` finds an appropriation's own list (the board on
screen, `APPNS`, the stored board's pack, the file's `PUBS` pack, else the newer `MS.svc`); `rpSvcOf(r)` is the only
place a row's service is read; position-only rows are read against their own appropriation's list and marked "(by
position)" until picked; Unresolved is a summary line and counted. `rpAmtText` / `rpAmtTyped` read the $M field exactly
from its digits -- whole dollars only, anything finer refused with the dollar figure; a saved fraction is kept, marked
and named in the export's Check column. `rpNetM` nets exactly (BigInt counts of the finest unit).

**62 -- the nine cells (W5, W6).** Every cell is proven arithmetic by an oracle worked from the fixture (money map: 123
proven, 206 n/a, 0 open). The Two Spend Plans oracle found the plans funding a service above its ask when allocated or
directed above it, and a trimmed forced Disc/Mand row asking for its whole ask: `fsDirVec(it)` (inside `basisSvcOf`) is
now what both plans read. The ACE breakout's note and the Excel Two-Plan Forecast's note (integration) said things that
were not so; both now say what the export does.

**Checks.** New scenarios in `audit-board.mjs` (W1, W4, integration), `audit-import.mjs` (W1, W2, W3, W5) and
`audit-recon.mjs` (W5, W6, integration); 162 new mutations (1,052 in all) and 11 existing ones retargeted, each presweept. W6 seeded
`.math-reports/catchers-*.json` (git-ignored) so recon is tried first for its ids -- order only, never the verdict.

**Gate.** `npm run validate:math` passed on the final tree, 5 Oct 1:42–7:17 pm Eastern, 334.9 minutes: sweep 1041/1041 caught, 11 equivalent (1,052), fuzz seed 47996740, money map 123 proven / 206 n/a / 0 open. The suites stage took 7.4 minutes (4.8 in v1.96) and the sweep 319 (179); the fuzz and file verifier were unchanged, so the time is the new checks, not the machine -- allow about six hours.

### v1.98 (item 83): RCN/EEIC by service on a prime's CLINs, and every RCN/EEIC surface reads one rule

Built on Brad's go of 5 Oct 2026 after a prototype he saw first. His answers: the CLIN wins; flag gaps; include the
RCN-EEIC by Month fix.

**The rule.** `rcnOfClinSvc(id, clinKey, service)`: `clinRcnSvc[id][clinKey][service]` (a CLIN's choice for one
service, filed by the service's NAME), then `clinRcn[id][clinKey]` (the CLIN's standard), then `rcnAsgOf(id, service)`
(the requirement's choice for that service, then its own). `rcnOfClin` keeps its old meaning (standard, then the
requirement) and is what the standard picker and the "= CLIN's" label read. Every RCN/EEIC surface asks
`rcnOfClinSvc` for a CLIN's money and `rcnAsgOf(id, service)` for a requirement's: the RCN-EEIC by Month tab
(`monSheet` with `clinOf`), the Spend Plan Source, `aceAlignRows` (the ACE breakout and the panel's expansion) and
`rcnKeysOf` (now read off `aceAlignRows`' lines, so the mapping's "(n combinations)" is what the breakout prints).

**RCN-EEIC by Month.** `monSheet` splits a prime's money per service by `clinSplit` (month by month, NO DATE and the
ask the same way) and files each CLIN's part under `kk(clinOf(...))`: a key the list does not hold is NONE, because the
rollup prints only `keysOf()` and the not-assigned row -- anything else was in no row and no total. A requirement row
carries `mixed` when its service's money rides more than one combination; a prime funded nothing goes to `pend` the
same way. A grid with nothing on a service (`clinSplit(...,0)` empty) leaves that money whole on the requirement's own.
The SI tab passes no `clinOf` and is unchanged (its unknown codes now count as not set rather than vanishing).

**`aceAlignRows`.** `push` groups a line's Air Force services by their combination; one group is one line (as before);
several are a line per combination plus a line for the money no RCN/EEIC governs. The lines add to the line replaced.

**The CLIN grid.** `clinRcnCell` / `clinRcnStrip` beside `clinTable`. `clinAfSvcs(id, c)` -- the Air Force services
with money in the CLIN's grid or a request; the button shows for two or more. `clinRcnGaps(id, c)` -- services with grid
money whose combination does not exist (`rcnEntry`), the amber flag, and what the RECORD panel's field reads for a
prime. `rcnOptsFor(svs, chosen)` offers combinations that `rcnFits` every service in `svs`, plus a saved choice that
does not (marked ⚠) or no longer exists (its own option). `rcnClinOpen[id+'|'+key]` is UI state only.

**Hygiene.** `rcnDel` clears `clinRcn` and `clinRcnSvc` too (it cleared neither CLIN map before, though `rcnUse`
counted `clinRcn`). `clinRemove` of an ADDED CLIN clears its `clinRcn`, `clinRcnSvc` and `clinWbs` -- `clinAdd` hands
the next CLIN the first free `n<i>`, so they were inherited; a base CLIN keeps them for `clinRestore`. `clinRcnSvc` is
on the normalise list, `IMP_CATS`, `REQ_MAPS`, `yearCopy`'s `cp()` list and `PKG_DIFF_NAMES`; `svcRenameKeys` re-keys
it and `svcSaveList` drops a removed service's entries BEFORE the renames (so a rename onto a freed name keeps its own).
The round-trip fuzz and the hostile board seed it.

**Checks.** `audit-recon.mjs`: the RCN-EEIC tab (summary, requirements, prime contracts), the Spend Plan Source, their
agreement, the ACE breakout and mapping, against a typed oracle; a CLIN on a removed combination; a prime funded
nothing. `audit-board.mjs`: the grid's controls, flags and choices; removing a combination; deleting an added CLIN; a
service renamed or removed; a copy, the import report and a corrupt container; and the REQ_MAPS sample. 28 new
mutations (1,080 in all), each presweept.

**Gate.** `npm run validate:math` passed on the final tree, 5 Oct 9:06 pm – 6 Oct 2:58 am Eastern, 352.3 minutes: sweep
1069/1069 caught, 11 equivalent (1,080), fuzz seed 712613839, money map 123 proven / 206 n/a / 0 open.

### v1.99 (items 85–98): the 6 Oct review of v1.98 -- exact splits, a batch that cannot be put back, the whole $M entry, a cycle's own list; whole quantities, codes refused, the funds flow's scope, estimate-only offices, side lists across a reload, an estimate's earlier copies

Built on Brad's go of 6 Oct 2026. An independent review of v1.98 (main `0b6dc1b`) found four more; each was confirmed on
v1.98 first.

**`apportion` is exact (item 85).** Each weight is an exact integer (`apExact`: a whole number as itself; a fraction as its
mantissa, all over the smallest power of two among them, read from the bits -- nothing is rounded), each share the exact
BigInt quotient and remainder, floored toward minus infinity; leftover dollars go to the largest remainders, ties to the
lower service; no weight, evenly. Floating point put a dollar on the wrong service when two remainders were close (it
reached budget size: $100M over [19,698,320, 191,126,895, 89,174,792]) and let rounding noise settle exact ties. About 2 µs
a split. The test's oracle makes fractions exact by doubling, a different route on purpose.

**A batch that cannot be put back (item 86).** `lsAll` returns its before-reading (`held`) on success too; `importBoards`
uses it and never reads storage a second time. After a late refusal: `lsPutBack(W, held)` tries every key, newest first,
whatever happened to the one before, and reads each back ('was' / 'new' / '?'); `impBoardSnap` / `impBoardBack` keep and
restore the board on screen (the old MS object is untouched by `applyStore`, which works on a copy) and `_jrnlOff`. All
back → "Nothing was imported". Anything else → `batchImportUndone`: `IMP_MIXED_KEY` with the keys and `kind:'batch'`,
`_impRescue = {pre, incs:[{text,name}], mixed:true}` (`fullImportRescue('inc0')`…, `impRescueBtns()`), and
`impMixedBlock` holds Save, the folder and the autosave exactly as after a full import. `impMixedMsg` names the batch.

**The whole $M entry (item 87).** `rpAmtTyped` reads the whole trimmed entry; longer than `RP_AMT_MAX` (200) is refused as
such; `rpCut` shortens only what is quoted back.

**A cycle's own RCN / EEIC list (item 88).** `cycLock` stores `rcn` (a copy of `rcnReg()`) on the cycle; `cycView` sets the
throwaway `MS.rcn` from `cycRcnOf(c)`, so every RCN reader (`rcnReg()`) in the view reads the cycle's list. `cycRcnOf`
returns null for a cycle without one (locked before v1.99, or a list that is not a list); then the view's message, the
cycle's row (`.cyc-rcn-today`) and the RCN-EEIC tab's title (`cycRcnTodayNote()`) say `CYC_RCN_TODAY`. Nothing else a
cycle reads is frozen yet -- destinations, bands, the BAE list, the data pack (item 89); the panel and Help say so (item 98).

**Items 90–98 (Brad's scope of 6 Oct 2026).**
- *Quantities (90).* `qtyRead(txt)` takes a whole number from 0 (digits, or groups of three with commas; blank is 0; a
  safe integer) and refuses anything else, sign and Unicode minus included. `buyDollars(q,c)` is null unless both are safe
  integers ≥ 0 and the product is a safe integer ≤ `IN_MAX_AMT`, read through `buyMax()`: `applyState` recomputes the buy
  lines BEFORE the line declaring `IN_MAX_AMT` runs, and reading the const there threw and stopped the board opening (found
  by the import suite; a check now opens a saved board with buy lines and wants no script error). A saved quantity that is
  not one is kept as saved, counts $0 (`buyDollarsAt`), is listed (`buyBadOf`, `ATT_KIND.buy`) and marked in the panel.
- *Codes (91).* `codeTake(val, kind)` → `{v}` or `{why}`; limits in `codeMax`. Prompt paths reopen holding the text
  (`editPid(id, typed)`, `editCcar`, `clinAdd(pid, num, desc)`, `clinSetField(..., typed)`, `wbsQuick(id, typed)`); inline
  fields keep what was typed, marked (`codeRefusedIn`); the new-requirement form refuses and keeps its text (no silent
  `maxlength`). An imported code is kept whole: `codeIssues()` lists it (Needs attention `code`, Pre-flight `code`). It runs
  as the board draws, so it reads a prime's CLINs through `clinListRO` -- `clinList` creates empty entries on a read, and
  a reopened board then held CLIN entries its file never had (the round-trip fuzz caught it).
- *Funds flow (92, 93).* `exportFlow` reads `flowCalc()` -- the panel's years and appropriations; `flowFy` is gone. Partner
  and FMS money is portfolio-wide, once (`flowOtherNote()`). `flowYearKind(fy, slug)` decides executed or planned per
  appropriation (`flowExecYears(slug)`); `flowYearMix` / `flowMixWords` name a mixed year in the scope.
- *ACE tie-out (94).* An office with estimate lines and no requirement in a year is a row (estimate only) in the block and
  its TOTAL; beneath it, `Estimate for this appropriation = the TOTAL + lines under no office`, and DOES NOT TIE if not.
  The panel's `.aceestonly` row and `.aceacct` line say the same. Lines Nothing Claims notes beneath its list that it is not
  a second count.
- *Side lists (95).* Imports stage (`importSrc`: `nextAll`; `driveAcePull`: `staged`); `driveRpPush` keeps the merge
  (`persistRp`) before writing the folder and says "kept in this browser, not shared"; `destFold` reports `touched` and
  `destMigrate` stores it. Across a reload: `sideBootNote(key, fb)` keeps each store's file copy as text and what storage
  held BEFORE opening (`.pre`); `sideBootCheck()` at the end of opening flags a list (and another appropriation's plan or
  cycle store) storage held that a save would write over a different one. What opening an older file wrote itself is not
  counted: it is remembered against `PUB_FILE_TS` under `SIDE_OPEN_KEY` ('prio27sideOpen'), so the next opening of the same
  file reads it the same way. `sideUnsaved()` reads `sideBootNote.keys`; `sideWrote()` clears them; `sideBootSay()` names
  them in the saved-state tooltip. A list storage refused while opening stays unsaved (`_lsRefused` is no longer cleared).
  What opening writes into the destination list or a cycle store's list link is stored without a new time
  (`_destOpening`) unless a key was made or moved (`stamp`): stamping it made two openings of one file save different
  bytes (`boot-same` caught it).
- *An estimate's earlier copies (96).* `aceHistShow()` lists `history/spmt-ace-<name>-<stamp>.json` (`ACE_HIST_RE`);
  `aceRestore(i, gen)` pins the copy by name before the first wait (a list read again since refuses), runs Replace's
  checks, keeps the folder's copy (`snapKeep(..., force, out)`) and reads it back byte for byte, reads the folder again
  before the write, and writes the kept bytes exactly. The board is not changed. `snapMatch` escapes what it is given.
- *Classify grid (97).* `matClinOwn(id)` counts a prime's CLINs with their own RCN / EEIC; the cell, the bulk preview
  (`clinOwn`) and `.mat-rcnnote` say so. The setter is unchanged.
- *Cycles (98).* The panel's `.cyc-keeps` hint and Help (`t-fydp`) say what a cycle keeps and what it reads as it is now.

**Checks.** `audit-recon.mjs` "v1.99 item 85", "item 87", "item 88", "items 92–93", "item 94", "items 97–98";
`audit-import.mjs` "v1.99 — a batch that cannot be put back…", "…side lists… (item 95)", "…a cost estimate the folder kept…
(item 96)", and the version pattern (v1.99, v2.00, malformed labels, compared as (major, minor)); `audit-board.mjs` "v1.99 —
a buy quantity…(item 90)", "…a code longer than its limit…(item 91)". Every check fails on v1.98 unless labelled a CONTROL.
Expected changes kept to expectations, fixtures as they were: the funds-flow tests set the panel's selection
(`flowCfg().fys`), and the v1.97 ACE tie-out test checks the new accounted-for line on its own. 59 new mutations, 7
retargeted (1,139 in all).
**Gate.** `npm run validate:math` passed on the candidate tree (`5942e58`), 7 Oct 2026, run as a restart-proof batch runner
(same nine stages, commands and pass rules, sweep in twelve saved batches) after four six-hour single runs were each ended
by a container restart: 5:09 am – 2:58 pm Eastern on one container, sweep 1128/1128 caught, 11 equivalent (1,139), 0 survived
/ not applied / crashed / disagreeing, fuzz seed 721033589, money map 123 proven / 206 n/a / 0 open. Independent
expected-versus-actual on the candidate board: 23 comparisons, 0 differences. The batch runner is kept at
`scratchpad/v199/gate-chunked/` with per-stage and per-batch logs.

### v2.00 (item 99): imported text is drawn as text, imported names cannot reach inside the program, and a file a reviewer can navigate

Built on Brad's go of 7 Oct 2026. A security review of v1.99 for Brad's security team confirmed 27 places where a value
from an imported board, package, workbook, peer folder file or side store reached the page as HTML; each was reproduced on
v1.98 and v1.99 and shown running in Chromium. Details and crafted files are in the owner's private annex, outside the repo.
Four later passes found more:

- a self-audit of the first candidate (two more sinks);
- a second audit (two over-escapes);
- a final review and full sweep of the second candidate (raw siblings of fixed sinks, and others);
- a key sweep, which found imported names used as object keys. Brad put that class in v2.00 on 9 Oct.

**Escaped where drawn.** A value that did not come from this board's own code is escaped where it is drawn, however it
arrived; the import check is not relied on to have cleaned it. Every display sink the review and the later passes found
passes the value through `esc()` at the point it is written into markup, an attribute or an inline handler. When you fix
one, look at its siblings: the misses the final review found were all next to a fixed sink -- the Cancel lines beside
`pkgApply`'s escaped success line, the kept and no-FY counts beside `flowImportReport`'s escaped read count.

**Escape where drawn, and nowhere else.** `esc()` turns & < " ' into entities, which is right only where the string is
parsed as HTML. Never escape where a value is BUILT, and never escape text that is not HTML: a `confirm()` / `alert()`
message, a workbook cell, a download file name, `document.title`, or a string that will be escaped again later.

- **The first candidate (`608444e`) broke this twice.** `flowCalc` escaped the pseudo-appropriation token into `F.label`,
  so "O&M Carry" read "O&amp;M Carry"; and `bmSeedFromPp`'s `confirm()` escaped the service names. `F.label` is built
  raw, and every HTML use escapes it.
- **The cycle and position labels come in two forms.** `blLblTxt(key)` and `ppLblTxt(key)` give text, for dialogs, file
  names, the title, `textContent` and workbook cells. `blLbl(key)` and `ppLbl(key)` return that text escaped once, for
  markup, and are never wrapped in `esc()` again. Use the `Txt` form anywhere that is not HTML.
- **Text that came from markup is decoded, not stripped.** Where markup has to become plain text (the package conflict
  dialog, the journal search, the pre-flight copy, the Put back question), it goes through `diagPlain()`, which decodes
  entities through an inert parser. A regex strip leaves `&amp;` behind.

**Numbers on load.** `wholeOr(v, d)` returns a whole number or the fallback. `normalizeStore` reads `MS.base` and `MS.cur`
through it, `pkgNums` reads a package ledger's sequences through it, and the cycle view reads `c.base` through it. Only
year and sequence fields are converted on load. Buy quantities and other counts are escaped where they are drawn, so a
saved quantity such as "7" stays as saved (v1.99 item 90 lists it for repair).

A few counts drawn into markup are now read as whole numbers where they are drawn:

- the CCaR records count in the Data menu (`wholeOr`);
- the roll panel's year count;
- the cost-estimate line counts (`Array.isArray(...) ? length : 0`).

**Structural guards.**

- `jrnlPathSafe`: Put back refuses a journal path through a built-in name, and the import check refuses a journal entry
  that carries one.
- `inShapeWhy(o, path, asPlan)` checks every year's plan, whether or not it has an `order` list, including a store
  whose `years` is a list.
- `pkgApply` refuses a package whose `seq` is not a whole number, or whose `ap` is not one of `PKG_NAMES`' own keys.
- `confSameHost(link)` follows a Confluence download link only on the configured host.
- `linkOpenable(name)` opens only a file name, a path or an http(s)/file address, never another scheme ending in
  `.html`.

**The names every object already answers to.** A value used as a property name on an ordinary object can be a name the
object inherits: `__proto__`, `constructor`, `toString`, `hasOwnProperty` and the rest of `Object.prototype`. Reading
one returns the built-in; writing through `__proto__` writes onto every object in the page. Four rules:

- **The door refuses them.** `IN_KEY_BAD` is `Object.getOwnPropertyNames(Object.prototype)` plus `__proto__` and
  `prototype`, read from the browser itself. The import check refuses one as any field name, as a requirement id
  (`inIdWhy`), as an office, service or band name (`inNameWhy`), as a submittal's office, and as a destination key or a
  destination a requirement names. Typed office and service names go through `inNameWhy` too. `importAce` will not file
  an estimate under one.
- **A value the board later uses as a key is checked as a key.** `IN_VAL_KEYS` (`id`, `nid`, `k`, `key`, `grp`, `pmo`,
  `svc`, `ap`): wherever such a field sits in an imported file, its text value may not be a built-in name, and an id or
  `nid` may not begin with two underscores (`planWalk` keeps `__rem` and `__cut` beside the ids). A data pack holding
  only `items` is still checked as a requirement list (`/\.dataPack$/`). Add a field here when the board starts keying
  anything by a new imported value.
- **Workbook data never passes the door, so its maps have no prototype.** An execution row's title, event, office,
  month or budget; an estimate line's service; a BAE budget name; a cost element; a PPBE line key. Any map keyed by one
  is created with `Object.create(null)`, as in `exAgg`, `rlg*`, `posExec`, `pmoDetail`, `ceSummary`, `exportPPT`,
  `exportExcel`, `cycProjOf`, `spGather`, `destFold`, `forecast`, `fydpRows`, `exportCycCompare`, `acePmoGroups`,
  `fydpAceShare`, `spProjByPmo`, `exX` and the others (57 maps on 50 lines).
- **A fixed table looked up by an imported value is read by own key.** `MIPR_LBL` has no prototype; the saved estimate
  choice, `destPkgIn`'s re-pointing, `blGet`, `flowSubOf`, `ppSetV` and `rpMerge`'s deletion marks read own keys only
  (`Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call`).

So: never key an ordinary `{}` by a value from a file or a workbook. Use `Object.create(null)`, or read with
`hasOwnProperty`. Upper-cased or prefixed keys (`'r:'+id`, `ccarNorm`) are safe as they are.

**Reviewability.**

- 42 section banners (`// §NN TITLE`, anchored to a function, with the table of sections at §01 -- search for §).
- 21 functions and 2 variables nothing used were removed (`removeYear`, the chip picker, `rpAdd` / `rpSet` / `rpDel`,
  `RPST`, `rowChipId`, ...).
- Stale comments corrected.
- The PowerPoint template line fenced: `check.mjs` parses `TPL_PARTS` as JSON, requires every value to be base64, and
  checks the line's SHA-256 against `tools/tpl-parts.sha256` (re-record it on a deliberate template change).

**Checks.** `tools/export-audit/audit-inject.mjs` is in `audit:suites` and last in the mutation sweep's catching set. It
holds checks for 44 findings plus 4 text-fidelity guards, of four kinds, each labelled in the file:

- **Runtime draws** go through the board's own renderer. A runtime check must call the board's function: the first
  candidate had eight that built their own escaped markup and passed on any board. They were removed in `23a18ee`
  (8 Oct), after `608444e`'s gate and before any later one.
- **Probes** drive one structural guard each.
- **Source pins** cover sinks deep inside a panel render. The hostile sweep does not plant those fields, so the pin is
  their only check.
- **Key checks** (K1–K8) drive `inboundCheck`, `exAgg`, the `rlg*` scans, `posExec`, `pmoDetail`, `ceSummary`,
  `rpMerge`, the destination re-pointing, the `IN_VAL_KEYS` rule and the lookups keyed by an office, a category or a
  position line, with built-in names. They require that nothing lands on `Object.prototype` or a built-in, and that
  every dollar is still counted. K5 pins the safe maps in the source.

The guards: T1 drives `flowCalc`. T2–T4 pin the plain forms, so each guards the over-escape it names, not every possible
one.

The suite is clean on v2.00. On v1.99 (`BOARD=<a v1.99 board> node tools/export-audit/audit-inject.mjs`), all 44 finding
checks fail, and so do T3 and T4, which pin helpers v2.00 introduced: 159 failing assertions. The banner counts
assertions, not checks.

135 mutations (`v200-*`) each undo one fix or re-add one over-escape. Seven existing mutations were retargeted to lines
v2.00 rewrote. That makes 1,274 targets, each matching exactly once -- applicable, which is not the same as caught. At
`1c94537`, `audit-inject` alone did not catch `v200-id-builtin-open` or `v200-spmt-pmo-open`: the K1 inputs for those rules
sat in fields `IN_VAL_KEYS` refuses too. **A K-check input must reach only the rule it is for.** K1's `order` (a valueOf id
in a plan's priority order) and `pmoChars` (a submittal office carrying markup) do. A mutation's catch is claimed only
from a run that saw it fail.
With no catcher on record, `mutate.mjs` tries `audit-inject` first for a `v200-*` id (`FIRST_BY_PREFIX`); a recorded
catcher still goes first. Order only, never the verdict. Add a prefix there when a new family has one suite built to
see it.

**Gate.** v2.00 shipped on a **text-only lean gate**, not `validate:math`. That was Brad's decision on 18 and 19 Oct: no
mutation run before the merge, `mutate.mjs` read as text only. It ran on the pinned integration tree `e2d0bc6`, 19 Oct
2026, Eastern. The merged `b71b18e` adds only `recovery/` records, and its board and tools are identical.

What ran:
- `npm run check` passed (6:22 am).
- audit-import, whole and alone: ALL CHECKS PASSED (12 min 52 s).
- Every other suite, one at a time: passed (audit-board, audit-recon, audit-hostile, audit-ppt, legacy-fsrc, boot-same,
  audit-inject, audit-other, audit, audit-ui).
- mcount: 2,015 of 2,015 targets match once.
- `verify:grid` in both font lanes: only the carried limits (layout-fits D ×8, and toolbar-fits at 1152 px, which the left
  menu later closed).
- The text stages at 7:36 am, every one exit 0:
  - defect register: 13 known defects, guarded by 43 mutations;
  - money coverage: 26/26 surfaces;
  - money map: 329 cells, 123 proven, 206 n/a, 0 open;
  - trace matrix: every link holds;
  - round-trip fuzz: nothing lost, seed 398037213;
  - file verifier self-test: held.
- `npm run standards` found 9 new findings in the new layout. **`main` shipped with them at `b71b18e`.** The fix
  (`91484f0`, 0 findings at `bcc49cd`) was pushed to the branch after the merge and reached `main` with v2.01.

**Not run:**
- The mutation sweep, of any target. The 1,139 pre-v2.00 entries were not swept at this tree, and neither were the
  ~880 v2.00 targets: they were proven one at a time against their own checks, never as a set (v2.02 item A1).
- The per-commit check agents for the four 19 Oct blocker commits. The builders' checks and the every-suite run above
  replaced them.
- UI-C1's acceptance, repeated on the integrated tree.
- The G22 cross-track review of the exports. The locked-views review was done by Codex (D3); its one finding, D3-1,
  shipped in v2.01.
- U37's round-8 tests review. It was stopped; its money review was clean.

Records are on `claude/v200-fixes-wip`: `recovery/v200/evidence/final-e2d0bc6/` and
`session-records/FINAL-GATE-RUNBOOK.md`.

### v2.01: what v2.00 left on its branch, a carriage return written as &#13;, a readable RECORD panel, a longer PID

PR #13 merged v2.00 at `b71b18e` on 19 Oct; the eight commits that followed on `claude/orbit-v2.00` (the left menu, the nine
Section 508 fixes, the lockfile, the generator note, Codex D2-1 and D3-1) ship here unchanged -- `NEXT-VERSION.md` lists them by
commit. **Before pushing anything meant for a PR, read the PR's state and check `merged`.**

The one fix: `_xesc` writes a carriage return as `&#13;`. XML allows a CR, so U37 does not refuse it, but every XML reader turns
a raw CR into a line feed before the text is seen; ORBIT's own `_xunesc` keeps it, so round trips here could not notice.
**A text check on an export reads the bytes the way another program does** -- unzip what reached the Blob and parse each part
with a real XML parser -- never with the board's own reader, which agrees with the writer by construction. Guarded by
`audit-recon.mjs` "v2.01 (D4-1) — …" and `v201-d41-cr-raw`.

**The RECORD panel prints nothing it cannot show whole** (items 102, 103). A figure in the By service table is a `.fig` span
that never breaks; the cells do not clip; the UFR column takes its widest figure's width; and `inspSplitFit()` steps the
table's type down (not below 9px) until every box and figure fits the panel's width -- it runs at the end of every
`inspectorBody()`, on a resize and when the panel's width changes (a ResizeObserver: the docked panel takes its width after it
is drawn, and `drawerOpen()` returns early when docked). The BAE pickers each take the panel's width. `tools/layout-fits.mjs`
K reads every requirement's panel as the browser drew it and fails on anything clipped, ellipsed, spilled, past the edge, a
field cutting its value or a picker cutting its name. **A new control or figure in the panel must pass K.** A PID is up to 35
characters (`codeMax`).

### v2.02: ATR/BTR tracking removed; the log is carried, not shown

Brad, 21 Oct 2026: *"For ATR/BTRs and the tracking there, this can be removed entirely from ORBIT. ... ATRs and BTRs do not
effect future year funding, just active year funding. ORBIT is the future, while CCAR is the active year."*

**Removed:** the ⚖ Reprogramming Actions panel (`#rpPanel`, `rpBody`, `rpSummaryHtml`), the ⬇ ATR/BTR Log export
(`exportReprog`), the tracker's code (`toggleRp`, the filters, `rpSvcsOf`/`rpListsOf`/`rpSvcOf`, the whole-dollar $M helpers
`rpAmtTyped`/`rpNetM` and the rest, `rpCheckOf`, `rpSvcSel`, `rpMigrate`, `rpAddEntry`, `rpSetField`, `rpDelEntry`), the
folder share (`driveRpPush`, `driveRpPull`, `DRIVE_RP` and their calls; the folder's `spmt-reprog.json` is left alone), its
menu entries (Execution ▸, and the Reports & exports section *Execution & reprogramming*), the Live view row, the guide's
section, and the refusal in `svcListBlock` to remove a service reprogramming rows sit on (nothing could show or edit them,
so it could never be satisfied).

**Carried, byte for byte, because deleting user data to tidy up is not a trade this project makes:** `RPDB` (the boot pick
of the file's `rpDb` and `prio27reprog`, with `sideBootNote`), `persistRp()`, `savedHtmlOf`'s `rpDb` (Save, the PMO copy,
the work kept aside, the board as it was before a full import), `fileKeyOf`/`sideBootCheck`/`sideBootSay`, a full import's
carry, `impMemSnap`/`impMemRestore`, `importStores`' merge (`rpMerge`, with `rpGone`/`rpTouch`), `oneAppnHtml`, `pkgStrip`,
`diagSecrets`, the FYDP frame shim, and the legacy `S.reprog` normalisation. The service-list upkeep keeps a stored row's
service meaning right (`rpAll`, `rpSlug`, `rpNamed`, `svcRpIdx`): a rename renames `svcName`, a move moves the place and the
seal, and a row that carried only the PLACE of a removed service is given that service's name, sealed where it stood
(`v202-rp-removed-place-unsealed`). The tracker can come back to a log that means what it meant.

The feature's checks went with it (audit-recon "the reprogramming log", the five W5 log scenarios, "v1.99 item 87", the ATR
parts of W7 F1; audit-import NR03, W5.7, item 95's share and U13's share); the carry checks stay, the W2 unsaved-list check
now changing the log through `persistRp`. 27 mutations retired as obsolete, 3 retargeted, 1 new.

### v2.03: cleaner code -- what nothing could reach, the card leftovers, one message line, one field registry

Brad, 21 Oct 2026: *"I would like to optimize our code"*, then **cleaner code**, all four items, built before the sweep.
`NEXT-VERSION.md` › Shipped › v2.03 has every detail.

**Unreachable code is found by a call graph, not by a name count** (item 104). Counting references found nothing dead:
every function was named somewhere, often only in a comment or by another dead function. A parser-based trace from the
top-level code and every handler in the markup found 15. The command bar, `setBATot`, `moveTo`, `chipBg`/`bandInk`,
`srcBudget` and `inboundCheckDest` went; four functions only the tests call stay as the tests' entry points.

**The card build is gone from the code too** (item 105): the guide's `data-b`, `fldHasPanel`, the row expander's state,
the Filter menu's ☰ By-service detail (which only the card's rows read), and `gridDedupe`. `render()` keeps its wrapper
(`renderBoard`, then the RECORD panel) on purpose -- see the note at the top of this file.

**A message that matters stays until it is read** (item 19). `setStat()` is still the one way to say something. Every
message is also kept for the session in `STAT_LOG`; a warning or refusal (`diagIsWarn`) or an import's result
(`statKeep`) is kept UNREAD until More, its ✕, or the list opens it, and **⚠ N unread** (`#statKeep`) beside the line opens
**Messages this session** (`#msgPanel`). The list is the tab's and never the board's: nothing goes into the file or the
journal (Brad: "session log, not file"), so a refused import still changes nothing. Shown as text, so an old message's
buttons cannot act on the board as it is now. **A new import's result goes through `statKeep()`, not `setStat()`.**

**A requirement's fields are named in one place** (item 20). `REQ_FIELD` (declared early, beside `PMODE`, because the
board draws its rows while the script is still being read) maps each field's key to its label; `FL(k)` and `FLI(k)` (with
the icon) read it. `recF()` takes the KEY and each field carries `k`; code compares keys, never labels (the proposal gate
used to match labels, which is how a test goes hollow on a rename). The guide names a field with
`<span data-fl="key">` (`data-fi` for the icon), filled by `helpSections`. `npm run check` "field labels" refuses a
`recF()` given a label, a `PMO_FIELDS` row that spells one, and the three written fields' names spelled anywhere outside
the registry; audit-board renames fields at runtime and every surface must follow. **A new field gets a `REQ_FIELD` entry
first; a new surface that names a field reads `FL()`.** The registry is a first slice: other labels are moved onto it as
they are touched, never as a big-bang rewrite.

**Two browser checks had been failing since v2.02 unseen.** `keyboard-reach` G2 and `left-menu` A still expected the
ATR/BTR workbook and its two calls. `verify:grid` stops at `layout` (the 8 carried D results), so nothing after it runs
in the nightly gate: **run `keys`, `dropdowns`, `dialogs`, `leftmenu` and `standards` yourself** after a change that moves
a menu or an export.

### v2.04: the CCaR records list names the record; an AI review carried by hand; Clear this computer; Confluence, SharePoint and the JSON endpoint removed

Brad, 22 Oct 2026, "Go to all". `NEXT-VERSION.md` › Shipped › v2.04 has every detail.

**The CCaR records list names the record** (item 108). `recName` is a typed name, then the list's **Title**
(`ccarRecNameOf`), then the data pack's title -- READ, never copied, so a newer list renames. A requirement with no number of
its own is named by its CLINs: the one record they share, or "Multiple records (N)". The list's **Short Description** is
offered (`ccarDescOf`), written only into an EMPTY Description when a number is typed (`ccarDescFill`); the RECORD panel offers
to replace a written one and asks first. **The Description is the person's text: never overwrite it without asking.** A CLIN
line on the Spend Plan Source carries the CLIN's own number and name.

**The AI review never calls anything** (item 109). The file has no network route and a key would travel in every save, so
the round trip to GenAI.mil is carried by hand: `aiExport` writes the request, `aiImport` reads the answer through
`inboundCheck`, `aiRead` checks each line against the board, and the list is advisory and lives in the tab only. **Nothing
from it is written to the board** -- Brad: "it provides suggestions and analysis for human interaction and decision
management". A direct GenAI.mil call belongs to the Azure build (item 107), server-side.

**Clear this computer** (item 106, in part): every `prio27`/`spmt` key and `prio-fh`, never the file. A new storage key must
start `prio27` or `spmt`, or Clear this computer will not reach it.

**Confluence, SharePoint and the JSON endpoint are gone** (items 110, 111; Brad: "remove ... until we get closer to actually
going live with azure"). The Live view shares through the shared folder only, and the file makes no request to another host
(the legacy state file beside the board is the one `fetch` left). A machine set up for a removed transport opens on the
folder, off, is told, and loses its stored key. Do not bring a network transport back into the file: in the Azure build the
server talks to anything.

**The Content-Security-Policy says so to the browser** (item 106, built once the transports were gone). A `<meta>` in the head,
before any script, refuses every load from or connection to another host; `connect-src` is exactly `'self' file: data: blob:`.
`npm run check` holds its text and `npm run csp` proves Chromium enforces it (each refusal read from the
`securitypolicyviolation` event, because a request that merely fails offline proves nothing). A new feature that needs a
network source is an Azure feature (item 107), not a hole in this policy.

**The five-year spend plan workbook was rebuilt** (item 112; Brad, 22 Oct 2026). One model (`fydpBookModel`, from each
appropriation's own `fydpData`) is read by the workbook (`fydpBookSheets`) and the panel (`fydpBody`), so the panel cannot
print a figure the workbook does not: a Summary with a chart, a tab per appropriation, per service and per PMO; the base year
and the next four; Requirement and Allocated (**the lane draw, d+m** -- what a lock stores and every other surface calls
Funded) in $M; 1-N under the CCaR record printed once; — for a year not held. 📊 FYDP Spend Plan (Excel) opens the panel on
the plan on screen (`fydpOnScreen`) and its Export prints the plan shown. By Requirement, By PMO, By Service, the v1.85 PMO
tabs, the cost estimate and the PPBE position beside the plan are gone; the sections above that describe them are the record.
The service tabs and the PMO tabs each partition the appropriation tab.

**Every report workbook wears one house style** (item 113, style only). `buildXlsx(sheets, extra, xlsxHouse(name))` asks for
it; `_housePlan` paints from the styles the sheet already uses, so an export changes nothing but its call. The Spend Plan
Source and the 1A9A never ask (CCaR and the comptroller read them in), and the IDS template sheet is `plain` (`idsHouse`).
audit-other "v2.04 (item 113)" holds the rule that matters: **the values written dressed are the values written plain**, and
every part is XML another program reads, with counts, cell order and part order as Excel demands. It found the one defect the
build had: a cell the writer emits (`{v:'', s:2}`) padded a second time -- two cells with one reference, which Excel calls a
damaged file. **A new export that writes a workbook passes `xlsxHouse`**, or is added to that check's list of the two that do
not, with the reason.

**recon and import read old builds from git history** (`git show <sha>:…`): in a fresh shallow clone they fail with *invalid object name*; run `git fetch --unshallow` first (v2.06). **recon and import need a bigger heap on this container.** The default here is about 2 GB, and both die out of heap at it
(`exit 134`) on the commit before items 112-113 as well as after. Run them with `NODE_OPTIONS="--max-old-space-size=8192"`.

**A `let` that `syncBtns` reads is declared beside `let MS`** (the AI list's `aiIsOpen`/`AIREV`): the first build of 109
declared them with their code, and the left menu silently lost its Reports & exports box on boot. `npm run leftmenu` caught it.
**Mutation ids are single-quoted** (`{ id: '…'`): `money-coverage.mjs` reads only that form, so a double-quoted id is invisible
to the coverage census.

After a lock the status names the appropriations holding a plan that have not locked that cycle (`cycNotLocked`). Locking and
reopening stay on each appropriation's own board: a figure for another appropriation worked out on this page would be a second
engine (the v1.85 rule). **Who can lock is not enforced**: the file has no sign-in; the name is the one typed under Audit › My
editor name, the folder's permissions are the guard, and Help says the Azure build will enforce the role (item 107).

**Checks.** audit-board: "v2.05 — each appropriation is locked on its own board, named, and seen side by side (item 116)", the
v1.96 menu block's Reports & exports checks (sections, titles and tags, no icons, Find, Linked tool), the view's names.
audit-inject: two T4 pins and two K5 pins moved with the lines. Mutations: 15 new (`v205-114-*` 6, `v205-116-*` 9), 9 retargeted,
26 retired (2,108 targets, each matching once); every new and retargeted one was run against its check and caught.

**Proved with** every suite and browser check on the final tree (layout: only the carried D ×8; standards: nothing new), the
defect register, money coverage, the money map, the trace matrix, the fuzz and the file verifier; the sweep was not run. The
first whole run after the push found recon and ui still expecting v2.04's words -- **a change of wording is a change to every
suite that quotes it: grep the suites for the old text before the run, not after.**

**A test written as a JS string inside `ev(\`…\`)` is a template literal**: `\s` and `\p` there are `s` and `p` unless the
backslash is doubled. The first cut of the item 116 test "failed" on text with every s removed.

### v2.06 (items 117–120): a requirement from another year, linked; the PPBE positions box reads straight, closes its totals and takes $K

Brad, 23 Oct 2026, from photos of the real board; built on his "Go" of 24 Oct. `NEXT-VERSION.md` › Shipped › v2.06 has every
decision.

**Add from another year, linked (item 117).** The Add panel's second way in, *Or from another year* (`naFromDraw`): a fiscal
year (every year holding requirements but the one on screen), then that year's requirements for the office chosen at the top
(`naFromOffice`: the PMO filter, else a copy's own office, else the panel's PMO box). Each one added is a NEW requirement with
its own id (`reqFromYear`; `NEW-n`, `NEW-<office>-n` in a copy) carrying `it.prior = {fy, id}` -- Brad: *"link the requirements
together for trend data but keep them as separate actions"*. That is NOT Copy to year or Roll forward, which keep a board
requirement's id. What starts from that year's record is `NA_FROM_CARRY` plus the CCaR, the value by service and the award dates
moved by the year difference; the allocation, the funding split, ★ MC, the CLIN breakdown, the cost-estimate tie and the UPL
mark are not carried. **An added requirement's id is its year's own** (`newReqSeq` numbers each year by itself, as `yearCopy`
does), so FY27's NEW-1 and FY28's NEW-1 can be two requirements: `naFromHere` matches by id only an id from the board's data;
an added one is "already here" when linked or by its event and title. The link is read by `priorLink`/`priorTxt`: the RECORD
panel's *Continues FY27 A — title* (`priorHtml`, *no longer on the board* when that year lost it), the Priority Drill's last
column *Continues*, the review row (*from FY27 …*). It travels: Copy to year, a save, a PMO copy's proposal (`pmoProposalOf`),
`pmoTake` (`priorOk`), `pmoAcceptProposal`. The door refuses any other shape (`inPriorWhy`, on a plan's added requirement and
a submittal's proposal). A trend report that follows the chain is not built; the links are in place for one.

**PPBE Budget Positions (items 118–120).** Each RCN / EEIC line is one row: `.fctab` is `table-layout:fixed`, where `min-width`
is ignored, so the label column now has a `width` (`PP_LBL_W`), its cell never wraps (`td.pplbl`) and the table is as wide as
its columns (the wrapper scrolls). Both TOTAL rows have their Total (`ppTotCell`, from the cells the row prints; a bare
`<td></td>` before). A service can be **entered in $K** (`P.unit[svc]='k'`; Brad's USAF exhibits are in thousands, and BES28
USAF FY27 read $2.28M for $2.28B): **the model stays in whole dollars** -- a bare number typed under $K is read through amtRead's
own K (`ppAmtIn`, never rounded first), a figure with its own unit is read as written, the boxes show thousands (`ppAmtShow`).
Turning $K on for a service holding figures asks whether they were typed in $K; yes multiplies them and records it on the position
(`P.kx`), because `MS.ppbe` is outside the change journal; *undo the ×1,000* (`ppUnitUndo`) divides back and refuses while a
figure would not come back to a whole dollar. Turning $K off never moves a figure.

**Checks.** audit-board "v2.06 — PPBE Budget Positions: the TOTAL corners, and figures entered in $K (items 119, 120)" reads both
corners off the rendered box and holds each to its row and its column (n figures may sit n cents off, the $M rounding); audit-import
"v2.06 — add a requirement from another year, linked…" covers the picker, the office, "already here" both ways, what is and is not
carried, the drill, a reopen, the door, Copy to year, and a PMO copy through submit and accept. `npm run layout` L opens the
box (`toggleP2` -- setting `ppOpen` alone leaves it hidden, and the first cut measured nothing but zeros) and holds every line
to one row at four widths; it fails on v2.05. Mutations: 30 new (`v206-117-*` 18, `v206-118-*` 2, `v206-119-*` 4, `v206-120-*`
6), each run against its block on its own mutant and caught. The two 118 mutants pass the browser check one at a time (the
width alone, or the no-wrap alone, holds the row at these fonts) and are caught by the jsdom check; v2.05, with neither, fails it.

**The pre-merge review (25 Oct 2026; Brad: "Fixes plus the second review, then merge").** Five independent reviewers, each
serious finding re-run by a second trying to refute it, confirmed three defects and reported ten more; all are fixed:
- **A linked requirement is the same work.** `yearLinkOf(id, y)` names the requirement in a year that carries `id` by its link
  (a board-data id whatever year the link names; an added one only its own year), and `yearHas` reads it -- so Roll forward
  skips it, Copy and Move to year ask, Restore asks and Restore all names them (Cancel keeps them removed). A proposal that
  continues something the year already holds says so on the review row (`pmoPriorHere`); accepting it is still the reviewer's
  call (Brad: duplicates "will be caught and discussed in reviews").
- **A proposal carries its renamed plan event** (`titles`, through `pmoTake` and `pmoAcceptProposal`).
- **$K survives a service rename** (`svcRenameKeys` re-keys `P.unit` and `P.kx`); **the undo puts back only the cells the
  conversion multiplied** (`P.kx[svc].cells`, each only while it still holds what it became) and is offered only while $K is on;
  turning $K off ends it. **The message names what was read** (`ppAmtText` hands amtNo the K-suffixed text).
- **ALL SERVICES adds the column above it**: a service the position holds and the board no longer lists is a row
  (*not on this board*).
- **The picker:** "already here" by event and title is the same office's only; Add refuses if the office changed since the
  list was drawn (`naFromFor`), or the year has gone.
- **`reqNewId(g, n)`** mints a copy's `NEW-<office>-n` without a character an id may not carry (an office named *Brad's*
  minted an id the import check refused, the whole submittal with it; addReq had the same scheme).
- **A link to a year not held** says *FY25 is not on this board* (or *not in this locked cycle*), never *no longer on the board*.
  **Reset year keeps the numbers handed out** (`newSeq`), so a link never lands on a new requirement. **The record-name pen**
  replaces a carried record name.
- **A PMO copy submits only the year it was published for** (`pmoSubmit`): switched to another year it built a package of that
  year's work under the published one. Older than v2.06, reached anew by *Go to FY..*.
Checks: audit-import "v2.06 — the pre-merge review’s fixes…" and audit-board "v2.06 — the pre-merge review’s PPBE fixes…", both
failing on the candidate (be4de83); 20 `v206-fix-*` mutations, each caught by its block; six older ones retargeted to the lines
the fixes rewrote (`v197w1-guard-ppSetV`, `v197w4-rename-ppbe-left`, three `v206-120-*`, `v206-117-proposal-drops-link`).
**A second review of those fixes** (each finding re-run by an agent trying to refute it) confirmed four more, all fixed:
`yearLinkOf` now sees an added original in its own year (a requirement brought into FY28 and copied back to FY27 asked
nothing and put the work there twice); `pmoPriorHere` reads a board-data id whatever year the link names, as `yearLinkOf`
does; **Copy to year links a copy of an added requirement to its source** (`prior`, copy only, not move) and `naFromHere`
matches by the source's own link, so a copy given to another office is still the same work to the picker; and
`svcListBlock` refuses a rename onto a name a PPBE position still holds for a service no longer on the list (it laid one
service's figures over the other's and handed over the $K setting). The undo's refusal now guards only a hand-made record:
a recorded cell is always a multiple of 1,000. Checks: audit-import "v2.06 — the second review: …", audit-board "v2.06 — the
second review: a service is not renamed onto a name a PPBE position still holds"; 5 `v206-fix2-*` mutations, each caught;
`v206-117-yearcopy-drops-link` retargeted.

### v2.07 (item 121): Add requirement is one box that builds the whole record

Brad, 24 Oct 2026 (the request, the mock-up he saw twice and his answers, item by item); built on his "Go on 121" of 25 Oct.
`NEXT-VERSION.md` › Shipped › v2.07 has every decision.

**The strip is gone; the box is `#addPanel`**, a `.modal-bg` under the v1.93 box controller (named from its heading, Esc asks
before typing is lost, the keyboard kept inside). Every way in opens it: Review's ＋ Add requirement (`uiAddReq`), the Review
tools, the empty-year message. Two tabs, buttons with `aria-pressed` (never ARIA tabs): **New requirement** and **From another
year** (item 117's picker, moved in). `arShow()` draws the whole form ONCE (`arDraw`); `render()` only toggles the box
(`arSync`) and never redraws a field, which would take the caret and the text with it. `arHide()` empties it. Nothing is written
until Add, and then once: `reqCreate()` and one `save()`.

**One office** (Brad: *"Once they select their PMO, that's the only PMO they can support"*). `arOfficeStart()`: a copy's own office
(`myPmo`), else the office chosen at the top, else the box asks first (`#arOffStep`) and `arSetOffice()` locks it for as long as
the box is open. **A keyboard move only moves** (`arOffKey`, `arOffPick`, `arOffNav`): Chromium fires `change` on every arrow key
of a closed select, and the first cut locked the box to the first office on the first ArrowDown; Enter chooses, a pick with the
mouse chooses at once.

**What the box holds, and what it does not.** The plan event, Prime contract / OGC (two buttons), the CCaR record and PID
(through `codeTake`: refused over the limit, never cut, the box keeping the text), the value by service (`parseAmt`, whole
dollars, a figure rounded to the dollar said on Add; *Spread evenly* through `apportion`), a BAE budget per service with a value
from **that office's** names only (`budPickListFor(office, svc, 'disc')`, written by `budPutUnf` as the RECORD panel's not-funded
line writes it; a budget is read only where its service has a value), obligation category, cost element, WBS (never for a prime:
logged per CLIN), Goes to, and for a government activity (`destKeyIsOgc`) which awards to and the MIPR type, description, impact
statement and note. **Not in it, by Brad's word:** Mission Critical (*"Starts unstarred"*), the decision band (*"allocated by the
SFWG"*), award and obligation dates (*"people are just guessing"*), RCN / EEIC (the O&M team's, unless carried from another year),
and the record title, which is never typed: it is read from the CCaR records list for the number typed (`ccarNameOfNum`), and that
list's short description fills an EMPTY description, or one the box filled itself (`arDescAuto`), never one somebody wrote.

**The required list is ONE function, `reqNeeds(draft)`**, read by the box's rail and its Add, by a copy's proposal gate
(`pmoProposalGate` → `reqDraftOf`) and Pre-flight, and by From another year's *Ready* column (`reqDraftFrom`). The plan event,
Prime / OGC, the requirement, a budget for every service with a value, obligation category, WBS (waived for a prime), Goes to,
description, impact statement; and, for a government activity, which awards to and MIPR type. **A budget is asked for only where
one can be chosen**: a service whose office has no name for it, or a board with no BAE list, is shown waived -- a required field
nobody can fill would stop every add. Until v2.07 a copy's gate also asked for the projected award date and the PMO, and not for
the plan event or the budgets. Add pressed early marks every missing field, sends the keyboard to the first, and the button
reads *Add requirement — N still needed*.

**`reqCreate(g, rec, from, f)` is the one writer of a new requirement**: the box, a requirement brought in from another year
(`reqFromYear`, the carry of item 117 unchanged), and every test fixture (`addReq()` is gone). It writes the requirement, the
order (bottom), the office's ranking and its Split (`seedSplit`), then the box's fields; it never saves. A requirement brought
in that is complete is added at once; one that is not opens in New requirement filled in from its year's record and linked
(`arQueue`, `arFinNext`, *Skip this one*), carrying what the box does not show (`AR_CARRIED`).

**Fits whole at 1366 × 768 and up, one column that scrolls below** (Brad). Three columns at 1 : 1.75 : 1; the PID spans its
grid (a 35-character PID); the services table is 66 / 110 px (the service name never wraps) and the rest for the budget picker, which prints the longest real
BAE name whole in both fonts. `npm run layout` M measures it in Harbor and Midnight, this font and the Segoe stand-in.

**The reviewer sees what the box required.** A box-made proposal has no record title until the CCaR list names it, so the
review, `pmoFmtVal('__new')` and the change report read it by its plan event (`titles || event`), never "(untitled)" or its
provisional id; and the proposed text carries the PID, the budgets (`pmoNewBudTxt`, each marked *⚠ not offered for PSM on this
board* where this board's BAE list does not offer it -- accepted, it would read "chosen, and cannot be used"), the description,
the impact statement and the note.

**Found on the way, fixed:** `budParseName` built its second RegExp from the raw office name (the first was escaped), so an
office named *C++* made `budReg()` throw and the board stop drawing; `budReg()`'s cache is keyed on the offices too (one added
in Setup got no budgets until a reload); and, found by the hostile sweep, a BAE file entry with no readable date
(`budRegBody`) and an estimate with no totals (`spBodyDraw`) each stopped every redraw while their panel was open.

**Checks.** audit-other "v2.07 — the Add requirement box (item 121)" (the box through its rendered controls: office, the
keyboard on the office question, the required list, Add early, Spread, budgets, OGC, prime, the record title, refusals, the
reference peeked not spent, exactly what Add writes, Esc and Cancel, Add and open, no BAE list, a locked cycle, J / K, the
empty year, offices); audit-import "v2.07 — the Add box in a PMO copy (item 121)" (the copy's office, the budgets travelling,
an older proposal held back for its budget, the review and the change report, accepted, a copy on another year) and the
v2.06 blocks migrated to the box; audit-board's add fixtures through `reqCreate` and the box, and "v2.07 — a BAE file entry with
no date, or an estimate with no totals, does not stop the board drawing"; the hostile sweep through nine steps of the box;
`npm run panels`, `dialogs`, `keys` M, `standards` and `layout` M; and the review's two blocks, audit-other "v2.07 — the
review of the Add box: rounding, an office named (AV), …" and audit-import "v2.07 — the review of the Add box: Roll forward,
From another year, the budget gate, the reviewer". **154 new mutations** (92 `v207-121-*` and `v207-*` from the build, 31
`v207-rv-*` from the review, 31 `v207-rv2-*` / `v207-rv3-*` from the second review), each run against its own block on a mutant
of the final board and caught; 8 older ones retargeted to the box (`proposal-gate-open`, `add-total-*` 3,
`add-whole-refusal-gone`, `v197w1-guard-addReq`, `v199-newreq-ccar-cut`, `v206-117-no-link`). 2,317 targets, each matching
once.

**Found by an independent review before merge, fixed in v2.07** (25 Oct; six reviewers by area, each finding re-run by a
skeptic trying to refute it: 16 distinct defects confirmed, 1 refuted):
- **By name only where there is a name.** A box-made requirement has no record title until the CCaR list names it, so `yearHas`
  and `naFromHere` matched every requirement of one plan event by "event + blank title": Roll forward promised two copies and
  made one ("already there"), and From another year greyed out a different requirement. The name test needs a title now, and
  `yearHas` finds a rolled copy by its link itself (`yearLinkOf` skips a copy that took the same id).
- **What the form holds is never thrown away**: adding from From another year emptied New requirement (or closed the box);
  now those to finish wait behind what the form holds (`naFromAdd`).
- **The proposal gate reads a budget chosen on the mandatory line** where the RECORD panel shows that service no other line
  (`reqDraftOf` via `budLines`), checked against the office's names in any lane; a copy's proposal funded only from mandatory
  could otherwise be held back for good after a clear.
- **The box:** every rounding said, to $0 and in Spread evenly's total; an office named from punctuation ("(AV)") gets its
  budgets (`budParseName`'s one pattern, `offRe`, no word boundary, no lookbehind); an early Add's marks come off the control
  as well as its box; a key that opens the office list hands the choice back to the list; *Add and open its record* takes
  off a PMO filter that hides the new row, and is hidden while more brought in wait to be finished; a ticked row in From
  another year keeps the keyboard (`naFromCb_<id>`); a budget stored in another letter case is preselected; a WBS that is
  null or one blank line is read alike by the Ready column and the form; *Also carried* names fields by `FL()`.
- **The reviewer** reads a proposal as its row will (`pmoNewName`: plan event, and a typed record title beside it -- a
  copy before v2.07 left the event "(added at drill)").
- Text that still described the strip (the Review tool's tooltip, Reset year's message, Submit's hold-back message).
- **And two the review's own fixes needed**, found by the agents writing their checks: a requirement brought in can now wait
  behind one typed by hand, and Add's status line read the year off the one just added and threw (it reads the one now
  open, `nx`); and the office pattern stopped matching an office that starts with punctuation glued to the word before it
  ("…Discretionary(AV)", as it did before) -- such a name needs no separator, one that starts with a letter still does.

**A second review of those fixes** (three reviewers, each finding re-run by a skeptic: 12 confirmed, 0 refuted), all fixed:
- **"The same work", once more.** A year started as a copy holds a box-made requirement under the same id with no link: found
  by the id and its name, in `yearHas` and `naFromHere`. A candidate whose link shows it is the copy of ANOTHER requirement of
  that year is never this one, whatever the names (two box-made ones on one CCaR record and one plan event). A requirement whose
  original is the one on screen reads "already in" (`naFromHere`, yearLinkOf's rule).
- **The queue, redesigned.** The first review's fix let ones brought in wait behind a form typed by hand, invisibly, with no
  Skip, dropped by the next Add or by Esc. Now: a row in the form or waiting (`naFromWaiting`) cannot be ticked again (it was
  added twice); one being finished keeps its place and its bar counts what waits behind it live (`arFinBarDraw`); a form typed
  by hand is replaced only after a question, and Cancel changes nothing (`arFormHeld`); Add's status line names every year
  still waiting.
- **The gate:** on a service only mandatory pays, a usable mandatory choice wins over a retired discretionary one, and a
  service whose mandatory line has nothing to offer (the office's names are all discretionary) is waived (`nolist`).
- A WBS refused for its length keeps its mark while other fields are typed; Reset year's message reads as a sentence.
- And one more, found writing those checks: after a copy both years number from the same count, so the copy rule (same id,
  no link, same name) took two requirements added later, one each side, for one. It asks the office and the CCaR record too.

**Not changed, on purpose:** the row's ⋯ *Rename the CCaR record name* stays (the box does not type a record title; a person may
still name a record by hand afterwards); a copy published before v2.07 still gates its proposals by the v2.06 list (the board
does not judge a proposal again when it arrives); editing the whole record in one box is item 122.

### v2.08 (items 122, 123; F1–F7): the whole record in one box, the defects found mapping it, the security review kit

Brad, 26 Oct 2026: his answers to the twelve decisions of `recovery/v208/ITEM-122-BRIEF.md` ("Agree on all, D9 retire the
rename"), the seven defects found mapping item 122 ("build them with 122 in v2.08", F6: "lock the drill"), and "Go on 123".
`NEXT-VERSION.md` › Shipped › v2.08 has every decision.

**One box edits a requirement's record (item 122).** `#editPanel` (prefix `er`) replaces 📋 Requirement edit (`recPanel`,
`recBody`, `recOpen`, `recCount`) and the ⚠ / 📄 pop-ups (`fldPanel`, `FLD_DEF`, `openFld`, `fldSave`); the prompts
`editTitle`, `editCcar`, `editPid` and the row's `editRecName` went with them.
- **Opened** by `openEdit(id, at)` from the RECORD panel's three buttons (`toggleCons`, `toggleDesc`, `toggleNote`,
  `toggleRec` -- each at its own field), its ✎, CCaR and PID buttons, Needs attention's Go to (`attGo` imp, ccar, si) and the
  PMO hint (`pmoReassignHint`). Never from the row. On a locked cycle it does not open (`cycBlock`).
- **Holds the record fields** (D1): plan event, CCaR record, PID, Prime contract / OGC, PMO, obligation category, cost
  element, WBS (one line; a split or a prime's is shown with a way to its own editor), RCN / EEIC and per service, Navy SI
  code, Goes to, which awards to and MIPR type (an OGC's only), description, impact statement, note. **No money** (D4): the
  value by service, the BAE budgets, the band, ★, the awards and Planning stay on the RECORD panel. The record title is read
  (`recName` for the stored CCaR record, the list's name for one newly typed), never typed (D9).
- **A held draft** (D6), drawn once when it opens (`erDraw`; render() only toggles it, `erSync` -- the Add box's rule, so F2
  cannot reach it). `erStored(id)` is the record as stored, `erSnap` it as the box opened, `erRead()` what the box holds,
  `erChanges()` the difference. **Save** (`erSave`) refuses when the requirement is gone or its record changed under the box,
  refuses a code over its limit or an empty plan event with the typing kept, asks before an office change naming what
  follows (D8: this year only, last in the new office's ranking, the BAE choices naming the old office), then writes through
  **`reqApply(id, changes, draft)`, the one edit writer**: each changed field by its setter's rule, without their `save()`
  and `render()`, then one `save()` (one journal capture: an entry per changed path) and one `render()`. Cancel, ✕ and Esc
  discard; the box controller asks first (every field is `data-held`). ↑ prev / next ↓ ask before throwing typing away.
- **The required list warns** (D5): `reqNeeds()` on `erDraft(erRead())`, the value and budgets said to be set in the RECORD
  panel; the 📋 button counts the same list (D11).
- **A PMO copy** (D7): `erNotSent(id)` says up front when an edit will not be sent -- another office's requirement (judged by
  `pmoHomeOf`, so a hand-over is sent; a proposal by its office now), or a year the copy cannot submit. A copy's own proposal
  keeps its office. The renamed plan event of a requirement the board added is kept as an override (`S.titles`) so it is
  sent (F5).
- Goes to that is not a government activity takes which awards to and the MIPR type with it (D10).
- **The review of the box** (three reviewers, each finding re-run by a skeptic, `wf_cc204adf-d52`), all fixed:
  - **Kept as saved.** A stored value the box's lists do not hold (an obligation category, Goes to, RCN / EEIC, a service's
    own) is offered as itself, marked, and left alone. Read as not set, Save deleted it.
  - **Goes to** is written in `setDest`'s shape, changing only what changed. No empty MIPR container is made. A hop hidden
    behind a prime never comes back, from the box or from `setDest`, and is not counted as a destination in use
    (`flowEntUse`).
  - **A WBS typed after Prime → OGC is saved.** The SI code has *follows the rules* (`'\u0004'`: deletes `S.si`).
  - **↑ prev / next ↓** re-snapshots the box controller.
  - **In Brief the box does not open** and says so: Brief's label says nothing is edited there.
  - **F5 at both ends.** An added requirement's plan event is its own event (`pmoVal`), compared and sent. Copies older
    than v2.08 are excepted.
  - **A requirement restored in a copy** is said in the box, and named at Submit (it was published removed).
  - **Classify.** The per-service RCN / EEIC bulk reaches only rows that offer it. The office cell is chosen with Enter or
    a mouse pick and never brushed past by an arrow key (`selNavKey`). A copy's proposal keeps its office (`setGrp`).
  - **WBS from execution data** in a copy counts the CLINs it could not write apart. The absorber's ✕ is dimmed in a copy.
  - **F2 holds only the focused field.** An `input` event on a field that is no longer in the page reaches nothing.
- **A second review, of those fixes** (`wf_f7756a59-8e9`: 11 confirmed, 2 partly, none refuted), all fixed:
  - **The review compares a field as it is read, never as it is stored** (`pmoBare`: awards without their bookkeeping, Goes to
    as `destOf` reads it, a WBS line without an empty vector). `{to:'lm'}` and `{to:'lm', end:''}` are one answer; compared
    as stored, a Goes to or a WBS both sides agreed on was offered as a conflict. An accepted Goes to is written in `setDest`'s
    shape (`pmoNorm`). **A new field the review compares, whose setters can store one answer two ways, goes through `pmoBare`.**
  - **A rename is an override filed by id, for every requirement** (`reqApply` writes `S.titles[id]`, never `it.event` on an
    added one). The journal files an added requirement's own fields by its PLACE in `S.custom` (`['custom', 1, …]`), so a
    Put back after a delete above it lands on the next one; the plan event is now out of that, and the rest is item 124.
  - A rename a copy older than v2.08 sent as a cleared override (null) is compared with the override alone, so it settles.
  - Classify's bulk office in a copy counts the copy's own proposal as left alone (`matBulk` `grp`: `applies`).
  - **F2 in Chromium.** The browser fires `change` on the focused field as `innerHTML` removes it, and the page hears it.
    `inspectorBody()` takes the held field's `onchange` off for the redraw, and `inspHeldBack` puts the typing back whatever
    that change did to `__pend`. jsdom fires nothing: audit-board fires it inside the draw (`inspectorDraw` wrapped), and
    `npm run keys` P types into the real panel in Chromium.
  - **Classify's office cell chooses after the click, not on its mousedown** (`selNavBlur`, `_selPtrDown`): chosen on the
    mousedown, the redraw took the clicked control away and the click went nowhere. A press on the cell itself lets a mouse
    pick choose at once (`__nav` cleared). `npm run keys` Q.
  - The CCaR record: typing the file's own record back leaves no override, and clearing an override says it is back to the
    file's. The SI code: a pick the rules give anyway is no change (`erSiRule`). The record name's source, the RCN / EEIC note
    under the field (`erRcnNote`, from the draft, service by service), and the way to the RECORD panel after ↑ prev / next ↓
    follow the box. A kept value's option reads "(kept)", the explanation on hover: a closed select cannot wrap.

**The defects** (each reproduced on v2.07 by `recovery/v208/repro/found-on-main.mjs`, which now reads PRESENT on v2.07 and GONE
here):
- **F1** `setBand('')` leaves the impact statement.
- **F2** `inspectorBody()` keeps an input typed into and not committed (`__pend`, set on input, cleared on change) across a
  redraw, its keyboard and caret too (`inspHeld`, `inspHeldBack`; matched by handler, so never on another requirement).
- **F3** `setGrp` does nothing for the office a requirement is in (its move is `grpMove`, shared with `reqApply`); Classify's
  bulk set leaves rows that already say the value alone, and says so.
- **F4** `destOf()` reads the second hop, and `miprOf()` the MIPR type, only for a government activity; `setDest` clears both
  when Goes to is not one. A file saved before keeps them, read as nothing.
- **F6** `clinLocked()`: in a PMO copy every CLIN drill writer refuses with a reason, the copy's style dims them, and the drill
  says it is read only.
- **F7** Classify keys columns and bulk setters by field id (`recF` gives `id`; `matColLbl` prints the label): the per-service
  RCN / EEIC column `rcnSvc|USAF` sets RCN / EEIC; the bulk budget setters are `bud|USAF`.
- `setAbsorber` and `setAbsorbFloor` are refused in a copy (`allocLocked`).

**Checks.** audit-board "v2.08 — the defects found mapping item 122" (a part per defect, F2 included) and "the edit box: the

holding kept values; each fails on 3898d95. `npm run layout` N measures the box, filled with long values, at 1366 × 657 and 1920 × 969, in

`v208-122-*`; the review retired one of them), 29 from the review (`v208-rv-*`) and 16 from the second (`v208-rv2-*`), 86 in

check with `recovery/v208/bin/prove.py` (`recovery/v208/v208-proofs.json`, `v208-proved.jsonl`). No sweep.
