### v2.09 (items 125–128): CLIN amounts by pointer, a PMO copy's five years, Save in a PMO copy, the SI tab by code

Brad, 26 Oct 2026: his four requests and his answers to the questions they raised; built on his "Go on v2.09" in four
workstreams in parallel (W1 = 125, W2 = 126, W3 = 127, W4 = 128), one branch each from the slot commit `6405e01`, merged at
`9bc5496`. `NEXT-VERSION.md` › Shipped › v2.09 has every decision; `recovery/v209/notes-W*.md` each workstream's own record.

**A click on a control uses the control (item 125).** The row-selection delegate selects quietly (`setCur(id, true)`: the
docked RECORD panel follows, no drawer slides over, the keyboard stays) when the press left focus on a control in the row or
the click landed in `.clin-detail`; a click on the row itself still opens the panel. **A pressed control survives a redraw**:
`PTR` notes it at pointerdown by its handler, the nearest element with an id and its place among same-handler controls --
never by position alone -- and `ptrAfterRender()` (after `gridAfterRender()`, in the render wrapper) gives its new copy the
keyboard, delivering a button's press once. `selNavBlur` reads `PTR.down` (v2.08's `_selPtrDown` generalised). **v2.08's F2
hold covers the rows** (`#inspector, #list`): a figure half typed in a CLIN box, the rank box or Fund's allocation box is put
back after a redraw with its keyboard and caret, and `heldCommit` applies a put-back figure when the field is left, as a
browser applies any typed field (a script-set value moves the browser's change baseline). **A CLIN's request is per service**
(`clinSetAsk(pid, key, j, val)`, `c.ask[j]`; `clinSetReq` is gone): two lines per CLIN in the drill, Request and Allocation, the
grid still independent and still feeding no funding math (the foot shows the prime's requirement value and the difference, as
information only). `clinGo(id)` is the one route into the drill from elsewhere (the edit box, Classify): it closes what is over
it, opens it (never toggles), scrolls it into view and puts the keyboard on its first box. `tools/pointer-edit.mjs` (`npm run
pointer`) clicks with `page.mouse` at each control's centre, sliding and docked, 1366 and 1920. **A check of a pointer path
clicks with a real pointer**: a `click()` in jsdom never moves focus and never redraws between the press and the click, which
is exactly where item 125's defect lived.

**A PMO copy carries every year of the FYDP (item 126).** `publishPmoCopy()` keeps the published year as it was (`mode.fy`,
`mode.base`, `mode.ord`, `pv:2`) and adds `mode.yb[fy]` and `mode.yord[fy]` for every other year of the window
(`pmoPubYears()`: base…base+4, and the year on screen outside it), and `mode.yn`, the years the board had not started. A
five-year copy is one with `mode.yb`; **`PMO_PV` stays 2** (it is the field-set version: raising it would read every copy out
as the pre-v1.84 eight fields). Every copy-side reader of the baseline reads the year on screen's (`pmoBaseOf`, `pmoOrdOf`).
The package keeps the published year at its top, exactly as before, and every other year under `byYear`; it carries `build`,
so a v2.08 board refuses it whole by the import check's newer-build rule, and `fys` marks it as five-year. Proposal ids are
unique across the copy (`reqSeqNext`). `pmoTakeYears` takes each year through `pmoTake`'s own path, all or nothing; `pmoApply`
asks once, naming each year and its count, starts a year the board has not started (`pmoYearStart`: rolled forward or blank,
as the team chooses) and writes each year inside `aceWithYear(y)` (`pmoEachYear`), oldest first. This replaces v1.95 item 52's
"applies nothing while a ticked package is held for another year". The year tools are off in a copy (`copyYearBlock`).
`tools/pmo-years-render.mjs` (`npm run pmoyears`).

**Save in a PMO copy (item 127).** `pmoSaveCopy()` -- in a copy only, through `saveSerial` -- writes the office's own file:
its handle under `pmo-copy|<publish stamp>|<office>` in `prio-fh` (never the board's `FNAME` entry), the first Save offering
a new file named for the office (`pmoSaveName`), every Save refusing a target that is not this copy's file for this office
(`pmoSaveTargetWhy`: the copy as published, another office's file, another copy's, the board). The office rides in the plan
(`MS.pmoFor`), never in `pubMode`, so the storage keys, the publish stamp and Submit's baseline are as published; opening adopts
it and Submit refuses another office (`pmoForBlock`, one line at the top of `pmoSubmit`); the board drops it on every load. A
copy is marked saved exactly as the board's Save marks it (`LASTKNOWN`, the base, `_saveGen`, `sideWrote`); `WRITER` is never
consulted. **A PMO copy comes in only through the PMO import screen** (Brad): `pmoCopyWhy`/`pmoCopyRefuse` refuse it at
`importState`, `fullImportText`, `importBoards` and `consTakePick`, naming its office; the board's Save refuses to write over
one. The copy's side lists are sealed (`SIDE_SFX`; `EXDB_KEY`, `ACEDB_KEY`, `BUDDB_KEY`, `RPDB_KEY`, `SIDE_OPEN_KEY`). **A new
storage key a copy writes is sealed with `SIDE_SFX`**, or the portfolio team's own browser shares it with every copy it opens.
`tools/pmo-save.mjs` (`npm run pmosave`).

**The SI tab lists the requirements under each SI code (item 128).** `monSheet({byReq:true})` (the SI call only) pivots its
own pass -- each requirement's entries in `bag[g][k].its`, and `pend[g][k]` for asks that drew nothing -- so a code's TOTAL
is that code's row in each service block by construction, never a second walk. Funded is d + m from `Tv`, NO DATE included,
so it follows ◨; the requirement value is printed and enters no total. A prime's CLIN lines split by `clinSplit`, whole dollars
that add to its row; `clinRead` puts back the very objects `clinList`/`clinVec` would have created, so the export changes no
state v2.08's did not. The RCN-EEIC tab is byte-identical to v2.08's, proved against `git show ebe702e` in the suite.

**Three reviews before merge** (`NEXT-VERSION.md` › Shipped › v2.09 has every finding; `recovery/v209/notes-RV*-*.md`
each fixer's record). What they leave for the next change:

- **A link to an office's proposal names the copy that minted it.** A provisional id (`NEW-PSM-2`) means something only
  beside the copy that gave it out. Two copies out at once both mint `NEW-PSM-1`, and a team copy of a link carries no
  provenance of its own. So the copy's stamp travels on the link (`prior.pmo`, `prior.pub`; `pmoLinkStamp`, carried by
  `yearCopy` through `pmoLinkKeep`). One rule decides what a link names (`pmoLinkTo` → `pmoProvOne`); `pmoRepointLinks`, at
  the end of every Apply, and `priorLink` both read it. The second review found the first round's fallback, "the one
  proposal under that id", matching more widely than the rule beside it. **A fallback looser than its rule is a defect
  waiting for the case it was not written for.** The same-work twin rule and the duplicate walk still compare `{fy, id}`
  only: item 129.
- **A control is found again by what names it, and only where that item still stands.** `focusMark`/`focusRestore` key a
  control by its handler and its row, never by class and index: a class that changes with the value (`svcmode`) put the
  keyboard on another requirement's box. A handler that names a place (`rmAwd(id,0)`, `wbsDel(…,i)`, `ndSetDrawn(i)`)
  stands, after a redraw, for whatever moved into that place. It is found again only where its box's controls come back
  the same (`ctlShape`, `ctlPlaces`, `ctlSame`); otherwise the keyboard goes nowhere, and a box keeps it on the box
  (`dlgBack`). The press record (`PTR`, item 125) still matches by handler alone (a known gap).
- **The boot writes browser storage only where it has just kept unsaved work aside.** Opening a newer file over unsaved
  work keeps that work aside, then writes the file's state into storage so the record and storage agree (the second
  review's data-loss fix). Writing on every newer opening (the first cut) meant that opening a file just to look at it
  replaced what this browser would open next. Two tabs can still leave the record clean over unsaved work (`_baseDirty`
  is cached per page): item 129.
- **The office belongs to the page, and follows the work.** Reopening adopts the office the file was saved for
  (`pmoAdopt`, recording how and what it replaced); only the person's own choice writes `prio27pmo`. Work put on screen
  takes its own office (`pmoFollowWork`).
- **Prove every mutation together on the merged board, not only each round's own.** Each fixer proved its mutations on its
  own branch. The combined run (`recovery/v209/proved-integrated.jsonl`) found one that a later fix had made unreachable
  from its check: `v209-w2-link-proposal-unresolved`. The re-point at the end of Apply meant the W2 block never met a
  link still on a copy's id. A person can still reach it, by Put back of the re-point, and that path is now checked.
- **A copy takes nothing through a board door, and gives nothing out except Submit.** Every import door refuses a file in a
  copy. Appropriation packages are refused in a copy, and a package a copy wrote is refused at the board (by its office
  mark or the `[PMO COPY …]` name stamp). The shared-state sidecar is not read in a copy. The copy's stopped-import hold
  is sealed (`IMP_MIXED_KEY` carries `SIDE_SFX`).

**Checks.**
- **Mutations:** 261 new (`v209-*`): 153 from the build, then 57, 42 and 9 from the three reviews. Each was proved against
  its own block, and then all together on the merged board (`recovery/v209/proved-integrated.jsonl`). 4 were retired and 37
  retargeted. There are 2,651 targets.
- **New browser checks in `verify:grid`:** `npm run pmosave`, `pmoyears` and `pointer`. `verify:grid` still stops at
  layout's carried D ×8, so run `keys`, `dropdowns`, `dialogs`, `leftmenu`, the three new ones and `standards` yourself.
- **`validate:nightly` gives the suites 8 GB** (`tools/validate-math.mjs`, `SUITE_HEAP`). v2.09 took audit-board past node's
  ~2 GB default here, and the first nightly died in it before import and recon.

### v2.10 (item 130): FYDP Disconnects -- the Spruill charts by appropriation, on screen and in Excel

Brad, 27 Oct 2026, from photos of his PB27 Spruill slides: *"I need to produce these Excel spreadsheets with this exact look,
color scheme, structure, etc."*; built on his "Go" by two builders to one contract (`recovery/v210/item130-contract.md`: A the
figures and the box, B the workbook), joined at `4c531a0`, reviewed and fixed. `NEXT-VERSION.md` › Shipped › v2.10 has every
decision; `recovery/v210/notes-*.md` each builder's and fixer's record.

**FYDP › FYDP Disconnects** (`#fdcPanel`, `toggleFdc`) sets a cost estimate's requirement against a PPBE cycle's controls,
appropriation by appropriation (O&M, Procurement = Full Fund, Initial Spares; OPN never): a *Summary* and a sheet each for
USAF (with ANG and AFRC), STOVL, USMC CV and USN CV. **`fdcModel(opts)` reads, it never works a figure out again**: the
requirement is `ACEDB`'s lines, the controls each appropriation's PPBE Budget Position under the cycle (`ppSvcFy`, d and m),
both `D$` at the door. A year an appropriation logs no position for is null and prints —, never $0; anything that needs it
is null too. The International requirement and funding (Summary only) are `FDC_INTL`, typed in $M through `amtReadM`, kept
for the session and in nothing the board saves. `exportFdc` → `fdcSheets(model)` → `buildXlsx`: the slides' look, no house
style (audit-other "v2.04 (item 113)" lists it beside the Spend Plan Source and the 1A9A; the count is 16). Not in a PMO copy.

What it leaves for the next change:
- **A service is grouped by its name, and a name the groups do not hold is the Summary's alone.** `FDC_GROUPS` takes the board's
  own alias table (`SVC_ALIAS`, read by own key), so Full Fund's `USMC-B` is the STOVL sheet's; the box names any service in no
  group, and since v2.11 so does every sheet of the workbook. **A sheet that should add up to another is checked by
  adding it up**: A-1 shipped to review with STOVL printing $0 Procurement controls because every check read each sheet alone.
- **Another appropriation is read as its own board opens it**, the shared folder's copy when newer (`fdcFolderRead` →
  `fydpWhen(fydpStoreFolder(k, loc, true))`), and the box states no figure until the folder has answered (Export waits for the
  same answer). **`strict`: only NotFoundError means "no copy there"** -- a copy mid-sync, cut short or not a board names that
  appropriation and prints —, as `driveStoreRead` reads it, and an appropriation not read is never listed among those that log
  no position. The five-year plan still calls it without `strict` (item 131).
- **A redraw on a change event waits when a pointer is moving the keyboard.** Chromium fires `change` on the field being left
  before the click's focus lands; redrawn then, the box controller hands the keyboard back to the field just left and the next
  figure goes in the wrong year. `fdcIntlSet` stores the figure at once and defers the box's writes (`setTimeout 0`) when the
  keyboard is nowhere and no Tab is on its way. Held by `npm run keys` R with a real mouse.
- **`buildXlsx`'s new options are opt-in names on a sheet**: `heights`, `grid:false`, `page:{wide, tall}`, a font `name`, and
  `share` for fills and formats in `_stylesXml`. A workbook asking for none is byte-identical to v2.09's (B's hook compared 279
  workbooks from every suite). **An indent needs a horizontal alignment**: Excel and LibreOffice draw `indent` only beside left,
  right or distributed, so `fdcSheets` writes `horizontal="left"` with it (WB-1); the house style has the same omission (item 131).
- **On screen the appropriation band's text is navy** (white on `#8DB4E2` is 2.15:1, a 1.4.3 finding; Brad kept it, 27 Oct); the
  workbook prints the slides' white. The mock-up (`recovery/v210/item130-mockup/`) uses invented figures: no figure from Brad's slides is in the repo.

**Checks.** audit-other's "v2.10 A" (every line of the five sheets from the fixture's literals; the box through its rendered
controls; nothing stored), "v2.10 B" (the workbook read back from the bytes, every cell's look), the end-to-end export, and
the review's four blocks (A-1 adding the sheets up, both ways round; A-2; A-3 with a stand-in folder, part 4 a copy that
cannot be read; WB-1). Browser: layout-fits O, keys R, panels, dialogs, left-menu, menu-paths, standards. Money coverage and
the money map register `exportFdc`. **Mutations:** 133 new (`v210-*`: A 54, B 61, integration 2, review 16), none older retired
or retargeted; each proved against its own check (`recovery/v210/proved-*.jsonl`). 2,784 targets. **A fix round that runs
only the suites its checks live in leaves the others' expectations unrun**: the review's fixers ran other and board whole, and
the nightly then stopped in import on two expectations v2.10 had moved (the guide's size budget, now 64 KB; a version string
pinned to v2.09). Run every suite whole once on the integrated board before the gate.

### v2.11: the FYDP Disconnects workbook names the services counted on the Summary only

Brad, 27 Oct 2026: *"Keep the band text navy; add the workbook note line."* `fdcLoneSay(st, M)` (moved out of `fdcNotes`) is
the one statement of the services no sheet takes; `fdcModel` carries it as `M.note` **only when there is one**, `fdcNotes`
prints `M.note`, and `fdcSheets` writes it under the table on every sheet (merged across, italic Arial 10, left and wrapped,
14 pt for each line it wraps to -- `fdcWrapLines`, words packed at about 120 characters, a longer word taking the lines it needs,
held at Excel's 409-point row limit -- a blank row each side, the footer last). **Size a wrapped row by the lines its words wrap
to, never by its length**: the first cut divided the length, and the review's long unbroken names were cut top and bottom. **A model with no note is the v2.10 model and
its workbook the v2.10 workbook**: audit-other "v2.11 — …" reads v2.10's `fdcSheets` out of git (`b2cb041`) and compares, so a
shallow clone needs `git fetch --unshallow` first, as recon and import already do. A note the box shows and the workbook
writes come from one string; never word the workbook's from the notes list.

CHECKS-PLACEHOLDER

## Colour: one token, one job

Two tokens were each doing two jobs, and both only showed up when a light theme arrived:

- `--red` was the unfunded **data** lane *and* the alarm **status**. Unfunded is now `--unf`/`--unfD`.
- `--onGold` was text on the gold accent fill *and* text on the fixed `GCOLOR` pastel PMO chips.
  Chip text is now `--onChip`, set once in `:root` and deliberately never overridden — those
  pastels are theme-independent and always light, so the text on them is dark in every theme.

If you find yourself tuning a token for one context and breaking another, that is the signal to
split it rather than compromise.

`Harbor` is the first palette built for colour-deficient reading rather than tinted for it after
the fact. Its lanes leave the green/red axis entirely — blue discretionary, ochre mandatory,
**neutral** unfunded. Unfunded is colourless because a sweep of the whole hue wheel showed no
colour beats a neutral once the third lane must stay readable as text *and* stay apart from the
other two for a red-green reader. Nothing in it is colour-alone: lanes carry their word, unfunded
is a dashed outline, Mission Critical is the star glyph.

Check colour with numbers, never by eye — **`npm run colours` now does it**, in a real browser, for
every theme and both builds. The validator is vendored at `tools/vendor/validate_palette.mjs`
because it ships with a skill in a temp directory: a check that depends on that would pass today and
vanish tomorrow.

**What actually protects a colour-deficient reader here is not the lane hues.** The charts carry
meaning by fill **density** — solid discretionary, hatched mandatory, open dashed unfunded — off a
single blue ramp (`--fcSolid` / `--fcMand` / `--fcEdge`, 7° of hue between them), so they read the
same in greyscale and under every deficiency. The lane hues (`--green`, `--purp`, `--unf`) tint
labelled cards and chips, where the word is beside the colour. So the checks are:

| Held | Why |
|---|---|
| the chart never references a lane hue, and still draws its hatch | the real guarantee, asserted against the **rendered** SVG |
| its fills stay one hue ramp | a well-meant tweak giving mandatory its own colour breaks greyscale |
| Harbor clears the CVD gate (ΔE ≥ 8) | it is the theme that promises this — it measures **13.1** |
| no theme's lane separation gets worse than baseline | records the truth without pretending |
| text clears WCAG (AAA body, AA muted and dim) | measured 12.3–15.9 : 1, 7.2–7.4 : 1, 4.9–6.1 : 1 |

**The measured truth, recorded rather than papered over.** Worst all-pairs CVD ΔE on the three lane
accents: Midnight **5.2**, Navy **4.1**, Parchment **4.8**, Blueprint **4.7**, Harbor **13.1**. The
validator's floor is 6.0. Four themes sit on the green/red axis and their discretionary and unfunded
accents are hard to tell apart for a deuteranope — which is the pre-existing design and exactly why
Harbor was built. Those numbers are a BASELINE, not a gate: a token tweak that makes any theme worse
fails loudly, without asserting a standard those four palettes were never built to meet. **The card
build still defaults to Midnight**; the grid build defaults to Harbor.

Each guard is proven against its own failure: point the chart at `var(--purp)` → *leaked: --purp*;
put Harbor's unfunded back on red → *worst ΔE 4.1 (gate 8)* and a baseline regression; dim `--mut` →
*1.6:1*.

## House style

Match what is there. The file explains *why*, not *what* — the comments record the failure that
motivated the code ("this was duplicated in nine places and the missed one does not throw"). That
is why it is maintainable by someone who did not write it. Keep it.

Errors degrade rather than throw: an unknown appropriation slug falls back to the slug itself,
because a board that covers one appropriation oddly is recoverable and a board that will not open
is not. Preserve that posture.
